\iota:O_{F}\rightarrow\operatorname{End}(G)
, and hence a
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-grading on
\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K})
. Then we let
\mathscr{A}=

\operatorname{gr}_{0}\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K})
. It is a free
O_{K}
-module of rank
n
equipped with an
O_{K}
-hyperplane:
\mathscr{H}\coloneqq\operatorname{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K})\cap\mathscr{A}
by the Kottwitz signature condition. Note that
\mathscr{H}
contains the image of
L^{\flat}
under the identification of
[
13
, Lemma 3.9]
.

Note that the kernel of
O_{K}[\epsilon]\rightarrow O_{K}
has a natural nilpotent divided power structure. Then according to Grothendieck-Messing theory, a lift
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{Z}(L^{\flat})(O_{K}[\epsilon])
of
z
corresponds to an
O_{K}[\epsilon]
direct summand of
\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])

\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}
that lifts
\mathrm{Fil}^{1}\mathbb{D}(G)
and contains the image of
L^{\flat}
in
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}
. Here,
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}
is isotropic under the natural pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])}
on
\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])
induced by the polarization. Since
L^{\flat}\subset\operatorname{Hom}_{O_{F}}(\overline{\mathcal{E}},G)
has rank
n-1
, by Breuil’s theorem
[
19
, §4.3]
, we know that the image of
L^{\flat}
in
\operatorname{gr}_{0}\mathbb{D}(G)(S)
has rank
n-1
over
S
(the Breuil’s ring) and thus its image in the base change
\mathscr{A}
has rank
n-1
over
O_{K}
. In particular,
\mathrm{gr_{0}}\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}
is the unique
O_{K}[\epsilon]
-hyperplane
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}
of
\mathrm{gr}_{0}\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])
that contains the
O_{K}[\epsilon]
-module spanned by the image of
L^{\flat}
in
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}
.

To determine
\mathrm{gr}_{1}\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}
, we note that
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}
is a direct summand of
\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])
with rank
n
containing
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}
. Since
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}
is isotropic under
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])}
, we have
\mathrm{gr}_{1}\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}\subset(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}})^{\perp}\cap\mathrm{gr}_{1}\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])
. Here
(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}})^{\perp}
is the perpendicular subspace in
\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])
with respect to
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])}
. Moreover, since
\mathrm{det}\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])}\neq 0
in
O_{K}[\epsilon]
, we have
(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}})^{\perp}
has rank
n+1
. Note that
\mathrm{gr}_{0}\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])
is also isotropic under
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])}
. In particular,
\mathrm{gr}_{0}\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])\subset(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}})^{\perp}
which has rank
n
. Hence
(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}})^{\perp}\cap\mathrm{gr}_{1}\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])
is of rank one. Since
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}
is a direct summand of
\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])
, we know
\mathrm{gr}_{1}\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}=(\widetilde{\mathscr{H}})^{\perp}\cap\mathrm{gr}_{1}\mathbb{D}(G)(O_{K}[\epsilon])
. Hence
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}=\mathrm{gr}_{0}\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{1}\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}
is uniquely determined, and the lift
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{Z}(L^{\flat})(O_{K}[\epsilon])
of
z
is unique. Hence
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}=\mathrm{gr}_{0}\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}\oplus\mathrm{gr}_{1}\widetilde{\mathrm{Fil}}
is uniquely determined, and the lift
\tilde{z}\in\mathcal{Z}(L^{\flat})(O_{K}[\epsilon])
of
z
is unique.

We defer the proof of the cardinality of the summation index to Lemma
10.20
, where it is proved via analytic method.
∎

6.
Local modularity and Tate conjectures

First, as we have discussed in the introduction, we propose the following local modularity conjecture motivated by the analytic computation and the special case for
\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[0]}
(see
[
19
, Corollary 5.3.3]
).

Conjecture 6.1
.

For the Rapoport-Zink space
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
and an
O_{F}
-lattice
L^{\flat}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
of rank
n-1
, we have

\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\flat},\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{Z}(x))=-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat},\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{Y}(x)).

Remark 6.2
.

More precisely, as we wrote in Section
4
, Conjecture
6.1
is primarily motivated by (
4.2
) and, more generally, by Theorem
11.2
below along with
[
19
, Lemma 6.3.1]
and
[
30
, Theorem 8.1]
.

Conjecture 6.3
.

(cf.
[
19
, Corollary 5.3.3]
) For the Rapoport-Zink space
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
and an
O_{F}
-lattice
L^{\flat}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
of rank
n-1
, there are finitely many Deligne-Lusztig curves
C_{i}\subset\mathcal{N}_{3}^{[0]}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[h]}
, projective lines
D_{i}\subset\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{n}^{[h]}
and
\text{mult}_{C_{i}}
,
\text{mult}_{D_{i}}\in{\mathbb{Q}}
such that for any
x\in{\mathbb{V}}\backslash L^{\flat}_{F}
,

\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\flat})_{{\mathscr{V}}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})=\sum_{i}\text{mult}_{C_{i}}\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},O_{C_{i}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})+\sum_{i}\text{mult}_{D_{i}}\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},O_{D_{i}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)}).

Remark 6.4
.

Indeed, as we wrote in Section
4
, Conjecture
6.1
follows from Conjecture
6.3
. Conjecture
6.3
is implied by a stronger version of Tate conjectures on
1
-cycles for Deligne–Lusztig varieties
Y_{\Lambda}
in Proposition
6.6
(cf.
[
19
, Theorem 5.3.2]
).

Theorem 6.5
.

Conjecture
6.3
holds for
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{n},{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n},{\mathcal{N}}^{[n-1]}_{n},{\mathcal{N}}^{[n]}_{n}
, and
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{4}
.

To prove this theorem, we follow
[
19
, Section 5.3]
. First, we need to recall several notations and theorems from
[
2
]
. In
[
2
, Theorem 1.1]
, we proved that the reduced subscheme of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
has a Bruhat-Tits stratification and their components are certain Deligne-Lusztig varieties
Y_{\Lambda}
where
\Lambda
is a vertex lattice of type
t(\Lambda)
. More precisely, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 6.6
.

[
2
, Theorem 1.1]
Let
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n,red}
be the underlying reduced subscheme of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
. Then, we have

{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n,red}={\cup}_{t(\Lambda)\leq h-1}Y_{\Lambda}\text{ }{\cup}\text{ }{\cup}_{t(\Lambda)\geq h+1}Y_{\Lambda},

where
Y_{\Lambda}
denotes certain Deligne-Lusztig varieties associated with vertex lattices
\Lambda
. Also, the dimension of
Y_{\Lambda}
is
\frac{1}{2}(t(\Lambda)+h-1)
(resp.
\frac{1}{2}(t(\Lambda)+n-h-1)
) if
t(\Lambda)\geq h+1
(resp.
t(\Lambda)\leq h-1
).

By
[
19
, Corollary 5.3.3]
, we know that the Conjecture
6.3
holds for
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{n}
and
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n]}_{n}
. Also, by
[
2
, Theorem 1.1]
, the irreducible components of the reduced subscheme of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{4}
are
{\mathbb{P}}^{2}
and their Chow groups are well-known. Therefore, let us focus on
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n},{\mathcal{N}}^{[n-1]}_{n}
.
Since
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n-1]}_{n}
, we only need to consider
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
. In this case, by Proposition
6.6
(see
[
2
, Theorem 1.1]
for more detail), we know that the reduced subscheme of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
has a Bruhat-Tits stratification and their components are Deligne-Lusztig varieties
Y_{\Lambda}
where
t(\Lambda)\geq 2
or projective spaces
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{\Lambda}
, where
t(\Lambda)=0
. Let us describe
Y_{\Lambda}
more precisely.

Let
k_{F}
be the residue field of
F
and let
V_{2d+2}
be the unique (up to isomorphism)
k_{F}/k
-hermitian space of dimension
2d+2
. Let
\Lambda/\pi\Lambda^{\vee}=V_{2d+2}
where
t(\Lambda)=2d+2
, and let
J_{2d+2}
be the special unitary group associated to
(V_{2d+2},(\cdot,\cdot))
. Let
(W_{2d+2},S_{2d+2})
be the Weyl system of
J_{2d+2}
and let
B_{2d+2}
be the standard Borel subgroup. For
I\in S_{2d+2}
, we define
W_{I}
as the subgroup of
W_{2d+2}
generated by
I
and
P_{I}\coloneqq B_{2d+2}W_{I}B_{2d+2}
. Note that
W_{2d+2}
can be identified with a symmetric group and
S_{2d+2}
with
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{2d+1}\}
where
s_{i}
is the transposition of
i
and
i+1
. We write

\begin{array}[]{l}I_{0}\coloneqq\{s_{1},\dots s_{d},s_{d+2},\dots,s_{2d+1}\},\\
I_{i}\coloneqq\{s_{1},\dots,s_{d-i},s_{d+i+2},\dots,s_{2d+1}\},1\leq i\leq d,\\
P_{i}\coloneqq P_{I_{i}}.\end{array}

Note that
P_{d}=B_{2d+2}
. Also, note that the elements in
J_{2d+2}/P_{i}
parametrize flags

0\subset T_{-i}\overset{1}{\subset}T_{-i+1}\dots\overset{1}{\subset}T_{-1}\overset{1}{\subset}\overline{A}\overset{1}{\subset}\overline{B}\overset{1}{\subset}T_{1}\dots\overset{1}{\subset}T_{i}\subset V_{2d+2}.

Now, by
[
2
, Theorem 1.1]
, we know that the reduced subscheme of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
is

(6.1)

{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n,red}=\cup_{\Lambda,t(\Lambda)\geq 2}Y_{\Lambda}\cup\cup_{\Lambda,t(\Lambda)=0}{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{\Lambda}.

Here,
Y_{\Lambda}=X_{P_{0}}(id)\sqcup X_{P_{0}}(s_{d+1})
where
2d+2=t(\Lambda)
(see
[
2
, Definition 3.10]
). By
[
2
, Lemma 2.21]
(cf.
[
27
, Lemma 2.1]
), we have the following statement (cf.
[
27
, Theorem 2.15]
).

Proposition 6.7
.

(cf.
[
27
, Theorem 2.15]
)
There is a decomposition of
X_{P_{0}}(id)\sqcup X_{P_{0}}(s_{d+1})
into a disjoint union of locally closed subvarieties

X_{P_{0}}(id)\sqcup X_{P_{0}}(s_{d+1})=\sqcup_{i=0}^{d}\{X_{P_{i}}(s_{d+2}\dots s_{d+i+1})\sqcup X_{P_{i}}(s_{d+1}s_{d+2}\dots s_{d+i+1})\}.

Proof.

We can follow the proof of
[
27
, Theorem 2.15]
with
[
2
, Lemma 2.21]
.
∎

Since
P_{d}=B_{2d+2}
, we have that
X_{P_{d}}(s_{d+2}\dots s_{2d+1})
and
X_{P_{d}}(s_{d+1}s_{d+2}\dots s_{2d+1})
are classical Deligne-Lusztig varieties. For
0\leq i\leq d
, let us write

\begin{array}[]{l}X_{i,1}^{\circ}\coloneqq X_{P_{i}}(s_{d+1}\dots s_{d+i+1}),\\
X_{i-1,2}^{\circ}\coloneqq X_{P_{i}}(s_{d+2}\dots s_{d+i+1}),\\
Y_{i,1}^{\circ}\coloneqq X_{B_{2i+2}}(s_{i+1}\dots s_{2i+1}),\\
Y_{i-1,2}^{\circ}\coloneqq X_{B_{2i+2}}(s_{i+2}\dots s_{2i+1}),\\
\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\circ}\coloneqq X_{i,1}^{\circ}\sqcup X_{i,2}^{\circ},\\
\widetilde{X}_{i}\coloneqq\sqcup_{m=0}^{i}\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\circ},\\
Y_{d}\coloneqq\sqcup_{i=0}^{d}\{X_{P_{i}}(s_{d+2}\dots s_{d+i+1})\sqcup X_{P_{i}}(s_{d+1}s_{d+2}\dots s_{d+i+1})\}.\end{array}

Then, by the above proposition and a Bruhat-Tits stratification in
[
2
, Theorem 1.1]
, we have that
X_{i,1}^{\circ}
(resp.
X_{i,2}^{\circ}
) is a disjoint union of isomorphic copies of
Y_{i,1}^{\circ}
(resp.
Y_{i,2}^{\circ}
). Also, the dimensions of
Y_{i,1}^{\circ}
and
Y_{i,2}^{\circ}
are
i+1
.

For any
k_{F}
-variety
S
, we write
H^{j}(S)(i)
for
H^{j}(S_{\overline{k}},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}}(i))
where
l\neq p
is a prime and
\overline{k}
is an algebraically closed field containing
k_{F}
. Let
{\mathscr{F}}=\text{Fr}_{k_{F}}
be the
q^{2}
-Frobenius on
H^{j}(S)(i)
. Then, the following analogous statement of
[
19
, Lemma 5.3.1]
holds.

Lemma 6.8
.

(cf.
[
19
, Lemma 5.3.1]
) For any
d,i\geq 0
and
s\geq 1
, the action of
{\mathscr{F}}^{s}
on the following cohomology groups are semisimple, and the space of
{\mathscr{F}}^{s}
-invariants is zero when
j\geq 1
.

(1)

H^{2j}(Y_{d,1}^{\circ})(j)
.

(2)

H^{2j}(Y_{d-1,2}^{\circ})(j)
.

(3)

H^{2j}(\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\circ})(j)
.

(4)

H^{2j}(Y_{d}-\widetilde{X}_{i})(j)
.

Proof.

Here, we follow the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 5.3.1]
with some modification.

(1)

By
[
21
, (7.3)
(\text{}^{2}\text{A}_{2d+1},{\mathscr{F}})
]
(or we refer to the proof of
[
22
, Lemma 2]
), we have the following table on the eigenvalues of
{\mathscr{F}}
on
H^{j}_{c}(Y_{d,1}^{\circ})
.

\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c}j&d+1&d+2&d+3&\dots&2d-1&2d&2d+1&2d+2\\
\hline\cr&1&q^{2}&q^{4}&\dots&q^{2d-4}&q^{2d-2}&q^{2d}&q^{2d+2}\\
\hline\cr&-q^{3}&-q^{5}&-q^{7}&\dots&-q^{2d-1}&&&.\end{array}

By the Poincaré duality, we have a perfect pairing

H^{2d+2-j}_{c}(Y_{d,1}^{\circ})\times H^{j}(Y_{d,1}^{\circ})(d+1)\rightarrow H^{2d+2}_{c}(Y_{d,1}^{\circ})(d+1)\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{l}.

Therefore, the eigenvalues of
{\mathscr{F}}
on
H^{2j}(Y^{\circ}_{d,1})(j)
are given by
q^{2(d+1-j)}
times the inverse of the eigenvalues in
H^{2(d+1-j)}_{c}(Y_{d,1}^{\circ})
. More precisely,

\begin{array}[]{ccc}&H^{2(d+1-j)}_{c}(Y_{d,1}^{\circ})&q^{2(d+1-j)}\times\text{the inverse}\\
j=0&q^{2d+2}&1\\
j=1&q^{2d-2}&q^{2}\\
j\geq 2&q^{2d+2-4j},-q^{2d-4j+5}&q^{2j},-q^{2j-3}.\end{array}

Therefore, the eigenvalue of
{\mathscr{F}}^{s}
cannot be
1
when
j\geq 1
. The semisimplicity of the action of
{\mathscr{F}}^{s}
is from
[
21
, 6.1]
.

(2)

By
[
21
, (7.3)
(\text{A}_{d+1},{\mathscr{F}})
]
(or we refer to the proof of
[
22
, Lemma 2]
, here, note that
{\mathscr{F}}
is
q^{2}
-Frobenius), we have the following table on the eigenvalues of
{\mathscr{F}}
on
H^{j}_{c}(Y_{d,2}^{\circ})
.

\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c}j&d+1&d+2&d+3&\dots&2d-1&2d&2d+1&2d+2\\
\hline\cr&1&q^{2}&q^{4}&\dots&q^{2d-4}&q^{2d-2}&q^{2d}&q^{2d+2}.\end{array}

By the Poincaré duality, we have a perfect pairing

H^{2d+2-j}_{c}(Y_{d,2}^{\circ})\times H^{j}(Y_{d,2}^{\circ})(d+1)\rightarrow H^{2d+2}_{c}(Y_{d,2}^{\circ})(d+1)\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{l}.

Therefore, the eigenvalues of
{\mathscr{F}}
on
H^{2j}(Y^{\circ}_{d,2})(j)
are given by
q^{2(d+1-j)}
times the inverse of the eigenvalues in
H^{2(d+1-j)}_{c}(Y_{d,2}^{\circ})
. More precisely,

\begin{array}[]{ccc}&H^{2(d+1-j)}_{c}(Y_{d,2}^{\circ})&q^{2(d+1-j)}\times\text{the inverse}\\
j=0&q^{2d+2}&1\\
j=1&q^{2d-2}&q^{2}\\
j\geq 2&q^{2d+2-4j}&q^{2j}.\end{array}

Therefore, the eigenvalue of
{\mathscr{F}}^{s}
cannot be
1
when
j\geq 1
. The semisimplicity of the action of
{\mathscr{F}}^{s}
is from
[
21
, 6.1]
.

(3)

This follows from (1) and (2) since
\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\circ}
is a disjoint union of
Y_{i,1}^{\circ}
and
Y_{i,2}^{\circ}
.

(4)

This follows from (3) since
Y_{d}-\widetilde{X}_{i}^{\circ}=\sqcup_{m=i+1}^{d}\widetilde{X}_{m}^{\circ}
.

∎

Theorem 6.9
.

(cf.
[
19
, Theorem 5.3.2]
) For any
0\leq i\leq d+1
and any
s\geq 1
, we have

(1)

The space of Tate classes
H^{2i}(Y_{d})(i)^{{\mathscr{F}}^{s}=1}
is spanned by the cycle classes of the irreducible components of
\widetilde{X}_{i}
.

(2)

Let
H^{2i}(Y_{d})(i)_{1}\subset H^{2i}(Y_{d})(i)
be the generalized eigenspace of
{\mathscr{F}}^{s}
for the eigenvalue
1
. Then
H^{2i}(Y_{d})(i)_{1}=H^{2i}(Y_{d})(i)^{{\mathscr{F}}^{s}=1}
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as
[
19
, Theorem 5.3.2]
with Lemma
6.8

∎

Proof of Theorem
6.5
.

Here, we follow the proof of
[
19
, Corollary 5.3.3]
. For
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{n}
and
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n]}_{n}
, this is from
[
19
, Corollary 5.3.3]
. For
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
and
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n-1]}_{n}
, note that
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
and
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n-1]}_{n}
are isomorphic, so we only need to consider the case
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
. By
[
2
, Theorem 1.1]
, we have (
6.1
):

{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n,red}=\cup_{\Lambda,t(\Lambda)\geq 2}Y_{\Lambda}\cup\cup_{\Lambda,t(\Lambda)=0}{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{\Lambda},

and any curve
C
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n,red}
lies in some
Y_{\Lambda}\simeq Y_{d}
or the projective space
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{\Lambda}
for some vertex lattice
\Lambda
. If it lies on
Y_{d}
, then by Theorem
6.9
, the cycle class of
C
can be written as a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear combination of the cycle classes of the irreducible components of
\widetilde{X}_{1}
and these are projective lines. Similarly, if it lies on
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{\Lambda}
, then by the Chow group of
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}_{\Lambda}
, we have that the cycle class of
C
can be written as a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear combination of projective lines. This finishes the proof of the theorem for
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
and
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n-1]}_{n}
.

For
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{4}
, we know that the irreducible components of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{4,red}
are
{\mathbb{P}}^{2}_{\Lambda}
for vertex lattices
\Lambda
, and hence by the description of Chow group of
{\mathbb{P}}^{2}_{\Lambda}
, the cycle class of
C
can be written as a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear combination of projective lines. Moreover, the projective lines here can be realized as images of embeddings of projective lines in
\mathcal{N}_{2}^{[1]}
.

Finally, the finiteness of curves is from Lemma
2.8
. This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

7.
Weighted representation densities and conjectures

In this section, we first recall the definition of weighted representation densities and formulas from
[
4
, Section 3.1]
. Then, we will recall the conjectural formula in
[
4
, Conjecture 3.17, Conjecture 3.25]
.

We denote by
∗
the nontrivial Galois automorphism of
F
over
F_{0}
. We fix the standard additive character
\psi:F_{0}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
that is trivial on
O_{F_{0}}
. Let
V^{+}
(resp.
V^{-}
) be a split (resp. non-split)
2n
-dimensional hermitian vector space over
F
and let
{\mathcal{S}}((V^{\pm})^{2n})
be the space of Schwartz functions on
(V^{\pm})^{2n}
. Let
V_{r,r}
be the split hermitian space of signature
(r,r)
and let
L_{r,r}
be a self-dual lattice in
V_{r,r}
. Let
\phi_{r,r}
be the characteristic function of
(L_{r,r})^{2n}
. Let
(V^{\pm})^{[r]}
be the space
V^{\pm}\otimes V_{r,r}
. For any function
\phi\in{\mathcal{S}}((V^{\pm})^{2n})
, we define a function
\phi^{[r]}
by
\phi\otimes\phi_{r,r}\in{\mathcal{S}}(((V^{\pm})^{[r]})^{2n})
.

Let
\Gamma_{n}
be the Iwahori subgroup

\Gamma_{n}\coloneqq\{\gamma=(\gamma_{ij})\in GL_{n}(O_{F})\mid\gamma_{ij}\in\pi O_{F}\text{ if }i>j\}.

We define the set
V_{n}(F)
by

V_{n}(F)=\{Y\in M_{n,n}(F)\mid\text{}^{t}Y^{*}=Y\}.

We define the set
X_{n}(F)
by

X_{n}(F)=\{X\in GL_{n}(F)\mid\text{}^{t}X^{*}=X\}.

For
g\in GL_{n}(F)
and
X\in X_{n}(F)
, we define the group action of
GL_{n}(F)
on
X_{n}(F)
by
g\cdot X=gX^{t}g^{*}
. For
X,Y\in V_{n}(F)
, we denote by
\langle X,Y\rangle=\mathrm{Tr}(XY)
. For
X\in M_{m,n}(F)
and
A\in V_{m}(F)
, we denote by
A[X]=^{t}X^{*}AX.
For a hermitian matrix
A\in X_{m}(F)
, we define

A^{[r]}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&\\
&I_{2r}\end{array}\right).

Now, let us recall the definition of usual representation densities.

Definition 7.1
.

For
A\in X_{m}(O_{F})
and
B\in X_{n}(O_{F})
, we define
\mathrm{Den}(A,B)
by

\mathrm{Den}(A,B)=\lim_{d\rightarrow\infty}(q^{-d})^{n(2m-n)}|\{x\in M_{m,n}(O_{F}/\pi^{d}O_{F})\mid A[x]\equiv B(\text{mod}\pi^{d})\}|.

Now, let us recall the definition of weighted representation densities in
[
4
]
.

Definition 7.2
.

[
4
, Definition 3.1]
Let
0\leq h,t\leq n
. Let
L_{t}
be a lattice of rank
2n
in
V^{+}
if
t
is even (resp. in
V^{-}
if
t
is odd) with hermitian form

A_{t}\coloneqq\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}I_{2n-t}&\\
&\pi^{-1}I_{t}\end{array}\right).

Let
1_{h,t}\in{\mathcal{S}}((V^{\pm})^{2n})
be the characteristic function of
(L_{t}^{\vee})^{2n-h}\times L_{t}^{h}
.

For
B\in X_{2n}(F)
, we define

W_{h,t}(B,(-q)^{-2r})\coloneqq\int_{V_{2n}(F)}\int_{M_{2n+2r,2n}(F)}\psi(\langle Y,A_{t}^{[r]}[X]-B\rangle)1_{h,t}^{[r]}(X)dXdY.

Here,
dY
(resp.
dX
) is the Haar measure on
V_{2n}(F)
(resp.
M_{2n+2r,2n}(F)
) such that

\int_{V_{2n}(O_{F})}dY=1\text{ }(\text{resp. }\int_{M_{2n+2r,2n}(O_{F})}dX=1).

The functions
\mathrm{Den}(A,B)
and
W_{h,t}(B,r)
have the following formulas.

Lemma 7.3
.

(
[
10
]
,
[
4
, Lemma 3.5]
) For
A\in X_{m}(F)
and
B\in X_{2n}(F)
, we have that

\mathrm{Den}(A^{[r]},B)={\sum}_{Y\in\Gamma_{2n}\backslash X_{2n}(F)}\dfrac{{\mathcal{G}}(Y,B){\mathcal{F}}(Y,A^{[r]})}{\alpha(Y;\Gamma_{2n})},

and

W_{h,t}(B,(-q)^{-2r})={\sum}_{Y\in\Gamma_{2n}\backslash X_{2n}(F)}\dfrac{{\mathcal{G}}(Y,B){\mathcal{F}}_{h}(Y,A_{t}^{[r]})}{\alpha(Y;\Gamma_{2n})}.

Here, we define
{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A^{[r]})
by

{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A^{[r]})\coloneqq{\int}_{M_{m,2n}(F)}\psi(\langle Y,A^{[r]}[X]\rangle)dX,

and we define
{\mathcal{F}}_{h}(Y,A_{t}^{[r]})
by

{\mathcal{F}}_{h}(Y,A_{t}^{[r]})\coloneqq{\int}_{M_{2n+2r,2n}(F)}\psi(\langle Y,A_{t}^{[r]}[X]\rangle)1_{h,t}^{[r]}(X)dX.

We define
{\mathcal{G}}(Y,B)
by

{\mathcal{G}}(Y,B)\coloneqq{\int}_{\Gamma_{2n}}\psi(\langle Y,-B[\gamma]\rangle)d\gamma,

where
d\gamma
is the Haar measure on
M_{2n,2n}(O_{F})
such that
\int_{M_{2n,2n}(O_{F})}d\gamma=1
.

Also, we define
\alpha(Y;\Gamma_{2n})
by

\alpha(Y;\Gamma_{2n})\coloneqq\lim_{d\rightarrow\infty}q^{-4dn^{2}}N_{d}(Y;\Gamma_{2n}),

where
N_{d}(Y;\Gamma_{2n})=|\{\gamma\in\Gamma_{2n}(\text{mod }\pi^{d})|\gamma\cdot Y\equiv Y(\text{mod }\pi^{d})\}|
.

Definition 7.4
.

For
r\geq 0
,
A\in X_{n+2r}(O_{F})
, and
B\in X_{n}(O_{F})
, we can regard
{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A^{[r]})
,
\mathrm{Den}(A^{[r]},B)
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{h}(Y,A_{t}^{[r]})
, and
W_{h,t}(B,(-q)^{-2r})
as functions of
X=(-q)^{-2r}
. We define

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,A)\coloneqq-\dfrac{d}{dX}{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A^{[r]})|_{X=1},

and

{\mathcal{F}}_{h}^{\prime}(Y,A_{t})\coloneqq-\dfrac{d}{dX}{\mathcal{F}}_{h}(Y,A_{t}^{[r]})|_{X=1}.

Also, we define

\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(A,B)=-\dfrac{d}{dX}\mathrm{Den}(A,B;X)|_{X=1},

and

W^{\prime}_{h,t}(B)\coloneqq-\dfrac{d}{dX}W_{h,t}(B,r)|_{X=1}.

Definition 7.5
.

[
5
, Proposition 2.7]
For
0\leq i,h\leq 2n
, we define the constant
\beta_{i}^{h}
by

\beta^{h}_{i}=\alpha_{i+1,h}^{-1}\Biggl(\dfrac{{\prod}_{1\leq m\leq 2n,m\neq i+1}(1-x_{m})}{{\prod}_{1\leq m\leq 2n+1,m\neq i+1}(x_{m}-x_{i+1})}\Biggl),

where

\begin{array}[]{lr}\alpha_{i,h}=(-q)^{(n+1-i)(2n-h)},&1\leq i\leq n;\\
\alpha_{i,h}=(-q)^{(2n+1-i)(2n+h)},&n+1\leq i\leq 2n;\\
\alpha_{2n+1,h}=1,\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{lr}x_{i}=(-q)^{n+1-i},&1\leq i\leq n;\\
x_{i}=(-q)^{i-2n-1},&n+1\leq i\leq 2n;\\
x_{2n+1}=1.\end{array}

Now, we can state
[
4
, Conjecture 3.17, Conjecture 3.25]
.

Conjecture 7.6
.

[
4
, Conjecture 3.17, Conjecture 3.25]

For a basis
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{2n-m},y_{1},\dots,y_{m}\}
of
{\mathbb{V}}
, and special cycles
{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{1})
,
\dots
,
{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{2n-m})
,
{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1})
,
\dots
,
{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{m})
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n]}_{2n}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\chi({\mathcal{N}}_{2n}^{[n]},O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}\dots\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{m})})=\dfrac{1}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}\{W^{\prime}_{m,n}(B)-{\sum}_{0\leq i\leq n-1}\beta_{i}^{m}W_{m,i}(B,1)\}.\end{array}

Here,
\chi
is the Euler-Poincaré characteristic and
\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}
is the derived tensor product. Also,
B
is the matrix

B=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}(x_{i},x_{j})&(x_{i},y_{l})\\
(y_{k},x_{j})&(y_{k},y_{l})\end{array}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2n-m,1\leq k,l\leq m}.

Assume that special homomophisms
\{x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n},x_{n+1},\dots,x_{n+h},y_{1},\dots,y_{n-h}\}
has the hermitian matrix:

(7.1)

B=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}(x_{i},x_{j})&(x_{i},y_{l})\\
(y_{k},x_{j})&(y_{k},y_{l})\end{array}\middle)_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i,j\leq n+h,\\
1\leq k,l\leq n-h\end{subarray}}=\middle(\begin{array}[]{ccc}T&&\\
&I_{h}&\\
&&\pi^{-1}I_{n-h}\end{array}\right),

for some
n\times n
matrix
T
. Then, by Proposition
2.6
and Proposition
2.7
, the arithmetic intersection number of special cycles
\chi({\mathcal{N}}_{2n}^{[n]},O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}\dots\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{n+h})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}\dots\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{n-h})})
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n]}_{2n}
can be identified with
\Int_{n,h}(T)=\Int_{n,h}(L)=\chi({\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[h]},O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}\dots\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{n})})
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
, where
L=\mathrm{Span}_{O_{F}}\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\}
. We note that the valuation of the determinant of
B
and
h+1
have the same parity. Now, Conjecture
7.6
is specialized to the following conjecture.

Conjecture 7.7
.

Consider a basis
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n+h},y_{1},\dots,y_{n-h}\}
of
{\mathbb{V}}
with moment matrix
B
as in (
7.1
). Let
L=\mathrm{Span}_{O_{F}}\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\}
. Then

(7.2)

\begin{array}[]{l}\Int_{n,h}(L)=\Int_{n,h}(T)=\dfrac{1}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}\{W^{\prime}_{n-h,n}(B)-{\sum}_{0\leq i\leq n-1}\beta_{i}^{n-h}W_{n-h,i}(B,1)\}.\end{array}

Note that
\Int_{n,h}(L)
is exactly the intersection number considered in Conjecture
3.3
. We show the analytic sides of Conjectures
3.3
and
7.7
also match in §
8
.

8.
Cho-Yamauchi constants

In this section, we will modify the result of
[
5
]
to get the Cho-Yamauchi constants in the case of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
. More precisely, we want to write the conjectural formula for the arithmetic intersection numbers of special cycles
\chi({\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[h]},O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}\dots\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{n})})
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
as a linear sum of representation densities. First, we start with the following proposition.

Proposition 8.1
.

Assume that
B
is of the form in (
7.1
). Then, we have

\displaystyle W_{n-h,n}^{\prime}(B)

\displaystyle=q^{-4n^{2}+(n+h)(n-h)}\mathrm{Den}(\pi A_{n},I_{n-h})\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n+h,h},\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}T&\\
&I_{h}\end{array}\right))

\displaystyle=q^{-4n^{2}+(n+h)(n-h)}\mathrm{Den}(\pi A_{n},I_{n-h})\mathrm{Den}(I_{n+h,h},I_{h})\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},T).

Proof.

One can use a similar method as in
[
13
, Corollary 9.12]
to prove this. For example, see
[
4
, Proposition A.3, Proposition A.4, (A.0.4), (A.0.5)]
.
∎

Note that in Proposition
8.1
, the terms
\mathrm{Den}(\pi A_{n},I_{n-h})
and
\mathrm{Den}(I_{n+h,h},I_{h})
are constants, and
\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},T)
is the derivative of a usual representation density. Now, by
[
5
]
, this can be written as a linear sum of usual representation densities. Let us follow the steps in
[
5
, Section 4.1]
. For this, we need to introduce some notations.

Definition 8.2
.

(1)

We write
{\mathcal{R}}_{n}
for the set

{\mathcal{R}}_{n}=\{Y_{\sigma,e}\mid(\sigma,e)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{n}\times{\mathbb{Z}}^{n},\sigma^{2}=1,e_{i}=e_{\sigma(i)},\forall i\},

where
{\mathcal{S}}_{n}
is the symmetric group of degree
n
, and

Y_{\sigma,e}=\sigma\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\pi^{e_{1}}&&0\\
&\ddots&\\
0&&\pi^{e_{n}}\end{array}\right).

Then
{\mathcal{R}}_{n}
forms a complete set of representatives of
\Gamma_{n}\backslash X_{n}(F)
.

(2)

We write
{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
for the set

{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}=\{\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}\mid\lambda_{1}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{n}\geq 0\}.

(3)

For
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
, we define
A_{\lambda}
by

A_{\lambda}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\pi^{\lambda_{1}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\pi^{\lambda_{n}}\end{array}\right).

(4)

For
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
, we define
|\lambda|
by

|\lambda|=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}.

Definition 8.3

(Cho-Yamauchi constant)
.

Assume that
B
is of the form in (
7.1
).
For
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
, we define
D_{n,h}(\lambda)
to be the constant satisfying

\dfrac{1}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}\{W^{\prime}_{n-h,n}(B)-{\sum}_{0\leq t\leq n-1}\beta_{t}^{n-h}W_{n-h,t}(B,1)\}={\sum}_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}}D_{n,h}(\lambda)\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda},T)}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda},A_{\lambda})}.

The existence and uniqueness of these constants are from
[
5
]
. The constant
D_{n,h}(\lambda)
is a version of the Cho-Yamauchi constant in
[
1
]
.

Remark 8.4
.

In
{\mathcal{N}}^{h}(1,n-1)
, let
L
be a rank
n

O_{F}
-lattice generated by special homomorphisms
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
in
{\mathbb{V}}
. Assume that
T
is the hermitian matrix of
L
. Then, the valuation of the determinant of
T
and
h+1
have the same parity. Therefore, in Definition
8.3
, the terms
\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda},T)
such that

{\mathrm{val}}(\det(A_{\lambda}))=\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\not\equiv h+1(\text{mod 2})

are always equal to 0.

Now, let us compute the correction terms
\dfrac{-{\sum}_{0\leq t\leq n-1}\beta_{t}^{n-h}W_{n-h,t}(B,1)}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}
.

Proposition 8.5
.

Assume that
B
is of the form in (
7.1
). If
n-h\leq t\leq n-1
, we have

\displaystyle W_{n-h,t}(B,1)

\displaystyle=q^{-4n^{2}+(n+h)(3n-2t-h)}\mathrm{Den}(\pi A_{t},I_{n-h})\mathrm{Den}(I_{n+h,t-n+h},I_{h})\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,t-n+h},T).

If
t<n-h
, we have that
W_{n-h,t}(B,1)=0
.

Proof.

One can use a similar method as in
[
13
, Corollary 9.12]
to prove this. For example, see
[
4
, Proposition A.3, Proposition A.4, (A.0.4), (A.0.5)]
.
∎

Proposition 8.6
.

For
n-h\leq t\leq n-1
, we have

\dfrac{\beta_{t}^{n-h}W_{n-h,t}(B,1)}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}=\dfrac{-(-q)^{\frac{(n-t)(n-t-1-2h)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(n-t)}}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,t-n+h},T)}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,t-n+h},I_{n,t-n+h})}.

Proof.

By Definition
7.5
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}\beta_{t}^{n-h}=(-q)^{-(n-t)(n+h)}\dfrac{(-1)^{n-1}(-q)^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-(n-t)}{\prod}_{l=n+1-t}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{n-t-1}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{(-1)^{t}(-q)^{2n(n-t)+\frac{t(t+1)}{2}}{\prod}_{l=1}^{t}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{2n-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
\\
=(-1)^{n-t-1}(-q)^{-\frac{(n-t)(1+2h+5n-t)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=n+1-t}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-t-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{t}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{2n-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.\end{array}

Also, by
[
4
, Proposition A.4]
, we have that

\mathrm{Den}(\pi A_{t},I_{n-h})=\mathrm{Den}(\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\pi I_{2n-t}&\\
&I_{t}\end{array}\right),I_{n-h})=\prod_{l=t-n+h+1}^{t}(1-(-q)^{-l}),

\mathrm{Den}(I_{n+h,t-n+h},I_{h})=\prod_{l=2n-h-t+1}^{2n-t}(1-(-q)^{-l}),

\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,t-n+h},I_{n,t-n+h})=q^{(t-n+h)^{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{2n-t-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{t-n+h}(1-(-q)^{-l}),

and

W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)=q^{-3n^{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l})^{2}.

Combining these and Proposition
8.5
, we get the proposition.
∎

Combining Proposition
8.1
and Proposition
8.6
, we get the following corollary which compares the analytic sides of Conjecture
3.3
and Conjecture
7.6
.

Corollary 8.7
.

Assume that
B
is of the in (
7.1
). By Proposition
8.1
, Proposition
8.5
, Proposition
8.6
, and
[
4
, Proposition A.4]
, we can write
\dfrac{1}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}\{W^{\prime}_{n-h,n}(B)-{\sum}_{0\leq t\leq n-1}\beta_{t}^{n-h}W_{n-h,t}(B,1)\}
in terms of usual representation densities as follows:

\dfrac{1}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}\{W^{\prime}_{n-h,n}(B)-{\sum}_{0\leq t\leq n-1}\beta_{t}^{n-h}W_{n-h,t}(B,1)\}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},T)}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,h},I_{n,h})}+{\sum}_{k=0}^{h-1}\frac{(-q)^{-\frac{(h-k)(h+k+1)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-k)}}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,k},T)}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,k},I_{n,k})}.

By Corollary
8.7
, Conjecture
7.7
can be rewritten as follows.

Conjecture 8.8

(
{\mathcal{Z}}
-cycles in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
)
.

For a basis
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}
of
{\mathbb{V}}
, and special cycles
{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{1})
,
\dots
,
{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{n})
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\chi({\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[h]},O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}\dots\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{n})})=\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},T)}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,h},I_{n,h})}+{\sum}_{k=0}^{h-1}\frac{(-q)^{-\frac{(h-k)(h+k+1)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-k)}}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,k},T)}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n,k},I_{n,k})}.\end{array}

Here,
\chi
is the Euler-Poincaré characteristic and
\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}
is the derived tensor product. Also,
T
is the matrix

T=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(x_{i},x_{j})\end{array}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}.

Proposition 8.9
.

Conjecture
3.3
is equivalent to Conjecture
8.8
.

Proof.

The intersection numbers from both conjectures are by definition the same. Hence we only need to show the analytic sides of both conjectures agree. By Corollary
8.7
, we only need to show
\beta_{t}^{n-h}
from Conjecture
7.6
is the same as
c_{n,t}
from Conjecture
3.3
. Since the
c_{n,t}
is characterized by
\partial\mathrm{Pden}_{n,h}(I_{n,t})=0
for
t\leq h-1
and
t\equiv h+1\pmod{2}
, we only need to show
D_{n,h}(I_{n,t})=0
for
t\leq h-1
and
t\equiv h+1\pmod{2}
. This is proved in Proposition
9.6
via a method we used throughout §
9
so we postpone the proof.
∎

Remark 8.10
.

Before we start to find the constants
D_{n,h}(\lambda)
, let us provide a brief explanation of the forthcoming steps. By Proposition
8.1
, we know that
\dfrac{W^{\prime}_{n-h,n}(B)}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}
is a constant multiple of
\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},T)
. Also, we know how to write
\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n},T)
in terms of a linear sum of representation densities by
[
1
]
and
[
19
, Theorem 3.5.1]
(see Proposition
8.15
below).

Now, we consider
\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},T)-(-q)^{nh}\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n},T)
. Then, it is possible to write the difference between these two in terms of a certain linear sum of representation densities (see (
8.11
)). Therefore, we can use the linear sum expression of
(-q)^{nh}\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n},T)
and the difference
\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},T)-(-q)^{nh}\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n},T)
to find all constants
D_{n,h}(\lambda)
. This is what we will do in the next few pages.

Definition 8.11
.

(1)

For
Y\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}
, we define

t_{0}(Y)=|\{e_{i}\mid e_{i}\geq 0\}|,

and for
k\geq 1
, we define

t_{k}(Y)=|\{e_{i}\mid e_{i}=-k\}|.

(2)

For
\eta\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
, and
k\geq 0
, we define

t_{k}(\eta)=|\{\eta_{i}\mid\eta_{i}=k\}|,

and

t_{\geq k}(\eta)=|\{\eta_{i}\mid\eta_{i}\geq k\}|.

(3)

For
Y\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}
, we define

t(Y)=(t_{0}(Y),t_{1}(Y),\dots).

Similarly, for
\eta\in{\mathcal{R}}^{0+}_{n}
, we define

t(\eta)=(t_{0}(\eta),t_{1}(\eta),\dots).

Definition 8.12
.

(1)

For
\lambda=(\overline{\lambda},\overset{t_{1}(\lambda)}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}},\overset{t_{0}(\lambda)}{\overbrace{0,\dots,0}})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
, and
0\leq s\leq t_{0}(\lambda)
, we define

\lambda_{s}^{+}\coloneqq(\overline{\lambda},\overset{t_{1}(\lambda)+s}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}},\overset{t_{0}(\lambda)-s}{\overbrace{0,\dots,0}}),

by replacing
s
zeros by
s
1’s.

(2)

For
\lambda=(\overline{\lambda},\overset{t_{1}(\lambda)}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}},\overset{t_{0}(\lambda)}{\overbrace{0,\dots,0}})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
and
0\leq s\leq t_{1}(\lambda)
, we define

\lambda_{s}^{-}=(\overline{\lambda},\overset{t_{1}(\lambda)-s}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}},\overset{t_{0}(\lambda)+s}{\overbrace{0,\dots,0}}),

by replacing
s
1’s by
s
zeros.

(3)

For
0\leq l\leq n
and
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}^{0+}_{n}
such that
t_{0}(\lambda)\geq l
, we define
\lambda^{\vee_{l}}
as the element in
{\mathcal{R}}_{n-l}^{0+}
such that
\lambda=(\lambda^{\vee_{l}},0,\dots,0)
.

Definition 8.13
.

Assume that
k\geq 0
,
\alpha,\eta\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
,
Y\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
, and
t(\eta)=t(Y)
.

(1)

We define

{\mathcal{B}}_{k}(Y)={\sum}_{i}\min(0,e_{i}+k)-\min(0,e_{i}),

and

{\mathcal{B}}_{k}(\eta)={\sum}_{i}\min(k,\eta_{i}).

Note that
{\mathcal{B}}_{k}(\eta)={\mathcal{B}}_{k}(Y)
since
t(\eta)=t(Y)
.

(2)

We define

{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha}(Y)={\sum}_{i}{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha_{i}}(Y),

and

{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha}(\eta)={\sum}_{i}{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha_{i}}(\eta).

Note that
{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha}(\eta)={\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha}(Y)
since
t(\eta)=t(Y)
.

(3)

We define
f(Y)
by

f(Y)=\prod_{i}(-q)^{n\min(0,e_{i})}.

By Lemma
7.3
and the fact that
{\mathcal{R}}_{n}
forms a complete set of representatives of
\Gamma_{n}\backslash X_{n}(F)
, we have that

(8.1)

\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},B)={\sum}_{Y\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}}\dfrac{{\mathcal{G}}(Y,B){\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n,h})}{\alpha(Y;\Gamma_{n})},

(8.2)

\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n},B)={\sum}_{Y\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}}\dfrac{{\mathcal{G}}(Y,B){\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n})}{\alpha(Y;\Gamma_{n})},

and for
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
,

(8.3)

\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda},B)={\sum}_{Y\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}}\dfrac{{\mathcal{G}}(Y,B){\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\lambda})}{\alpha(Y;\Gamma_{n})}.

By
[
5
, Lemma 3.2, Section 3.2]
, we know that
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n,h})
can be written uniquely as a linear sum of
{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\lambda})
,
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
. As in the proof of
[
4
, Lemma 3.15]
, we can compute that for

Y=Y_{\sigma,e}=\sigma\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\pi^{e_{1}}&&0\\
&\ddots&\\
0&&\pi^{e_{n}}\end{array}\right),

we have

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n,h})={\sum}_{j}\min(0,e_{j})(-q)^{h{\mathcal{B}}_{1}(Y)}f(Y),

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n})={\sum}_{j}\min(0,e_{j})f(Y),

and

{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\lambda})=(-q)^{{\mathcal{B}}_{\lambda}(Y)}f(Y).

Therefore, we have that (cf.
[
5
, (4.1.1)]
)

(8.4)

\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n,h})-(-q)^{hn}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}0&\text{if }t_{0}(Y)=0,\\
({\sum}_{j}\min(0,e_{j}))f(Y)((-q)^{h(n-1)}-(-q)^{hn})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=1,\\
\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
({\sum}_{j}\min(0,e_{j}))f(Y)((-q)^{h(n-k)}-(-q)^{hn})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=k,\\
\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
({\sum}_{j}\min(0,e_{j}))f(Y)(1-(-q)^{hn})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=n.\par\par\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Now, let us define the following constants and matrices.

Definition 8.14
.

(1)

(cf.
[
5
, Lemma 4.4]
) For
0\leq i\leq l
, we define constants
d_{il}
by

d_{il}=(-q)^{-in}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq m\leq l\\
m\neq i\end{subarray}}\dfrac{1}{((-q)^{-i}-(-q)^{-m})}.

Therefore, we have

\dfrac{d_{il}}{d_{i+1,l}}=-(-q)^{n+i+1-l}\dfrac{(1-(-q)^{-(i+1)})}{(1-(-q)^{(l-i)})}.

(2)

We define the upper triangular
(n+1)\times(n+1)
matrix
\Delta
by

\Delta=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}d_{00}&d_{01}&d_{02}&\dots&d_{0n}\\
0&d_{11}&d_{12}&\dots&d_{1n}\\
0&0&d_{22}&\dots&d_{2n}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&0&\dots&d_{nn}\\
\end{array}\right).

(3)

For
l\leq 0
, we define
{\mathfrak{M}}_{l}
by

{\mathfrak{M}}_{l}:\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}1&(-q)^{2n}&\dots&(-q)^{2ln}\\
1&(-q)^{2n-1}&\dots&(-q)^{l(2n-1)}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
1&(-q)^{2n-l}&\dots&(-q)^{l(2n-l)}\end{array}\right).

(4)

For
0\leq i,j\leq n
, we define constants
{\mathcal{A}}_{ij}
by

({\mathcal{A}}_{ij})_{0\leq i,j\leq n}={\mathfrak{M}}_{n}\Delta.

(5)

For
0\leq i\leq n
, we define constants
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}
by

({\mathcal{A}}_{ij})\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathcal{K}}_{0}\\
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}\end{array}\middle)=\middle(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
(-q)^{h(n-1)}-(-q)^{hn}\\
\vdots\\
1-(-q)^{hn}\end{array}\right).

Proposition 8.15
.

[
5
, Proposition 3.3, Proposition 3.4]
We define the following constants.

(1)

For
\alpha\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
such that
\sum\alpha_{i}=
odd, we write
{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha}
for

{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha}=\prod_{i=1}^{t_{\geq 1}(\alpha)-1}(1-(-q)^{i}).

Here, we define
{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha}=1
if
t_{\geq 1}(\alpha)=1
.

(2)

For
\alpha\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
such that
\sum\alpha_{i}=
even and
\alpha\neq(0,0,\dots,0)
, we write
{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha}
for

{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha}=-\prod_{i=1}^{t_{\geq 1}(\alpha)-1}(1-(-q)^{i}).

Here, we define
{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha}=-1
if
t_{\geq 1}(\alpha)=1
.

Also, if
\alpha=(0,0,\dots,0)
, we define

{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha}=\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n},I_{n})}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n},I_{n})}.

Then, we have that

\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n},B)}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n},I_{n})}={\sum}_{\alpha\in{\mathcal{R}}^{0+}_{n}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\alpha}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\alpha},B)}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\alpha},A_{\alpha})}.

Now, by
[
5
, (4.1.5)]
, we have that for
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
with
t_{0}(Y)\geq l
,

(8.5)

{\sum}_{0\leq i\leq l}d_{il}{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\lambda_{i}^{+}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0=A_{0l}{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\lambda})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=0,\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
0=A_{l-1,l}{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\lambda})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=l-1,\\
(-q)^{{\mathcal{B}}_{\lambda}(Y)}f(Y)={\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\lambda})=A_{ll}{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\lambda})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=l,\\
A_{kl}{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\lambda})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=k,\text{ }l+1\leq k\leq n.\end{array}\right.

Also, by Proposition
8.15
, we have that for
Y\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-l}^{0+}
,

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\dfrac{{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n-l})}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n-l},I_{n-l})}={\sum}_{\overline{\lambda}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-l}^{0+}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\overline{\lambda}}\dfrac{{\mathcal{F}}(Y,A_{\overline{\lambda}})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\overline{\lambda}},A_{\overline{\lambda}})}\\
\Longleftrightarrow&\sum_{i}\min(0,e_{i})f(Y)={\sum}_{\overline{\lambda}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-l}^{0+}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\overline{\lambda}}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n-l},I_{n-l})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\overline{\lambda}},A_{\overline{\lambda}})}(-q)^{{\mathcal{B}}_{\overline{\lambda}}(Y)}f(Y)\\
\Longleftrightarrow&\sum_{i}\min(0,e_{i})={\sum}_{\overline{\lambda}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-l}^{0+}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\overline{\lambda}}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n-l},I_{n-l})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\overline{\lambda}},A_{\overline{\lambda}})}(-q)^{{\mathcal{B}}_{\overline{\lambda}}(Y)}.\end{array}

Since running
\lambda=(\lambda^{\vee_{l}},0,\dots,0)
over
{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
with
t_{0}(Y)\geq l
is equivalent to running
\lambda^{\vee_{l}}
over
{\mathcal{R}}_{n-l}^{0+}
by removing
l
zeros, we have that

(8.6)

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+},t_{0}(Y)\geq l}{\mathcal{C}}_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n-l},I_{n-l})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}},A_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}})}{\sum}_{0\leq i\leq l}d_{il}{\mathcal{F}}_{0}(Y,A_{\lambda_{i}^{+}})\\
\\
=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0\quad=\quad{\mathcal{A}}_{il}\sum_{j}\min(0,e_{j})f(Y)&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)\leq l-1\\
{\mathcal{A}}_{il}\sum_{j}\min(0,e_{j})f(Y)&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)\geq l.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

In Definition
8.14
(5), we defined constants
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}
such that

({\mathcal{A}}_{ij})\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathcal{K}}_{0}\\
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}\end{array}\middle)=\middle(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
(-q)^{h(n-1)}-(-q)^{hn}\\
\vdots\\
1-(-q)^{hn}\end{array}\right).

Therefore, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{l=0}^{n}{\mathcal{K}}_{l}\{{\sum}_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+},t_{0}(Y)\geq l}{\mathcal{C}}_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n-l},I_{n-l})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}},A_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}})}{\sum}_{0\leq i\leq l}d_{il}{\mathcal{F}}_{0}(Y,A_{\lambda_{i}^{+}})\}\\
\\
=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}0&\text{if }t_{0}(Y)=0,\\
({\sum}_{j}\min(0,e_{j}))f(Y)((-q)^{h(n-1)}-(-q)^{hn})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=1,\\
\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
({\sum}_{j}\min(0,e_{j}))f(Y)((-q)^{h(n-k)}-(-q)^{hn})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=k,\\
\quad\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
({\sum}_{j}\min(0,e_{j}))f(Y)(1-(-q)^{hn})&\text{ if }t_{0}(Y)=n.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

By comparing this with (
8.4
), we have that

(8.7)

\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n,h})-(-q)^{hn}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(Y,I_{n})\\
={\sum}_{l=0}^{n}{\mathcal{K}}_{l}\{{\sum}_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+},t_{0}(Y)\geq l}{\mathcal{C}}_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n-l},I_{n-l})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}},A_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}})}{\sum}_{0\leq i\leq l}d_{il}{\mathcal{F}}_{0}(Y,A_{\lambda_{i}^{+}})\}.\end{array}

Now, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 8.16
.

(cf.
[
5
, Lemma 4.5]
) For
i=0
and
h+1\leq i\leq n
,
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}=0
. Also,
d_{hh}{\mathcal{K}}_{h}=1
and for
1\leq l\leq h-1
, we have

d_{h-l,h-l}{\mathcal{K}}_{h-l}=(-q)^{n}\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-h+l-1}}{1-(-q)^{-l}}d_{h-l+1,h-l+1}{\mathcal{K}}_{h-l+1}.

Proof.

As in
[
5
, Lemma 4.3]
, one can show that

(8.8)

\Delta\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathcal{K}}_{0}\\
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\\
\dots\\
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}\\
\dots\\
{\mathcal{K}}_{2n}\end{array}\middle)=\middle(\begin{array}[]{c}-(-q)^{nh}\\
0\\
0\\
1\\
0\\
0\end{array}\right)\begin{array}[]{l}\text{1st entry}\\
\\
\\
\text{(h+1)-th entry}\\
\\
.\end{array}

Since
\Delta
is an upper triangular matrix, we have that
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}=0
for
h+1\leq i\leq n
. Also, the
(h+1)
-th row of (
8.8
) implies that

d_{hh}{\mathcal{K}}_{h}+d_{h,h+1}{\mathcal{K}}_{h+1}+\dots+d_{h,n}{\mathcal{K}}_{n}=1,

and hence
d_{hh}{\mathcal{K}}_{h}=1
. Now, the proof of the last statement is almost identical to the proof of
[
5
, Lemma 4.5]
.
∎

Now, we are ready to write the following proposition on the constants
D_{n,h}(\lambda)
.

Proposition 8.17
.

(cf.
[
5
, Theorem 4.7]
)
For
0\leq h\leq n
and
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
such that
\lambda\neq(1^{t},0^{n}-t),t\leq h-1
, we have

(8.9)

\displaystyle D_{n,h}(\lambda)=

\displaystyle\dfrac{q^{h(n-h)}\prod_{l=1}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\mathcal{C}}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle+{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq h\\
\max\{i-t_{0}(\lambda),0\}\leq s\\
\leq\min\{i,t_{1}(\lambda)\}\end{subarray}}(-q)^{n(h-i)+(i-s)(2n-i+s+1)/2-h^{2}+s(2n-2t_{0}(\lambda)-s)}(-1)^{i+h}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{n-i}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=s+1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-i}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{i-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{l=t_{0}(\lambda)}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{l=t_{1}(\lambda)}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{l=t_{0}(\lambda)-i+s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{l=t_{1}(\lambda)-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{(\lambda_{s}^{-})^{\vee_{i}}}.

Here, we choose the following convention: for
k\leq 0
, we assume that

\prod_{l=1}^{k}(*)=1\quad\text{ and }\quad\sum_{l=1}^{k}(*)=0.

If
\lambda=(1^{t},0^{n-t}),t\leq h-1
, then
D_{n,h}(\lambda)=\text{ the right hand side of \eqref{eq5.10.1}}+\dfrac{(-q)^{\frac{-(h-t)(h+t+1)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t)}}
.
In particular,
D_{n,h}(\lambda)
depends only on
n
,
t_{0}(\lambda)
,
t_{1}(\lambda)
, and the parity of
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}
.

Remark 8.18
.

Note that when
h=0
, the sum
{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq h\\
\max\{i-t_{0}(\lambda),0\}\leq s\leq\min\{i,t_{1}(\lambda)\}\end{subarray}}(*)
in (
8.9
) is empty, and hence
D_{n,0}(\lambda)={\mathcal{C}}_{\lambda}
which is obvious by construction. Therefore, Proposition
8.17
decomposes
D_{n,h}
into a weighted summation of
D_{n,0}
, which is the Cho-Yamauchi constant in the good reduction case.

Proof of Proposition
8.17
.

The proof is almost identical to the proof of
[
5
, Theorem 4.7]
; therefore, let me just write which parts are different. First, note that for
B\in X_{n}(F)
of the form in (
7.1
), Proposition
8.1
implies that

(8.10)

\displaystyle\dfrac{W_{n-h,n}^{\prime}(B)}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{q^{-4n^{2}+(n+h)(n-h)}\mathrm{Den}(\pi A_{n},I_{n-h})\mathrm{Den}(I_{n+h,h},I_{h})\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},T)}{W_{n,n}(A_{n},1)}.

Now, by (
8.1
), (
8.2
), (
8.3
), and (
8.7
), we have

(8.11)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n,h},T)

\displaystyle=(-q)^{nh}\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n},T)+{\sum}_{l=0}^{n}{\mathcal{K}}_{l}\{{\sum}_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+},t_{0}(Y)\geq l}{\mathcal{C}}_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n-l},I_{n-l})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}},A_{\lambda^{\vee_{l}}})}{\sum}_{0\leq i\leq l}d_{il}\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda_{i}^{+}},T)\}.

Now, we can easily follow the proof of
[
5
, Theorem 4.7]
by using Lemma
8.16
, (
8.10
), and (
8.11
). Also, for
\lambda=(1^{t},0^{n-t}),t\leq h-1
, we use Proposition
8.6
.
∎

Note that
D_{n,h}(\lambda)
depends only on
t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)
,
t_{1}(\lambda)
,
t_{0}(\lambda)
, and the parity of
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}
. Also, by Remark
8.4
, we only need to consider
\lambda
such that
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\equiv h+1\pmod{2}
. Also, later on, we will establish inductive formulas relating
D_{n,h}(\lambda)
for
\lambda
with different
t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda)
and
t_{0}(\lambda)
. So we introduce the following notation to streamline the computation.

Definition 8.19
.

Assume that
h,n,a,b,c,i,j,s\geq 0
,
0\leq s\leq i\leq h\leq n
,
0\leq j\leq n
, and
n=a+b+c
.

(1)

If
c\neq n
, we write
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}(a,b,c)
for

{\mathcal{C}}_{j}(a,b,c)=(-1)^{j+1}\prod_{i=1}^{a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{i}).

Also, if
c=n
, we define

{\mathcal{C}}_{j}(0,0,n)=\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n},I_{n})}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n},I_{n})}.

Then, for
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
, we have that

{\mathcal{C}}_{\lambda}={\mathcal{C}}_{|\lambda|}(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda)).

(2)

Let
M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)
be the constant

\displaystyle M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)=

\displaystyle(-q)^{n(h-i)+(i-s)(2n-i+s+1)/2-h^{2}+s(2n-2c-s)}(-1)^{i+h}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{n-i}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=s+1}^{h}((1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-i}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{i-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{c-i+s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{b-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-s}(a,b-s,c+s-i).

(3)

For
(a,b,c)\neq(0,t,n-t),t\leq h-1
, we write
D_{n,h}(a,b,c)
for

D_{n,h}(a,b,c)={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq h\\
\max(i-c,0)\leq s\leq\min(i,b)\end{subarray}}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq\min(h,b)\\
s\leq i\leq\min(s+c,h)\end{subarray}}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s).

(4)

For
(a,b,c)=(0,t,n-t),t\leq h-1
,
t\equiv h+1\pmod{2}
, we write
D_{n,h}(a,b,c)
for

\displaystyle D_{n,h}(0,t,n-t)

\displaystyle={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq h\\
\max(i-c,0)\leq s\leq\min(i,b)\end{subarray}}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)+\dfrac{(-q)^{\frac{-(h-t)(h+t+1)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t)}}

\displaystyle={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq\min(h,b)\\
s\leq i\leq\min(s+c,h)\end{subarray}}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)+\dfrac{(-q)^{\frac{-(h-t)(h+t+1)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t)}}.

Proposition 8.20
.

For
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
, we have that

D_{n,h}(\lambda)=D_{n,h}(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda)).

Proof.

This follows from the definitions of
M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)
,
D_{n,h}(a,b,c)
, Proposition
8.17
, and the fact that

M_{n,h}(a,b,c,0,0)=\dfrac{q^{h(n-h)}\prod_{l=1}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1}(a,b,c).

∎

9.
Inductive relations among Cho-Yamauchi constants

In this section, we will prove some inductive relations among
D_{n,h}(\lambda)
. The main result of this section can be summarized as follows: Let
0\leq a,b,c,h,\leq n
,
a+b+c=n
. Assume further that
(a-1,b+1,c)\neq(0,h,n-h)
. Then, we have the following result.

(1)

(Theorem
9.4
) If
c\leq n-h
and
a\geq 1
, then

D_{n,h}(a,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c)=-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}D_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c).

(2)

(Lemma
9.5
, Proposition
9.6
) If
c>n-h
, then
D_{n,h}(a,b,c)=0
.

(3)

(Theorem
9.8
(1)) Assume that
a=0
and
h+1\leq b
. Then, we have

D_{n,h}(0,b,c)=\dfrac{\prod_{l=h+1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{l})}{(1-(-q)^{b-h})}.

(4)

(Theorem
9.8
(2)) Assume that
a=1
and
h-1\leq b
. Then, we have

D_{n,h}(1,b,c)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\text{ if }b=h-1,h;\\
\prod_{l=h+1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{l})&\text{ if }b\geq h+1.\end{array}\right.

Here, the main reason for excluding the case
(a-1,b+1,c)=(0,h,n-h)
is because it is not necessary by Remark
8.4
.

First, let us prove the following lemma.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
0\leq a,b,c,h\leq n
,
a+b+c=n
, and
0\leq s\leq\min(h,b)
.

(1)

If
c\leq n-h
and
(a,b,c)\neq(0,h,n-h)
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+c+s+1}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-h^{2}-\frac{n}{2}-cn+hn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-ch+\frac{s}{2}-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{b-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

(2)

If
c>n-h
and
a\neq 0
, we have that
\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)=0
.

Proof.

By our assumption, we have that
(a,b-s,c+s-i)\neq(0,0,n-i)
for any
i\leq s\leq\min(s+c,h)
. Therefore, by Definition
8.19
(1), we have that

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-s}(a,b-s,c+s-i)&=(-1)^{h-s}\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s-1}(1-(-q)^{l})\\
&=(-1)^{n+c+h+1}(-q)^{(n-c-s-1)(n-c-s)/2}\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l}),\end{array}

which is independent of
i
.
Also, by Definition
8.19
(2), we have that

M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)={\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-s}(a,b-s,c+s-i)Y_{n,h}(b,c,s)X_{n,h}(c,i,s),

where

\displaystyle Y_{n,h}(b,c,s)

\displaystyle\coloneqq(-1)^{h}(-q)^{-h^{2}+hn-\frac{s}{2}-\frac{3s^{2}}{2}-2cs+ns}\dfrac{{\prod}_{l=1}^{c}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{{\prod}_{l=1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{s}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{b-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})},

which is independent of
i
, and

\displaystyle X_{n,h}(c,i,s)\coloneqq\dfrac{(-1)^{i}(-q)^{\frac{i}{2}-\frac{i^{2}}{2}+is}{\prod}_{l=1}^{n-i}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{{\prod}_{l=1}^{h-i}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{i-s}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{c-i+s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

Now, we note that Lemma
9.1
follows from the following claim

(9.1)

\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}X_{n,h}(c,i,s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\dfrac{(-1)^{s}(-q)^{-ch+cs+\frac{s}{2}+\frac{s^{2}}{2}}{\prod}_{l=1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{n-s-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{{\prod}_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{c}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})}&\text{ if }c\leq n-h,\\
\\
0&\text{ if }c>n-h.\par\end{array}\right.

Hence it suffices to show that (
9.1
) holds.

If
h=s
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}X_{n,h}(c,i,s)

\displaystyle=X_{n,h}(c,s,s)=\dfrac{(-1)^{s}(-q)^{\frac{s}{2}+\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{c}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

Therefore, (
9.1
) holds in this case.

Similarly, if
c=0
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}X_{n,h}(c,i,s)

\displaystyle=X_{n,h}(c,s,s)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(-1)^{s}(-q)^{\frac{s}{2}+\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

Therefore, (
9.1
) holds in this case.

Now, assume that
c\geq 1
and
h>s
. In Lemma
9.2
below, we will prove that

(9.2)

\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}=\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c-1,h)}\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,i,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}.

Assume (
9.2
) holds for now. Then we are ready to prove (
9.1
).

If
c\leq n-h
, by applying (
9.2
) repeatedly, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}

\displaystyle=(-q)^{-c(h-s)}\dfrac{\prod_{l=n-h-c+1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=n-s-c+1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+0,h)}\dfrac{X_{n-c,h}(0,i,s)}{X_{n-c,h}(0,s,s)}

\displaystyle=(-q)^{-c(h-s)}\dfrac{\prod_{l=n-h-c+1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=n-s-c+1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

Combining this with

\displaystyle X_{n,h}(c,s,s)=\dfrac{(-1)^{s}(-q)^{\frac{s}{2}+\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{c}(1-(-q)^{-l})},

we see that (
9.1
) holds when
c\leq n-h
.

Now, assume that
c>n-h
. By (
9.2
), we have that

\displaystyle{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}=(-q)^{-(n-h)(h-s)}\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=h-s+1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c-n+h,h)}\dfrac{X_{h,h}(c-n+h,i,s)}{X_{h,h}(c-n+h,s,s)}.

Therefore, it suffices to show that
{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c-n+h,h)}\dfrac{X_{h,h}(c-n+h,i,s)}{X_{h,h}(c-n+h,s,s)}=0
.

Note that
s\leq\min(h,b)
and hence
s+c\leq b+c\leq n
. Therefore,
\min(s+c-n+h,h)=s+c-n+h
. For simplicity, let us write
c^{\prime}=c-n+h\geq 1
.

We claim that

(9.3)

{\sum}_{i=s}^{s+k}\dfrac{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},i,s)}{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},s,s)}=(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k+1)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=c^{\prime}-k}^{c^{\prime}-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})},

which specializes to

{\sum}_{i=s}^{s+c^{\prime}}\dfrac{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},i,s)}{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},s,s)}=0

when
k=c^{\prime}
.

Therefore it suffices to prove (
9.3
). We prove this by induction on
k
. Recall that by definition, we have

\dfrac{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},s+k,s)}{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},s,s)}=(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k-1)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=c^{\prime}-k+1}^{c^{\prime}}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

When
k=0
, both sides are
1
. When
k=1
,

\displaystyle{\sum}_{i=s}^{s+1}\dfrac{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},i,s)}{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},s,s)}=1-\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-c^{\prime}}}{1-(-q)^{-1}}=-(-q)^{-1}\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-(c^{\prime}-1)}}{1-(-q)^{-1}}.

Now, assume that (
9.3
) holds for
k
. Then, we have

\displaystyle{\sum}_{i=s}^{s+k+1}\dfrac{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},i,s)}{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},s,s)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k+1)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=c^{\prime}-k}^{c^{\prime}-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}+\dfrac{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},s+k+1,s)}{X_{h,h}(c^{\prime},s,s)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k+1)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=c^{\prime}-k}^{c^{\prime}-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\{}1-\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-c^{\prime}}}{1-(-q)^{-k-1}}{\}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k+1}(-q)^{-\frac{(k+1)(k+2)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=c^{\prime}-k-1}^{c^{\prime}-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k+1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

This shows that (
9.3
) holds and finishes the proof of (
9.1
), and hence the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 9.2
.

For
c\geq 1
and
h>s
, the function
X_{n,h}(c,i,s)
(defined in the proof of the Lemma
9.1
) satisfies

(9.4)

\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}=\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c-1,h)}\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,i,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}.

Proof.

To prove (
9.4
), we define
Z_{n,h}(c,j,s)
to be

Z_{n,h}(c,j,s)=(-1)^{j-s}(-q)^{-\frac{(j-s)(j-s+1)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=c-j+s}^{c-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-j+1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{j-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=n-j+1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

Note that if
c\geq 1
and
h>s
, we have that
\min(s+c,h)>s
.

Now, we claim the following statement: for
k\geq 1
, we have

(9.5)

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{i=s}^{s+k}\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}-\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}{\sum}_{i=s}^{s+k-1}\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,i,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}=Z_{n,h}(c,s+k,s).\end{array}

By definition, we have that

\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}=(-1)^{i-s}(-q)^{-\frac{(i-s)(i-s-1)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=c-i+s+1}^{c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-i+1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{i-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=n-i+1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

In particular, when
k=1
, we have that

\displaystyle{\sum}_{i=s}^{s+1}\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}

\displaystyle=1-\dfrac{(1-(-q)^{-c})(1-(-q)^{-(h-s)})}{(1-(-q)^{-1})(1-(-q)^{-(n-s)})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}-\dfrac{(-q)^{-1}(1-(-q)^{-(c-1)})(1-(-q)^{-(h-s)})}{(1-(-q)^{-1})(1-(-q)^{-(n-s)})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}+Z_{n,h}(c,s+1,s).

Therefore, (
9.5
) holds for
k=1
.

Now, assume that (
9.5
) is true for
k
, then it suffices to show that

(9.6)

\begin{array}[]{l}Z_{n,h}(c,s+k,s)+\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,s+k+1,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}\\
=\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s+k,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}+Z_{n,h}(c,s+k+1,s).\end{array}

Indeed,

\begin{array}[]{l}Z_{n,h}(c,s+k,s)+\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,s+k+1,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}\\
=(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k+1)}{2}}\dfrac{{\prod}_{l=c-k}^{c-1}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=h-s-k+1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{{\prod}_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=n-s-k+1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{{\{}}1-\dfrac{(1-(-q)^{-c})(1-(-q)^{-(h-s-k)})}{(1-(-q)^{-(k+1)})(1-(-q)^{-(-n-s-k)}}{{\}}},\end{array}

and

\displaystyle 1-\dfrac{(1-(-q)^{-c})(1-(-q)^{-(h-s-k)})}{(1-(-q)^{-(k+1)})(1-(-q)^{-(-n-s-k)})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(-q)^{k}(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s-k)}}

\displaystyle-\dfrac{(-q)^{-(k+1)}(1-(-q)^{-(-c-k-1)})(1-(-q)^{-(h-s-k)})}{(1-(-q)^{-(k+1)})(1-(-q)^{-(n-s-k)})}.

Now, note that

\displaystyle(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k+1)}{2}}\frac{\prod_{l=c-k}^{c-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-s-k+1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=n-s-k+1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\times\dfrac{(-q)^{k}(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s-k)}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k-1)}{2}}\frac{\prod_{l=c-k}^{c-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-s-k+1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=n-s-k}^{n-s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\times\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s+k,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}},

and

\displaystyle(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k+1)}{2}}\frac{\prod_{l=c-k}^{c-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-s-k+1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=n-s-k+1}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle\times\{-\dfrac{(-q)^{-(k+1)}(1-(-q)^{-(-c-k-1)})(1-(-q)^{-(h-s-k)})}{(1-(-q)^{-(k+1)})(1-(-q)^{-(n-s-k)})}\}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{k+1}(-q)^{-\frac{(k+1)(k+2)}{2}}\frac{\prod_{l=c-k-1}^{c-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-s-k}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k+1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=n-s-k}^{n-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle=Z_{n,h}(c,s+k+1,s).

Therefore, combining these two, we have that (
9.6
) holds, and hence (
9.5
) holds.

Now, we are ready to prove (
9.4
).
When
h\geq s+c
, we have that
\min(s+c,h)=s+c
and
\min(s+c-1,h)=s+c-1
. Also, by (
9.5
), we have

{\sum}_{i=s}^{s+c}\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}-{\sum}_{i=s}^{s+c-1}\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,i,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}=Z_{n,h}(c,s+c,s).

Now, note that
Z_{n,h}(c,s+c,s)=0
. This implies that (
9.4
) holds when
h\geq s+c
.

When
h\leq s+c-1
, we have that
\min(s+c,h)=h
and
\min(s+c-1,h)=h
. Therefore, by (
9.5
), we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}-\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c-1,h)}\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,i,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}\\
={\sum}_{i=s}^{h}\dfrac{X_{n,h}(c,i,s)}{X_{n,h}(c,s,s)}-\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}{\sum}_{i=s}^{h}\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,i,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}\\
=Z_{n,h}(c,h,s)-\dfrac{(-q)^{-(h-s)}(1-(-q)^{-(n-h)})}{1-(-q)^{-(n-s)}}\dfrac{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,h,s)}{X_{n-1,h}(c-1,s,s)}\\
=0.\end{array}

This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
0\leq a,b,c,h\leq n
,
a+b+c=n
, and
0\leq s\leq\min(h,b)
.
If
c\leq n-h
and
(a,b,c)\neq(0,h,n-h)
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)-\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,s)\\
=-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}\sum_{i=s-1}^{\min(s+c-1,h-1)}M_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c,i,s-1).\end{array}

Proof.

By Lemma
9.1
(1), we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)-\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,s)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+c+s+1}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-h^{2}-\frac{n}{2}-cn+hn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-ch+\frac{s}{2}-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}

\displaystyle\times{{{\{}}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{b-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{b+1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{b+1-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{{{\}}}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+c+s}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-h^{2}-\frac{n}{2}-cn+hn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-ch+\frac{s}{2}-\frac{s^{2}}{2}-(b+1-s)}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{b+1-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}\sum_{i=s-1}^{\min(s+c-1,h-1)}M_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c,i,s-1)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+c+s}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-(h-1)^{2}-\frac{n-1}{2}-c(n-1)+(h-1)(n-1)+\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{2}-c(h-1)+\frac{s-1}{2}-\frac{(s-1)^{2}}{2}+2n-h-1-b-2c}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{b+1-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+c+s}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-h^{2}-\frac{n}{2}-cn+hn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-ch+\frac{3s}{2}-\frac{s^{2}}{2}-b-1}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{b+1-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{s-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

Now, it is easy to see that the above two are the same. This finishes the proof of the lemma.

∎

Theorem 9.4
.

Let
0\leq a,b,c,h\leq n
and
a+b+c=n
. If
c\leq n-h
,
1\leq a
, and
(a-1,b+1,c)\neq(0,h,n-h)
, we have that

D_{n,h}(a,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c)=-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}D_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c).

Proof.

First, by Lemma
9.1
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\min(0+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,0)=

\displaystyle(-1)^{n+c+1}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-h^{2}-\frac{n}{2}-cn+hn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-ch}

\displaystyle\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{n-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

Since this does not depend on
a,b
, we have that

\sum_{i=0}^{\min(0+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,0)=\sum_{i=0}^{\min(0+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,0).

Since

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a,b,c)={\sum}_{s=0}^{\min(h,b)}{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s),\end{array}

we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c)\\
\\
={\sum}_{s=0}^{\min(h,b)}{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)-{\sum}_{s=0}^{\min(h,b+1)}{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,s)\\
\\
={\sum}_{s=1}^{\min(h,b)}{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)-{\sum}_{s=1}^{\min(h,b+1)}{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,s).\end{array}

Now, assume that
h\leq b
. Then
\min(h,b)=h
,
\min(h,b+1)=h
,
\min(h-1,b)=h-1
. Therefore, by Lemma
9.3
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c)\\
={\sum}_{s=1}^{h}\{{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)-{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,s)\}\\
={\sum}_{s=0}^{h-1}-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h-1)}M_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c,i,s)\\
=-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}D_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c).\end{array}

This finishes the proof of the theorem when
h\leq b
.

Now, assume that
h>b
. Then
\min(h,b)=b
,
\min(h,b+1)=b+1
. Therefore, by Lemma
9.3
, we have that

(9.7)

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c)\\
={\sum}_{s=1}^{b}\{{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)-{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,s)\}\\
\hskip 10.00002pt-{\sum}_{i=b+1}^{\min(b+1+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,b+1)\\
={\sum}_{s=0}^{b-1}-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h-1)}M_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c,i,s)\\
\hskip 10.00002pt-{\sum}_{i=b+1}^{\min(b+1+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,b+1).\end{array}

By Lemma
9.1
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}-{\sum}_{i=b+1}^{\min(b+1+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c,i,b+1)\\
=-(-1)^{n+c+b}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-h^{2}-\frac{n}{2}-cn+hn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-ch+\frac{(b+1)}{2}-\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{2}}\\
\quad\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-b-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-b-2}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-b-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
=-(-1)^{n-1+c+b+1}(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c+\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-(h-1)^{2}-\frac{n-1}{2}-c(n-1)+(h-1)(n-1)+\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{2}-c(h-1)+\frac{b}{2}-\frac{b^{2}}{2}}\\
\quad\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{n-1-c-b}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-1-c-b-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{(n-1)-(h-1)-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-1-b}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
=-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}{\sum}_{i=b}^{\min(b+c,h-1)}M_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c,i,b).\end{array}

Therefore, since
\min(h-1,b)=b
, (
9.7
) can be written as

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a-1,b+1,c)\\
=-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}\hskip 10.00002pt{\sum}_{s=0}^{b}\hskip 11.74988pt{\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h-1)}M_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c,i,s)\\
=-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}D_{n-1,h-1}(a-1,b,c).\end{array}

This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
0\leq a,b,c,h\leq n
, and
a+b+c=n
. If
c>n-h
and
a\neq 0
, we have that

D_{n,h}(a,b,c)=0.

Therefore, for
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
such that
t_{0}(\lambda)>n-h
, and
t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)\neq 0
, we have that

D_{n,h}(\lambda)=0.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
9.1
(2), Definition
8.19
(3) and Proposition
8.20
.
∎

Proposition 9.6
.

For
0\leq t\leq h-1
,
t\equiv h+1\pmod{2}
, and
\lambda=(1^{t},0^{n-t})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
, we have that

D_{n,h}(\lambda)=0.

Proof.

First, by Proposition
8.20
, we have that

D_{n,h}(\lambda)=D_{n,h}(0,t,n-t)={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq\min(h,b)\\
s\leq i\leq\min(s+c,h)\end{subarray}}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)+\dfrac{(-q)^{\frac{-(h-t)(h+t+1)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t)}}.

Before we start the proof of the proposition, let us say why we consider this case separately. Recall from Definition
8.19
(1) that
{\mathcal{C}}_{l}(0,0,k)
is defined separately. If
\lambda
is not of the form
(1^{t},0^{n-t})
,
t<h
, these constants
{\mathcal{C}}_{l}(0,0,k)
’s do not appear in the sum
{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq\min(h,b)\\
s\leq i\leq\min(s+c,h)\end{subarray}}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s).
However, if
\lambda=(1^{t},0^{n-t})
,
t<h
,
M_{n,h}(0,t,n-t,i,t)
has the term
{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-t}(0,0,n-i)
. Therefore, we need to be careful when we compute
D_{n,h}(0,t,n-t)
.

Now, let us start the proof of the proposition. Recall from the proof of the Lemma
9.1
that we have

M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)={\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-s}(a,b-s,c+s-i)Y_{n,h}(b,c,s)X_{n,h}(c,i,s),

Also, by (
9.1
), we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+n-t,h)}Y_{n,h}(t,n-t,s)X_{n,h}(n-t,i,s)=Y_{n,h}(t,n-t,s)\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+n-t,h)}X_{n,h}(n-t,i,s)=0\end{array}

Also, note that
\min(h,t)=t
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-s}(0,t-s,n-t+s-i)
does not depend on
i
if
s\neq t
. Therefore, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(0,t,n-t)-\dfrac{(-q)^{\frac{-(h-t)(h+t+1)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t)}}\\
={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq s\leq\min(h,t)\\
s\leq i\leq\min(s+n-t,h)\end{subarray}}{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-s}(0,t-s,n-t+s-i)Y_{n,h}(t,n-t,s)X_{n,h}(n-t,i,s)\\
={\sum}_{s=0}^{t-1}{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-s}(0,t-s,n-t+s)Y_{n,h}(t,n-t,s){\sum}_{i=s}^{\min(s+n-t,h)}X_{n,h}(n-t,i,s)\\
\hskip 10.00002pt+{\sum}_{i=t}^{h}{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-t}(0,0,n-i)Y_{n,h}(t,n-t,t)X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)\\
\\
={\sum}_{i=t}^{h}{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-t}(0,0,n-i)Y_{n,h}(t,n-t,t)X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t).\end{array}

Now, recall that
{\mathcal{C}}_{h+1-t}(0,0,n-i)=\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}^{\prime}(I_{n-i},I_{n-i})}{\mathrm{Den}(I_{n-i},I_{n-i})}={\sum}_{l=1}^{n-i}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{l}-1}.

This implies that it suffices to show that

(9.8)

Y_{n,h}(t,n-t,t){\sum}_{i=t}^{h}({\sum}_{l=1}^{n-i}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{l}-1})X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)=-\dfrac{(-q)^{\frac{-(h-t)(h+t+1)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t)}}.

First, we have that

\displaystyle{\sum}_{i=t}^{h}({\sum}_{l=1}^{n-i}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{l}-1})X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\sum}_{i=t}^{h}X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)({\sum}_{l=1}^{n-h}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{l}-1})

\displaystyle+{\sum}_{k=0}^{h-t-1}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{n-t-k}-1}{\sum}_{i=t}^{t+k}X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t).

Since
{\sum}_{i=t}^{h}X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)=0
(by (
9.1
)), we have that

(9.9)

{\sum}_{i=t}^{h}({\sum}_{l=1}^{n-i}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{l}-1})X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)={\sum}_{k=0}^{h-t-1}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{n-t-k}-1}{\sum}_{i=t}^{t+k}X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t).

We claim that

(9.10)

{\sum}_{i=t}^{t+k}X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)=\dfrac{(-1)^{t+k}(-q)^{\frac{(t+k+1)(t-k)}{2}}}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-t-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})(1-(-q)^{-(h-t)})}.

Indeed, it is easy to see that this holds for
k=0
. Now, assume that this holds for
k
. Then, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{i=t}^{t+k+1}X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)={\sum}_{i=t}^{t+k+1}\dfrac{(-1)^{i}(-q)^{\frac{i}{2}-\frac{i^{2}}{2}+it}}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-i}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{i-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
=\dfrac{(-1)^{t+k}(-q)^{\frac{(t+k+1)(t-k)}{2}}}{{\prod}_{l=1}^{h-t-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})(1-(-q)^{-(h-t)})}+\dfrac{(-1)^{t+k+1}(-q)^{\frac{t+k+1}{2}-\frac{(t+k+1)^{2}}{2}+(t+k+1)t}}{{\prod}_{l=1}^{h-t-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l}){\prod}_{l=1}^{k+1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
=\dfrac{(-1)^{t+k+1}(-q)^{\frac{(t+k+1)(t-k)}{2}}}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-t-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k+1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\{-\dfrac{(1-(-q)^{-(k+1)})}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t)}}+1\}\\
=\dfrac{(-1)^{t+k+1}(-q)^{\frac{(t+k+1)(t-k)}{2}}}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-t-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k+1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\dfrac{(-q)^{-(k+1)}(1-(-q)^{-h-t+k+1})}{(1-(-q)^{-(h-t)}}\\
=\dfrac{(-1)^{t+k+1}(-q)^{\frac{(t+k+2)(t-k-1)}{2}}}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-t-k-2}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k+1}(1-(-q)^{-l})(1-(-q)^{-(h-t)})}.\end{array}

This shows that (
9.10
) holds.

Now, by (
9.10
), we have that (
9.9
) can be written as

(9.11)

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\sum}_{i=t}^{h}({\sum}_{l=1}^{n-i}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{l}-1})X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)={\sum}_{k=0}^{h-t-1}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{n-t-k}-1}{\sum}_{i=t}^{t+k}X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)\\
=\dfrac{(-1)^{t}(-q)^{\frac{t^{2}}{2}+\frac{3t}{2}-n}}{(1-(-q)^{-(h-t)})}\times{\sum}_{k=0}^{h-t-1}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k-1)}{2}}}{(1-(-q)^{-(n-t-k)})\prod_{l=1}^{h-t-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.\end{array}

In Lemma
9.7
below, we will prove that

(9.12)

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{k=0}^{h-t-1}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k-1)}{2}}}{(1-(-q)^{-(n-t-k)})\prod_{l=1}^{h-t-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
=\dfrac{(-1)^{h-t-1}(-q)^{-\frac{(h-t)(h-t-1)}{2}}(-q)^{-(n-h)(h-t-1)}}{\prod_{l=n-h+1}^{n-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.\end{array}

Combining (
9.11
) and (
9.12
), we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}Y_{n,h}(t,n-t,t){\sum}_{i=t}^{h}({\sum}_{l=1}^{n-i}\dfrac{1}{(-q)^{l}-1})X_{n,h}(n-t,i,t)\\
=\dfrac{(-1)^{h}(-q)^{-h^{2}+hn-\frac{t}{2}+\frac{t^{2}}{2}-nt}\prod_{l=1}^{n-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{n-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
\times\dfrac{(-1)^{t}(-q)^{\frac{t^{2}}{2}+\frac{3t}{2}-n}}{(1-(-q)^{-(h-t)})}\times\dfrac{(-1)^{h-t-1}(-q)^{-\frac{(h-t)(h-t-1)}{2}}(-q)^{-(n-h)(h-t-1)}}{\prod_{l=n-h+1}^{n-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
=\dfrac{-(-q)^{\frac{-(h-t)(h+t+1)}{2}}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t)}}.\par\end{array}

This finishes the proof of the proposition.

∎

Lemma 9.7
.

For
0\leq t<h\leq n
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{k=0}^{h-t-1}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k-1)}{2}}}{(1-(-q)^{-(n-t-k)})\prod_{l=1}^{h-t-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
\\
=\dfrac{(-1)^{h-t-1}(-q)^{-\frac{(h-t)(h-t-1)}{2}}(-q)^{-(n-h)(h-t-1)}}{\prod_{l=n-h+1}^{n-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.\end{array}

Proof.

For
N>0
, we claim the following statement:

(9.13)

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\frac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k-1)}{2}}}{(1-(-q)^{-(N-k)}X)\prod_{l=1}^{N-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}=\frac{(-1)^{N-1}(-q)^{-\frac{N(N-1)}{2}}X^{N-1}}{\prod_{l=1}^{N}(1-(-q)^{-l}X)},

which specializes to the statement of the lemma when
X=(-q)^{-(n-h)}
. Therefore it suffices to show the claim (
9.13
).

Consider a Vandermonde matrix

{\mathfrak{X}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}1&1&\dots&1\\
x_{1}&x_{2}&\dots&x_{N}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
x_{1}^{N-1}&x_{2}^{N-1}&\dots&x_{N}^{N-1}\end{array}\right),

and let
{\mathfrak{X}}^{-1}=(y_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
. Note that
y_{ij}
is the
X^{N-j}
-coefficient of

\prod_{l=1,l\neq i}^{N}\dfrac{1-x_{l}X}{x_{i}-x_{l}}.

Now, assume that
x_{l}=(-q)^{-l}
. Then, we have that
y_{(N-k)j}
is the
X^{N-j}
-coefficient of

\begin{array}[]{l}{\prod}_{l=1,l\neq(N-k)}^{N}\dfrac{(1-(-q)^{-l}X)}{(-q)^{-(N-k)}-(-q)^{-l}}=\dfrac{(-1)^{N-k-1}(-q)^{\frac{(N-k)(N+k-1)}{2}}\prod_{l=1,l\neq(N-k)}^{N}(1-(-q)^{-l}X)}{\prod_{l=1}^{N-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
\\
=(-1)^{N-1}(-q)^{\frac{N(N-1)}{2}}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k-1)}{2}}\prod_{l=1,l\neq(N-k)}^{N}(1-(-q)^{-l}X)}{\prod_{l=1}^{N-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.\end{array}

Therefore, we have that

(9.14)

{\sum}_{j=1}^{N}{\sum}_{k=0}^{N-1}y_{(N-k)j}X^{N-j}=(-1)^{N-1}(-q)^{\frac{N(N-1)}{2}}{\sum}_{k=0}^{N-1}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-\frac{k(k-1)}{2}}\prod_{l=1,l\neq N-k}^{N}(1-(-q)^{-l}X)}{\prod_{l=1}^{N-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

Now, note that

(1\quad 0\dots 0){\mathfrak{X}}=(1\quad 1\dots 1)\Longleftrightarrow(1\quad 0\dots 0)=(1\quad 1\dots 1){\mathfrak{X}}^{-1}=({\sum}_{i=1}^{N}y_{i1}\hskip 11.74988pt{\sum}_{i=1}^{N}y_{i2}\dots{\sum}_{i=1}^{N}y_{iN}).

Hence, we have

(9.15)

{\sum}_{j=1}^{N}{\sum}_{k=0}^{N-1}y_{(N-k)j}X^{N-j}=X^{N-1}.

Therefore, (
9.14
) and (
9.15
) imply that (
9.13
) holds.
This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Now, let us state the following theorem.

Theorem 9.8
.

Assume that
0\leq h\leq n
.

(1)

Assume that
h+1\leq b\leq n
, and
b+c=n
. Then, we have

D_{n,h}(0,b,c)=\dfrac{\prod_{l=h+1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{l})}{(1-(-q)^{b-h})}.

(2)

Assume that
h-1\leq b\leq n
, and
b+c=n-1
. Then, we have

D_{n,h}(1,b,c)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\text{ if }b=h-1,h,\\
\prod_{l=h+1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{l})&\text{ if }b\geq h+1.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Recall from Lemma
9.1
that for
a=0
or
1
(i.e.,
b+c=n
or
n-1
, respectively), we have

(9.16)

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a,b,c)=\sum_{s=0}^{\min(h,b)}\sum_{i=s}^{\min(s+c,h)}M_{n,h}(a,b,c,i,s)\\
=(-1)^{n+c+1}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-h^{2}-\frac{n}{2}-cn+hn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-ch}\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-a}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})}\\
\quad\times\sum_{s=0}^{\min(h,b)}\dfrac{(-1)^{s}(-q)^{\frac{s}{2}-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s+a-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.\par\end{array}

First, assume that
a=1
,
b=h-1
, and
c=n-h
. Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{s=0}^{\min(h,b)}\dfrac{(-1)^{s}(-q)^{\frac{s}{2}-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-s+a-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}={\sum}_{s=0}^{h-1}\dfrac{(-1)^{s}(-q)^{\frac{s}{2}-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}}{\prod_{l=1}^{s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}=\dfrac{(-1)^{h-1}(-q)^{-\frac{h(h-1)}{2}}}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.\end{array}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle D_{n,h}(1,h-1,n-h)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+c+h}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-h^{2}-\frac{n}{2}-cn+hn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-ch-h(h-1)/2}\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{h-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle=(-q)^{\frac{(n-c-h)(n-c+3h-1)}{2}}=1.

Now, assume that
b\geq h
. In this case, we have that
\min(h,b)=h
. We claim that

(9.17)

{\sum}_{s=0}^{h}\dfrac{(-1)^{s}(-q)^{\frac{s}{2}-\frac{s^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{s}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-s}(1-(-q)^{-l})}=\dfrac{(-1)^{h}(-q)^{-hN+\frac{h(h-1)}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})},

where
N=n-c+a-1
.

To prove (
9.17
), we define the following constants: for
0\leq k\leq h
,
1\leq t\leq k+1
,

\displaystyle\omega_{k,t}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-(t-1)N+\frac{(t-1)(t-2)}{2}-\frac{(k-t+1)(k-t+2)}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k-t+1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-t+1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}&\text{ if }k\leq h-1,\\
0&\text{ if }k=h,t\neq h+1,\\
\dfrac{(-1)^{h}(-q)^{-hN+\frac{h(h-1)}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}&\text{ if }k=h,t=h+1,\end{array}\right.

and

\begin{array}[]{l}\tau_{k,t}=\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-tN+\frac{t(t-1)}{2}-\frac{(k-t-1)(k-t)}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-t+1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.\end{array}

Note that

\omega_{0,1}=\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{N}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}=\dfrac{(-1)^{0}(-q)^{\frac{0}{2}-\frac{0^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-0}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{0}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-0}(1-(-q)^{-l})},

and

\tau_{k,k}=\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-kN+\frac{k(k-1)}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}=\omega_{k,k+1}.

We claim the following two equations: for
k\geq 1
,
2\leq t\leq k
, we have

(9.18)

\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{\frac{k}{2}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}+\omega_{k-1,1}=\omega_{k,1}+\tau_{k,1},

and

(9.19)

\tau_{k,t-1}+\omega_{k-1,t}=\omega_{k,t}+\tau_{k,t}.

First, we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{\frac{k}{2}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}+\omega_{k-1,1}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{\frac{k}{2}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\{}\dfrac{1}{1-(-q)^{-k}}-\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-(N-k+1)}}{1-(-q)^{-h}}{\}}
