\displaystyle=\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{\frac{k}{2}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\{}\dfrac{(-q)^{-k}(1-(-q)^{-(h-k)})}{(1-(-q)^{-k})(1-(-q)^{-h})}+\dfrac{(-q)^{-(N-k+1)}}{1-(-q)^{-h}}{\}}

\displaystyle=\omega_{k,1}+\tau_{k,1}.

This shows that (
9.18
) holds.

For (
9.19
), we have

\displaystyle\tau_{k,t-1}+\omega_{k-1,t}=

\displaystyle\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-(t-1)N+\frac{(t-1)(t-2)}{2}-\frac{(k-t)(k-t+1)}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-t+2}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle\times{\{}\dfrac{1}{(1-(-q)^{-(k-t+1)}}-\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-(N-k+1)}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t+1)}}{\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{-(t-1)N+\frac{(t-1)(t-2)}{2}-\frac{(k-t)(k-t+1)}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k-t}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=h-t+2}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle\times{\{}\dfrac{(-q)^{-(k-t+1)}(1-(-q)^{-(h-k)})}{(1-(-q)^{-(k-t+1)})(1-(-q)^{-(h-t+1)})}+\dfrac{(-q)^{-(N-k+1)}}{1-(-q)^{-(h-t+1)}}{\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{k,t}+\tau_{k,t}.

This shows that (
9.19
) holds.
Now, by (
9.18
) and (
9.19
), we have that for
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{\frac{k}{2}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}+{\sum}_{t=1}^{k}\omega_{k-1,t}+{\sum}_{t=1}^{k-1}\tau_{k,t}={\sum}_{t=1}^{k}\omega_{k,t}+{\sum}_{t=1}^{k}\tau_{k,t},

and hence

(9.20)

\displaystyle\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{\frac{k}{2}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}+{\sum}_{t=1}^{k}\omega_{k-1,t}={\sum}_{t=1}^{k}\omega_{k,t}+\tau_{k,k}={\sum}_{t=1}^{k+1}\omega_{k,t}.

Here, we used
\tau_{k,k}=\omega_{k,k+1}
for the last identity.

Therefore, by (
9.20
), we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\sum}_{k=1}^{h}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{\frac{k}{2}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}+{\sum}_{k=1}^{h}{\sum}_{t=1}^{k}\omega_{k-1,t}={\sum}_{k=1}^{h}{\sum}_{t=1}^{k+1}\omega_{k,t}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\sum}_{k=1}^{h}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{\frac{k}{2}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}+\omega_{0,1}={\sum}_{t=1}^{h+1}\omega_{h,t}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\sum}_{k=0}^{h}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}(-q)^{\frac{k}{2}-\frac{k^{2}}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{k}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h-k}(1-(-q)^{-l})}=\omega_{h,h+1}\text{ (since $\omega_{h,t}=0$ for all $t<h+1$)}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{(-1)^{h}(-q)^{-hN+\frac{h(h-1)}{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{N-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

This shows that (
9.17
) holds.

Combining (
9.16
) and (
9.17
), we have

\displaystyle D_{n,h}(a,b,c)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+c+h+1}(-q)^{\frac{c}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}-h^{2}-\frac{n}{2}-cn+hn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-ch-h(n-c+a-1)+\frac{h(h-1)}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{n-c-a}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{n-c+a-1-h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{n-h-c}(1-(-q)^{-l})\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}.

If
a=1
, then we have
b=n-c-1
, and

\displaystyle D_{n,h}(1,b,c)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+c+h+1}(-q)^{\frac{(n-c-h-1)(n-c+h)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle=\prod_{l=h+1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{l}).

If
a=0
, then we have
b=n-c
, and

\displaystyle D_{n,h}(0,b,c)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+c+h+1}(-q)^{\frac{(n-c-h)(n-c+h-1)}{2}}\dfrac{\prod_{l=1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{-l})}{(1-(-q)^{-(n-c-h)})\prod_{l=1}^{h}(1-(-q)^{-l})}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\prod_{l=h+1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{l})}{(1-(-q)^{b-h})}.

This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

10.
Fourier transform

In this section, we will prove certain theorems on the Fourier transform of the analytic side of Conjecture
7.6
. Recall that
{\mathbb{V}}
is the space of special homomorphisms associated with
{\mathcal{N}}_{n}^{[h]}
and it is split (resp. non-split) if
h
is odd (resp. even). For an integrable function
f
on
{\mathbb{V}}
, we denote by
\widehat{f}
its Fourier transform

\widehat{f}(x)\coloneqq\int_{{\mathbb{V}}}f(y)\psi(\mathrm{Tr}_{F/F_{0}}\langle x,y\rangle)dy,\quad x\in{\mathbb{V}}.

For example, for an
O_{F}
-lattice
L\in{\mathbb{V}}
of rank
n
, we have

\widehat{1}_{L}=\vol(L)1_{L^{\vee}}=q^{-{\mathrm{val}}(L)/2}1_{L^{\vee}}.

Definition 10.1
.

Let
L\subset{\mathbb{V}}
be an
O_{F}
-lattice of rank
n
and let
L^{\flat}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
be an
O_{F}
-lattice of rank
n-1
.

(1)

Assume that
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
be the fundamental invariants of
L
. Then we define

D_{n,h}(L)=D_{n,h}(\lambda).

(2)

For
x\in{\mathbb{V}}\backslash L_{F}^{\flat}
, we define

\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\flat}}^{n,h}(x)\coloneqq{\sum}_{L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime})1_{L^{\prime}}(x),

where
L^{\prime}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
are
O_{F}
-lattices of rank
n
.

(3)

For
x\in{\mathbb{V}}\backslash L_{F}^{\flat}
, and
L^{\prime\flat}\supset L^{\flat}
, we define

\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)\coloneqq{\sum}_{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee},L^{\prime}\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime})1_{L^{\prime}}(x),

where
L^{\prime}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
are
O_{F}
-lattices of rank
n
.

Then, we have that

\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\flat}}^{n,h}(x)={\sum}_{{L^{\flat}}\subset L^{\prime\flat}\subset(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}}\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x).

By definition, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)

\displaystyle={\sum}_{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee},L^{\prime}\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime})\widehat{1_{L^{\prime}}}(x)

\displaystyle={\sum}_{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee},L^{\prime}\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat},x\in L^{\prime\vee}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime})\vol(L^{\prime}).

Also, by
[
19
, Lemma 7.2.1, Lemma 7.2.2, (7.4.2.3)]
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{ccc}[((\langle x\rangle+L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat})\backslash(L^{\flat}_{F}/L^{\prime\flat})]&\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}&\{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee},L^{\prime}\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat},x\in L^{\prime\vee}\}\\
u&\longmapsto&L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u\rangle.\end{array}

Now, assume that
x\perp L^{\flat}
and
{\mathrm{val}}(\langle x,x\rangle)\leq-1
. Then, as in the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 7.4.2]
, we have that

(\langle x\rangle+L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}=(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}\obot\langle x\rangle^{\vee},

and

[((\langle x\rangle+L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat})\backslash(L^{\flat}_{F}/L^{\prime\flat})]\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}.

Therefore, we have that

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}&\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}&\{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee},L^{\prime}\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat},x\in L^{\prime\vee}\}\\
(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})&\longmapsto&L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle,\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{ll}\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)&={\sum}_{(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle).\end{array}

Now, we need to compute
D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)
. First, let us define the following notations.

Definition 10.2
.

For
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{0+}
, we let
L_{\lambda}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
be an
O_{F}
-lattice of rank
n-1
with hermitian matrix
A_{\lambda}
. Consider a basis
{\mathbb{B}}=\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n-1}\}
such that the hermitian matrix of
L_{\lambda}
with respect to
{\mathbb{B}}
is
A_{\lambda}
.

(1)

For
i\geq 0
, we define
L_{\lambda\geq i}
to be the sublattice of
L_{\lambda}
generated by
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{t_{\geq i}(\lambda)}\}
. Therefore, the hermitian matrix of
L_{\lambda\geq i}
is

\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\pi^{\lambda_{1}}&&&\\
&\pi^{\lambda_{2}}&&\\
&&\ddots&\\
&&&\pi^{\lambda_{t_{\geq i}(\lambda)}}\end{array}\right).

In particular,
L_{\lambda\geq 0}=L_{\lambda}
.

(2)

For
i\geq 0
, we define
L_{\lambda=i}
to be the sublattice of
L_{\lambda}
generated by
x_{j}
’s such that
{\mathrm{val}}((x_{j},x_{j}))=i
. Therefore, the hermitian matrix of
L_{\lambda=i}
is
\pi^{i}I_{t_{i}(\lambda)}
.

(3)

For
i\geq j\geq 0
, we define
L_{(\lambda\geq i)-j}
be an
O_{F}
-lattice of rank
t_{\geq i}(\lambda)
with hermitian matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\pi^{\lambda_{1}-j}&&&\\
&\pi^{\lambda_{2}-j}&&\\
&&\ddots&\\
&&&\pi^{\lambda_{t_{\geq i}(\lambda)}-j}\par\end{array}\right).

Note that
L_{(\lambda\geq i)-j}
is not necessarily a sublattice in
{\mathbb{V}}
.

(4)

For an
O_{F}
-lattice
L
, we define

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu(L)=|(L^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L|,\\
\mu^{+}(L)=|(\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 1}/L|,\\
\mu^{++}(L)=|(\pi^{2}L^{\vee})^{\geq 2}/L|.\\
\end{array}

Consider a basis
{\mathbb{B}}=\{x_{1},x_{2}
,
\dots,x_{a},

x_{a+1},
\dots
,
x_{a+b}
,
\dots,

x_{a+b+c}\}
of
L^{\prime\flat}
(
a+b+c=n-1
) such that the hermitian matrix of
L
with respect to
{\mathbb{B}}
is
A_{\lambda}
where
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{a},1^{b},0^{c})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{0+}
,
\lambda_{i}\geq 2
. To simplify notation, we abbreviate
L_{\lambda\geq 2}
,
L_{\lambda=1}
to
L_{2}^{\prime\flat}
,
L_{1}^{\prime\flat}
, respectively. In other words,
L_{2}^{\prime\flat}
is the
O_{F}
-lattice generated by
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{a}\}
, and
L^{\prime\flat}_{1}
is the
O_{F}
-lattice generated by
\{x_{a+1},\dots,x_{a+b}\}
.

Note that

(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}=(L^{\prime\flat}_{2}\obot L^{\prime\flat}_{1})^{\vee,\geq 0}/(L^{\prime\flat}_{2}\obot L^{\prime\flat}_{1}).

Then, we have the following propositions.

Proposition 10.3
.

Assume that
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp}))\geq 2
, then we have the followings.

(1)

(Case 1-1) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c).

(2)

(Case 1-2) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c).

(3)

(Case 1-3) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1).

(4)

(Case 2-1) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2).

(5)

(Case 2-2) If

u^{\flat}\in((\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0},

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2).

(6)

(Case 3-1) If
u^{\flat}\in((\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot(\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 1}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c).

(7)

(Case 3-2) If
u^{\flat}\in((\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot(\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-((\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot(\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 1}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1).

(8)

(Case 4-1) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2).

(9)

(Case 4-2) If

u^{\flat}\in((\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-(\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0},

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2).

(10)

(Case 5) If
u^{\flat}\in(((L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2).

Proof.

This is a linear algebra problem, so we skip the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proposition 10.4
.

Assume that
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp}))=1
, then we have the followings.

(1)

(Case 1-1) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c).

(2)

(Case 1-2-1) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}
, and
{\mathrm{val}}(\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp},u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=1

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c).

(3)

(Case 1-2-2) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}
, and
{\mathrm{val}}(\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp},u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\geq 2
, we have

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c).

(4)

(Case 1-3) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1).

(5)

(Case 2-1) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2).

(6)

(Case 2-2) If

u^{\flat}\in((\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0},

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2).

(7)

(Case 3-1) If
u^{\flat}\in((\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot(\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 1}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c).

(8)

(Case 3-2) If
u^{\flat}\in((\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot(\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-((\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot(\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 1}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1).

(9)

(Case 4-1) If
u^{\flat}\in(\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2).

(10)

(Case 4-2) If

u^{\flat}\in((\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-(\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0},

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2).

(11)

(Case 5) If
u^{\flat}\in(((L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})\obot(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}
,

D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2).

Proof.

This is a linear algebra problem, so we skip the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proposition 10.5
.

Assume that
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
and
L_{\lambda}
is an
O_{F}
-lattice with hermitian matrix
A_{\lambda}
.

(1)

If
\lambda\geq(1,\dots,1)
, we have

\mu^{+}(L_{\lambda})=\mu(L_{\lambda-1}).

(2)

If
\lambda\geq(2,\dots,2)
, we have

\mu^{++}(L_{\lambda})=\mu^{+}(L_{\lambda-1}).

Proof.

By definition, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu(L_{\lambda-1})=|(L_{\lambda-1}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{\lambda-1}|,\\
\mu^{+}(L_{\lambda})=|(\pi L_{\lambda}^{\vee})^{\geq 1}/L_{\lambda}|.\end{array}

Note that the fundamental invariants of
L_{\lambda-1}^{\vee}
(resp.
\pi L_{\lambda}^{\vee})
) are
(-\lambda_{1}+1,\dots,-\lambda_{n}+1)
(resp.
(-\lambda_{1}+2,\dots,-\lambda_{n}+2)
). Now, for
(L_{\lambda-1}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{\lambda-1}
, and the hermitian form
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
, we consider the same set with the hermitian form
\pi\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
. Then, this is isomorphic to
(\pi L_{\lambda}^{\vee})^{\geq 1}/L_{\lambda}
. This proves (1). The proof of (2) is similar.
∎

Proposition 10.6
.

For
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{0+}
and
L_{\lambda}=L_{\lambda\geq 2}\obot L_{\lambda=1}=:L_{2}\obot L_{1}
, we have the followings.

(1)

(Case 1-1) We have that

|(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|=\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}).

(2)

(Case 1-2) We have that

\begin{array}[]{l}|\{(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
=q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}).\end{array}

(3)

(Case 1-3+Case 3-2) We have that

\begin{array}[]{l}|\{(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
+|\{((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{\lambda\geq 2})^{\vee})\obot(\pi(L_{1})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot(\pi(L_{1})^{\vee}))^{\geq 1}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1}).\end{array}

(4)

(Case 2-1+Case 4-1) We have that

\begin{array}[]{l}|\{(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
+|\{(\pi(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\times(\mu(L_{\lambda=1})-1).\end{array}

(5)

(Case 2-2+Case 4-2) We have that

\begin{array}[]{l}|\{((\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
+|\{((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
-|\{(\pi(L_{2})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\{\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\mu(L_{\lambda=1})\}.\end{array}

(6)

(Case 3-1) We have that

\begin{array}[]{l}|\{((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot(\pi(L_{1})^{\vee}))^{\geq 1}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|=\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1})-q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3}).\end{array}

(7)

(Case 5) We have that

\begin{array}[]{l}|\{(((L_{2})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|=\mu(L_{\lambda\geq 2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})-q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1}).\end{array}

Here, we choose the following convention:
\forall i\geq j\geq 0
, if
L_{\lambda\geq i}
is empty, then
\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq i)-j})=1
.

Proof.

We only prove the cases: (Case 1-2) and (Case 2-2+Case 4-2). The other cases can be proved similarly.

In (Case 1-2), note that
\pi(L_{1})^{\vee}=L_{1}
. Therefore, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}|\{(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|=|\{(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}\}/L_{2}|.\end{array}

If
L_{\lambda\geq 3}
is empty, then we have that
\pi^{2}L_{2}^{\vee}=L_{2}=L_{\lambda=2}
. Therefore,

|\{(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\}/L_{2}|=|\{(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}\}/L_{2}|=1.

If
L_{\lambda\geq 3}
is not empty, then we have

\begin{array}[]{l}|\{(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\}/L_{2}|=|\{(\pi^{2}L_{\lambda\geq 3}^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\}/L_{\lambda\geq 3}|=q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}|\{(\pi(\pi^{-1}L_{\lambda\geq 3})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\}/\pi^{-1}L_{\lambda\geq 3}|\\
=q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu^{+}(\pi^{-1}L_{\lambda\geq 3})=q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu^{+}(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-2})=q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3}).\end{array}

Here, we used Proposition
10.5
. Also, by definition, we have that
|\{(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}\}/L_{2}|=\mu^{++}(L_{2})=\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}).
Therefore, we have that (Case 1-2) is
q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})
.

Next, consider the case (Case 2-2+Case 4-2).
First, note that

\begin{array}[]{l}|\{((\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
+|\{((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
-|\{(\pi(L_{2})^{\vee}-\pi^{2}(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|\\
=|\{((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}-(\pi(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1})^{\vee}-\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|.\end{array}

Now, note that

\displaystyle|\{((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot(L_{1}^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|

\displaystyle=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}|\{((\pi^{-1}L_{2})^{\vee}\obot(L_{1}^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}\}/\pi^{-1}L_{2}\obot L_{1}|

\displaystyle=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(\pi^{-1}L_{2}\obot L_{1})

\displaystyle=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1}).

Also, since
\pi L_{1}^{\vee}=L_{1}
, we have

|\{((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee})\obot(\pi L_{1}^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|=|\{((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}|=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}),

|\{(\pi(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot((L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\times\mu(L_{\lambda=1}),

and

|\{(\pi(L_{2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\obot(\pi(L_{1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}\obot L_{1}|=|\{((\pi(L_{2})^{\vee}))^{\geq 0}\}/L_{2}|=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}).

Combining these, we have that (Case 2-2+Case 4-2) is

q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})-q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\times\mu(L_{\lambda=1}).

This finishes the proof of the proposition in the case (Case 2-2+Case 4-2).
∎

Proposition 10.7
.

(cf.
[
20
, Lemma 8.2.3]
,
[
7
, Lemma 8.4]
) Assume that
L
(resp.
M
) is an
O_{F}
-lattice of rank
n
with hermitian matrix
A_{\lambda}
(resp.
A_{\eta}
) such that
\lambda,\eta\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
,
\lambda,\eta\geq(1,\dots,1)
. Assume further that
L\subset M\subset\pi^{-1}L
. Then, we have

|((L^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\backslash(M^{\vee})^{\geq 0})/L|=q^{2n-1}|((\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\backslash(\pi M^{\vee})^{\geq 1})/L|.

Proof.

Here, we follow the proof of
[
20
, Lemma 8.2.3]
.
Consider the map

\begin{array}[]{ccc}((L^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\backslash(M^{\vee})^{\geq 0})/L&\longrightarrow&((\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\backslash(\pi M^{\vee})^{\geq 1})/L\\
x&\longmapsto&\pi x.\end{array}

To prove the proposition, it suffices to show that the above map is surjective and every fiber has size
q^{2n-1}
. Choose
x\in(\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\backslash(\pi M^{\vee})^{\geq 1}
. Then the fiber of
x
is given by

\{\dfrac{1}{\pi}(x+y)\in(L^{\vee})^{\geq 0},y\in L/\pi L\}.

Since
x\in\pi L^{\vee}
, the condition
\dfrac{1}{\pi}(x+y)\in(L^{\vee})^{\geq 0}
is equivalent to

y\in L\cap\pi L^{\vee}/\pi L,\text{ and }(y+x,y+x)\equiv 0(\text{mod }\pi^{2}).

Choose a basis
e=\{e_{1},\dots,e_{n}\}
of
L
such that the hermitian matrix of
L
with respect to
e
is
A_{\lambda}
. Then, we have

\pi L^{\vee}=\oplus_{i}O_{F}(\pi^{-\lambda_{i}+1}e_{i}).

Write

\begin{array}[]{l}x={\sum}_{i}\mu_{i}\pi^{-\lambda_{i}+1}e_{i},\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{i}\in O_{F},\\
y={\sum}_{i}\nu_{i}e_{i},\hskip 11.74988pt\nu_{i}\in O_{F}.\end{array}

Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}(y,y)\equiv{\sum}_{\lambda_{i}=1}(\nu_{i}e_{i},\nu_{i}e_{i})\equiv{\sum}_{\lambda_{i}=1}\pi\nu_{i}\overline{\nu_{i}}(\text{mod }\pi^{2}),\\
(y,x)={\sum}_{i}(\nu_{i}e_{i},\mu_{i}\pi^{-\lambda_{i}+1}e_{i})={\sum}_{i}\nu_{i}\overline{\mu_{i}}\pi.\end{array}

Since
x\in(\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 1}
, the condition
(y+x,y+x)\equiv 0(\text{mod }\pi^{2})
is equivalent to

(10.1)

\dfrac{1}{\pi}(x,x)+{\sum}_{\lambda_{i}=1}(\nu_{i}\overline{\nu_{i}}+\nu_{i}\overline{\mu_{i}}+\overline{\nu_{i}}\mu_{i})+{\sum}_{\lambda_{i}>1}(\nu_{i}\overline{\mu_{i}}+\overline{\nu_{i}}\mu_{i})\equiv 0(\text{mod }\pi).

Since
x\notin\pi M^{\vee}
, there is at least one
i
such that
\mu_{i}\not\equiv 0(\text{mod }\pi)
, and
\lambda_{i}>1
. Let
\mu_{k}
be such a coefficient, i.e.,
\mu_{k}\not\equiv 0(\text{mod }\pi)
, and
\lambda_{k}>1
.

Now, let
\beta=\dfrac{1}{\pi}(x,x)+{\sum}_{\lambda_{i}=1}(\nu_{i}\overline{\nu_{i}}+\nu_{i}\overline{\mu_{i}}+\overline{\nu_{i}}\mu_{i}).
Then, (
10.1
) can be written as

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\beta+{\sum}_{\lambda_{i}>1}(\nu_{i}\overline{\mu_{i}}+\overline{\nu_{i}}\mu_{i})\equiv 0(\text{mod }\pi)\\
\Longleftrightarrow&\mathrm{Tr}(\mu_{k}\overline{\nu_{k}})\equiv-\beta-{\sum}_{\lambda_{i}>1,i\neq k}(\nu_{i}\overline{\mu_{i}}+\overline{\nu_{i}}\mu_{i})(\text{mod }\pi).\end{array}

Choose any
\nu_{i},i\neq k
, so there are
q^{2(n-1)}
-choices. Also,
\mathrm{Tr}:{\mathbb{F}}_{q^{2}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
is surjective and every fiber has size
q
. Therefore, for each
\{\nu_{i}\}_{i\neq k}
, there are
q
-solutions of
\nu_{k}
, and hence

|\{\dfrac{1}{\pi}(x+y)\in(L^{\vee})^{\geq 0},y\in L/\pi L\}|=q^{2n-1}.

This finishes the proof of the proposition.

∎

Proposition 10.8
.

(cf.
[
20
, Lemma 8.2.6]
,
[
7
, Lemma 8.6]
)
Assume that
L
is an
O_{F}
-lattice of rank
n
and
e=\{e_{1},\dots,e_{n}\}
is a basis of
L
. Also, assume that the hermitian matrix of
L
with respect to
e
is
A_{\lambda}
where
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
,
\lambda\geq(1,\dots,1)
. We choose an
O_{F}
-lattice
M
as follows.

(1)

If
\lambda_{1}\geq 3
, we choose an
O_{F}
-lattice
M\supset L
such that
M=O_{F}(\dfrac{1}{\pi}e_{1}){\oplus}\oplus_{i\neq 1}O_{F}(e_{i})
with fundamental invariants
(\lambda_{1}-2,\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{n})
.

(2)

If
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=2
(so,
\lambda_{i}\leq 2
for all
i
), we choose an
O_{F}
-lattice
M\supset L
such that the fundamental invariants of
M
are
(\lambda_{1}-1,\lambda_{2}-1,\lambda_{3},\dots,\lambda_{n})
.

Then, we have that

|((\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\backslash(\pi M^{\vee})^{\geq 0})/L|=q|((\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\backslash(\pi M^{\vee})^{\geq 1})/L|.

Proof.

Here, we follow the proof of
[
20
, Lemma 8.2.6]
.

(1)

In this case, we have that

\pi L^{\vee}={\oplus}_{i}O_{F}(\pi^{-\lambda_{i}+1}e_{i}),

and

\pi M^{\vee}=O_{F}(\pi^{-\lambda_{1}+2}e_{1})\oplus(\bigoplus_{i\neq 1}O_{F}(\pi^{-\lambda_{i}+1}e_{i})).

Fix an element
x_{0}={\sum}_{i\neq 1}\mu_{i}\pi^{-\lambda_{i}+1}e_{i},\mu_{i}\in O_{F}
. Consider the sets

\begin{array}[]{l}S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}\coloneqq\{x\in(\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\backslash(\pi M^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\mid x=x_{0}+\mu_{1}\pi^{-\lambda_{1}+1}e_{1},\mu_{1}\in O_{F}\}/L\\
S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}\coloneqq\{x\in(\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\backslash(\pi M^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\mid x=x_{0}+\mu_{1}\pi^{-\lambda_{1}+1}e_{1},\mu_{1}\in O_{F}\}/L.\end{array}

Then, it suffices to show that
|S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}|=q|S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}|.

Note that
x\notin\pi M^{\vee}
if and only if
\mu_{1}\in O_{F}^{\times}
. Also, we have

(x,x)=(x_{0},x_{0})+\mu_{1}\overline{\mu_{1}}\pi^{-\lambda_{1}+2}.

Therefore, we have that

\begin{array}[]{lll}x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}&\text{ if and only if }&\pi^{\lambda_{1}-2}(x_{0},x_{0})+\mu_{1}\overline{\mu_{1}}\equiv 0(\text{mod }\pi^{\lambda_{1}-2}),\mu_{1}\in O_{F}^{\times},\\
x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}&\text{ if and only if }&\pi^{\lambda_{1}-2}(x_{0},x_{0})+\mu_{1}\overline{\mu_{1}}\equiv 0(\text{mod }\pi^{\lambda_{1}-1}),\mu_{1}\in O_{F}^{\times}.\end{array}

Let us write

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu_{1}=b_{0}+b_{1}\pi+b_{2}\pi^{2}+\dots,\\
-\pi^{\lambda_{1}-2}(x_{0},x_{0})=c_{0}+c_{1}\pi+c_{2}\pi^{2}+\dots.\end{array}

Then
x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}
if and only if
b_{0}\in O_{F}^{\times}
and

(10.2)

\begin{array}[]{l}c_{0}=b_{0}\overline{b_{0}},\\
c_{1}=b_{0}\overline{b_{1}}+b_{1}\overline{b_{0}},\\
\vdots\\
c_{\lambda_{1}-3}=b_{0}\overline{b_{\lambda_{1}-3}}+\dots+b_{\lambda_{1}-3}\overline{b_{0}}.\end{array}

Also,
x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}
if and only if
x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}
and

(10.3)

\begin{array}[]{ll}&c_{\lambda_{1}-2}=b_{0}\overline{b_{\lambda_{1}-2}}+\dots+b_{\lambda_{1}-2}\overline{b_{0}}\\
\Longleftrightarrow&\mathrm{Tr}(b_{0}\overline{b_{\lambda_{1}-2}})=c_{\lambda_{1}-2}-b_{1}\overline{b_{\lambda_{1}-3}}-\dots-b_{\lambda_{1}-3}\overline{b_{1}}.\end{array}

Now, for each
\{b_{0},b_{1},\dots,b_{\lambda_{1}-3}\}
satisfying (
10.2
), there are
q^{2}
-choices of
b_{\lambda_{1}-2}
so that
x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}
. Also, there are
q
-choices of
b_{\lambda_{1}-2}
so that (
10.3
) is true, and hence
x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}
. This shows that
|S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}|=q|S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}|.

(2)

In this case, we may choose a basis
f=\{f_{1},f_{2},\dots,f_{n}\}
of
\pi M^{\vee}
such that the hermitian matrix of
\pi M^{\vee}
with respect to
f
is

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc }\pi^{1}&&&&\\
&\pi^{1}&&&\\
&&\pi^{-\lambda_{3}+2}&&\\
&&&\ddots&\\
&&&&\pi^{-\lambda_{n}+2}\end{array}\right),

and
\pi L^{\vee}=O_{F}(\pi^{-1}(\varepsilon f_{1}+f_{2}))\oplus O_{F}(\pi^{-1}(f_{1}-\overline{\varepsilon}f_{2}))\oplus\bigoplus_{i\neq 1,2}O_{F}(f_{i}),
for some
\varepsilon
such that
1+\varepsilon\overline{\varepsilon}=\pi
. Then,
\{\pi^{-1}(\varepsilon f_{1}+f_{2}),f_{2},f_{3},\dots,f_{n}\}
forms a basis for
\pi L^{\vee}
.

We fix
x_{0}={\sum}_{i\neq 1}\mu_{i}f_{i},\mu_{i}\in O_{F}
, and let
x_{0}=x_{1}+\mu_{2}f_{2}
. Then, consider the sets

\begin{array}[]{l}S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}\coloneqq\{x\in(\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\backslash(\pi M^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\mid x=x_{0}+\mu_{1}\pi^{-1}(\varepsilon f_{1}+f_{2}),\mu_{1}\in O_{F}\}/L,\\
S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}\coloneqq\{x\in(\pi L^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\backslash(\pi M^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\mid x=x_{0}+\mu_{1}\pi^{-1}(\varepsilon f_{1}+f_{2}),\mu_{1}\in O_{F}\}/L.\end{array}

Note that
x\notin\pi M^{\vee}
if and only if
\mu_{1}\in O_{F}^{\times}
. Also, we have that

\displaystyle(x,x)

\displaystyle=(x_{1}+\mu_{2}f_{2}+\mu_{1}\pi^{-1}(\varepsilon f_{1}+f_{2}),x_{1}+\mu_{2}f_{2}+\mu_{1}\pi^{-1}(\varepsilon f_{1}+f_{2}))

\displaystyle=(x_{1},x_{1})+\mu_{2}\overline{\mu_{2}}\pi+\mu_{2}\overline{\mu_{1}}+\overline{\mu_{2}}\mu_{1}+\mu_{1}\overline{\mu_{1}}.

Therefore, we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}&\text{ if and only if }&(x_{1},x_{1})+\mu_{2}\overline{\mu_{2}}\pi+\mu_{2}\overline{\mu_{1}}+\overline{\mu_{2}}\mu_{1}+\mu_{1}\overline{\mu_{1}}\in O_{F},\text{and }\mu_{1}\in O_{F}^{\times},\\
x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}&\text{ if and only if }&(x_{1},x_{1})+\mu_{2}\overline{\mu_{2}}\pi+\mu_{2}\overline{\mu_{1}}+\overline{\mu_{2}}\mu_{1}+\mu_{1}\overline{\mu_{1}}\equiv 0(\text{mod }\pi),\text{and }\mu_{1}\in O_{F}^{\times}.\end{array}

Write

\displaystyle-(x_{1},x_{1})

\displaystyle=d_{0}+d_{1}\pi+d_{2}\pi^{2}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mu_{1}

\displaystyle=b_{0}+b_{1}\pi+b_{2}\pi^{2}+\cdots,

\displaystyle\mu_{2}

\displaystyle=c_{0}+c_{1}\pi+c_{2}\pi^{2}+\cdots.

Then,
x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}
if and only if
b_{0}\in O_{F}^{\times}
. Therefore, there are
q^{2}
-choices of
c_{0}
.

Also,
x\in S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}
if and only if
b_{0}\in O_{F}^{\times}
and

\begin{array}[]{ll}&d_{0}=c_{0}\overline{b_{0}}+\overline{c_{0}}b_{0}+b_{0}\overline{b_{0}}\\
\Longleftrightarrow&\mathrm{Tr}(c_{0}\overline{b_{0}})=d_{0}-b_{0}\overline{b_{0}}.\end{array}

Therefore, for each
b_{0}\in O_{F}^{\times}
, there are
q
-choices of
c_{0}
. This shows that
|S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 0}|=q|S_{x_{0}}^{\geq 1}|.
This finishes the proof of the proposition.

∎

Proposition 10.9
.

Assume that
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
.

(1)

If
\lambda_{1}\geq 3
, we define

\eta=(\lambda_{1}-2,\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{n})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+},

(if necessary, we change the order of
\eta_{i}
’s so that
\eta\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+})
.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\mu(L_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\mu(L_{\lambda\geq 1})

\displaystyle=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta\geq 1})+q^{2t_{\geq 1}(\lambda)+2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-2}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{2t_{\geq 1}(\lambda)+2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2}).

(2)

If
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=2
, we define

\eta=(\lambda_{1}-1,\lambda_{2}-1,\dots,\lambda_{n})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+},

(if necessary, we change the order of
\eta_{i}
’s so that
\eta\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+})
.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\mu(L_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\mu(L_{\lambda\geq 1})

\displaystyle=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta\geq 1})+q^{2t_{\geq 1}(\lambda)+2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-2}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{2t_{\geq 1}(\eta)+2t_{\geq 2}(\eta)}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2}).

Here, we choose the following convention: If
L_{\lambda\geq 2}
is empty, we assume
\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=1
.

Proof.

By Proposition
10.7
and Proposition
10.8
, we have

|((L_{\lambda}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\backslash(L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 0})/L_{\lambda}|=q^{2t_{\geq 1}(\lambda)-1}|((\pi L_{\lambda}^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\backslash(\pi L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 1})/L_{\lambda}|,

and

|((\pi L_{\lambda}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\backslash(\pi L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 0})/L_{\lambda}|=q|((\pi L_{\lambda}^{\vee})^{\geq 1}\backslash(\pi L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 1})/L_{\lambda}|.

Therefore, we have

(10.4)

|((L_{\lambda}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\backslash(L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 0})/L_{\lambda}|=q^{2t_{\geq 1}(\lambda)-2}|((\pi L_{\lambda}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}\backslash(\pi L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 0})/L_{\lambda}|.

Note that

\begin{array}[]{l}|(L_{\lambda}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{\lambda}|=\mu(L_{\lambda}),\\
|(L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{\lambda}|=q^{2}|(L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{\eta}|=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta}).\end{array}

Also, note that
(\pi L^{\vee}_{\lambda})^{\geq 0}/L_{\lambda}=(\pi(L_{\lambda\geq 2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{\lambda\geq 2}
, and it is trivial if
L_{\lambda\geq 2}
is empty. Since the fundamental invariants of
L_{\lambda\geq 2}
are at least
2
, any element
x
of
\pi^{-1}L_{\lambda\geq 2}
has valuation at least
0
. Therefore, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}|(\pi L^{\vee}_{\lambda})^{\geq 0}/L_{\lambda}|=|(\pi(L_{\lambda\geq 2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{\lambda\geq 2}|=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}|((\pi^{-1}L_{\lambda\geq 2})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/(\pi^{-1}L_{\lambda\geq 2})|\\
\\
=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(\pi^{-1}L_{\lambda\geq 2})=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}).\end{array}

Similarly, we have

|(\pi L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{\lambda}|=q^{2}|(\pi L_{\eta}^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{\eta}|=q^{2+2t_{\geq 2}(\eta)}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2}).

Now, by (
10.4
) and the fact that
t_{\geq 1}(\lambda)=t_{\geq 1}(\eta)
, we have the proof of the proposition.
∎

Lemma 10.10
.

For
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
,
\lambda\geq(1,1,\dots,1)
, we have that

\mu(L_{\lambda})=q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\lambda-1})-(-q)^{|\lambda|-1}(q-1).

Proof.

First, consider the case
\lambda=(1,1,\dots,1)
. Then, it is easy to see that (see
[
28
, Example 5.6]
, for example)

\displaystyle\mu(L_{1,\dots,1})

\displaystyle=|\{(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\in{\mathbb{F}}_{q}^{n}\mid x_{1}^{q+1}+\dots+x_{n}^{q+1}=0\}|

\displaystyle=q^{2n-1}+(-q)^{n}+(-q)^{n-1}=q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{0,\dots,0})-(-q)^{n-1}(q-1).

Similarly, if
\lambda=(2,\overset{n-1}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
, we have that

\displaystyle\mu(L_{\lambda})=|((L_{2,1,\dots,1})^{\vee})^{\geq 0}/L_{2,1,\dots,1}|=q^{2}\mu(L_{\underset{n-1}{\underbrace{1,\dots,1}}})

\displaystyle=q^{2n-1}+(-q)^{n+1}+(-q)^{n}

\displaystyle=q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{1})-(-q)^{n}(q-1).

Now, we will prove the lemma by induction on
|\lambda|
.

Assume that
\lambda=(\overset{a}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
,
a\geq 2
, and let
\eta=(\overset{a-2}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b+2}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
. Then, by Proposition
10.9
(2), we have

\mu(L_{\lambda})=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta})+q^{2n+2a-2}-q^{2n+2a-4}.

Also, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu(L_{\lambda-1})=\mu(L_{\underset{a}{\underbrace{1,\dots,1}}})=q^{2a-1}+(-q)^{a}+(-q)^{a-1},\\
q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta-1})=q^{2}\mu(L_{\underset{a-2}{\underbrace{1,\dots,1}}})=q^{2a-3}+(-q)^{a}+(-q)^{a-1},\\
\mu(L_{\lambda-1})=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta-1})+q^{2a-1}-q^{2a-3}.\end{array}

Therefore, we have that

\mu(L_{\lambda})-q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\lambda-1})=q^{2}\{\mu(L_{\eta})-q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\eta-1})\}.

Now, by our induction hypothesis, we have that

q^{2}\{\mu(L_{\eta})-q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\eta-1})\}=-(-q)^{|\eta|-1+2}(q-1)=-(-q)^{|\lambda|-1}(q-1).

This finishes the proof of the lemma when
\lambda=(\overset{a}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
,
a\geq 2
.

Now, assume that
\lambda=(\overset{n-a-b}{\overbrace{3,\dots,3}},\overset{a}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
, and let
\eta=(\overset{n-a-b-1}{\overbrace{3,\dots,3}},\overset{a}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b+1}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
. Then, by Proposition
10.9
(1), we have

\mu(L_{\lambda})=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta})+q^{4n-2b-2}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{4n-2b-2}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2}).

Also, by the previous case, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu(L_{\lambda-1})=q^{2n-2b-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-(-q)^{|\lambda|-n-1}(q-1),\\
\mu(L_{\eta-1})=q^{2n-2b-3}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2})-(-q)^{|\eta|-n-1}(q-1),\\
\mu(L_{\lambda-1})=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta-1})+q^{2n-2b-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{2n-2b-1}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2}).\end{array}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mu(L_{\lambda})-q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\lambda-1})

\displaystyle=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta})+q^{4n-2b-2}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{4n-2b-2}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2})

\displaystyle-q^{2n-1}\{q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta-1})+q^{2n-2b-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{2n-2b-1}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2})

\displaystyle=q^{2}\{\mu(L_{\eta})-q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\eta-1})\}

\displaystyle=-(-q)^{|\eta|+2-1}(q-1)\text{ }(\text{by our inductive hypothesis})

\displaystyle=-(-q)^{|\lambda|-1}(q-1).

This finishes the proof of the lemma when
\lambda=(\overset{n-a-b}{\overbrace{3,\dots,3}},\overset{a}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
.

Now, assume that
\lambda=(\lambda_{1}=4,\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{n-a-b},\overset{a}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
,
\lambda_{n-a-b}\geq 3
. Let

\eta=(\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{n-a-b},\overset{a+1}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}}).

Then, by Proposition
10.9
(1), we have

\mu(L_{\lambda})=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta})+q^{4n-2b-2}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{4n-2b}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2}).

Similarly, we have
t_{\geq 2}(\lambda-1)=n-a-b
,
t_{\geq 2}(\eta-1)=n-a-b-1
,
t_{\geq 1}(\lambda-1)=t_{\geq 1}(\eta-1)=n-b
, and

\mu(L_{\lambda-1})=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta-1})+q^{4n-2a-4b-2}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-q^{4n-2a-4b-2}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 3)-3}).

Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{cl}\mu(L_{\lambda})-q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\lambda-1})&=q^{2}\{\mu(L_{\eta})-q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\eta-1})\}\\
&+q^{4n-2b-2}\{\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{2n-2a-2b-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})\}\\
&-q^{4n-2b}\{\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2})-q^{2n-2a-2b-3}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 3)-3})\}\\
(\text{by our inductive hypothesis})&=-(-q)^{|\eta|+1}(q-1)+q^{4n-2b-2}(-(-q)^{|\lambda|-b-2(n-b)-1}(q-1))\\
&-(-q)^{4n-2b}(-(-q)^{|\eta|-b-2(n-b)-1}(q-1))\\
&=-(-q)^{|\lambda|-1}(q-1).\end{array}

This finishes the proof of the lemma when
\lambda=(\lambda_{1}=4,\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{n-a-b},\overset{a}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
,
\lambda_{n-a-b}\geq 3
.

Finally, assume that
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n-a-b},\overset{a}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}},\overset{b}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}})
,
\lambda_{1}\geq 5
. In this case, let
\eta=(\lambda_{1}-2,\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{n})
(if necessary, we change the order of
\eta_{i}
’s so that
\eta\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+})
. Then, by Proposition
10.9
(1), we have

\mu(L_{\lambda})=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta})+q^{4n-2b-2}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{4n-2b}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2}),

and

\mu(L_{\lambda-1})=q^{2}\mu(L_{\eta-1})+q^{4n-2a-4b-2}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-q^{4n-2a-4b}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 3)-3}).

Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{rl}\mu(L_{\lambda})-q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\lambda-1})&=q^{2}\{\mu(L_{\eta})-q^{2n-1}\mu(L_{\eta-1})\}\\
&+q^{4n-2b-2}\{\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{2n-2a-2b-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})\}\\
&-q^{4n-2b}\{\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 2)-2})-q^{2n-2a-2b-1}\mu(L_{(\eta\geq 3)-3})\}\\
(\text{by our inductive hypothesis})&=-(-q)^{|\eta|+1}(q-1)+q^{4n-2b-2}(-(-q)^{|\lambda|-b-2(n-b)-1}(q-1))\\
&-(-q)^{4n-2b}(-(-q)^{|\eta|-b-2(n-b)-1}(q-1))\\
&=-(-q)^{|\lambda|-1}(q-1).\end{array}

This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 10.11
.

Assume that
a\geq 1
, and
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Let
\kappa_{a,i}
be the constants such that

(1-X)(1-(-q)X)\dots(1-(-q)^{a-2}X)=\sum_{i=0}^{a-1}\kappa_{a,i}X^{i}.

Then, we have

D_{n,h}(a,b,c)=\sum_{i=0}^{a-1}\kappa_{a,i}(-q)^{i(a+b-h+1)}D_{n-i,h-i}(1,b+a-1-i,c).

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
9.4
.
∎

Lemma 10.12
.

Assume that
a\geq 1
. Then, we have

(10.5)

\begin{array}[]{ccl}&&D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)\\
+((-q)^{a}-1)&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)\\
+(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)\\
-(-q)^{2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})&\times&D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)\\
-(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{a})&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)\end{array}=0.

Proof.

To simplify notation, we assume that for
k\geq 1
,

\begin{array}[]{l}X_{0}^{k}\coloneqq D_{n-k+1,h-k+1}(1,b+a-k+1,c),\\
X_{1}^{k}\coloneqq D_{n-k+1,h-k+1}(1,b+a-k,c+1),\\
X_{2}^{k}\coloneqq D_{n-k+1,h-k+1}(1,b+a-k-1,c+2).\end{array}

Then, by Lemma
10.11
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)=X_{0}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+2}X_{0})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h+1}X_{0}),\\
D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)=X_{0}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+2}X_{0})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h}X_{0}),\\
D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)=X_{1}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1}X_{1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1}X_{1}),\\
D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)=X_{2}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h}X_{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1}X_{2}),\\
D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)=X_{2}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h}X_{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-2}X_{2}).\\
\end{array}

Therefore, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)+((-q)^{a}-1)D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)\\
=(-q)^{a}X_{0}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1}X_{0})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h}X_{0})\\
=(-q)^{a}X_{0}(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h}X_{0})(\sum_{t=0}^{a-1}\kappa_{a,t}(-q)^{t(a+b-h+1)}X_{0}^{t}).\end{array}

This implies that for
1\leq t\leq a+1
,
X_{0}^{t}
-terms are

(10.6)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(-q)^{a}\{\kappa_{a,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-1)}-(-q)^{2a+b-h}\kappa_{a,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-2)}\}X_{0}^{t}&\text{ if }t\neq a+1,\\
-(-q)^{3a+b-h}\kappa_{a,a-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(a-1)}X_{0}^{a+1}&\text{ if }t=a+1.\end{array}\right.

Similarly, for
1\leq t\leq a
,
X_{1}^{t}
-terms in
(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)
are

(10.7)

(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)\kappa_{a,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-1)}X_{1}^{t}.

Finally, for
1\leq t\leq a+1
,
X_{2}^{t}
-terms in

\begin{array}[]{l}-(-q)^{2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)\\
-(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{a})D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)\end{array}

are

(10.8)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{-\kappa_{a,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-1)+2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{a+b-1})&\\
+\kappa_{a,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-2)+4a+b-h-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})\}X_{2}^{t}&\text{ if }t\neq a+1,\\
\kappa_{a,a-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(a-1)+4a+b-h-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})X_{2}^{a+1}&\text{ if }t=a+1.\end{array}\right.

Note that if
a+b<h-1
, then
X_{0}^{t},X_{1}^{t},
and
X_{2}^{t}
are all zero. Therefore, all of the degree
t
-terms of the left-hand side of (
10.5
) are
0
, and hence (
10.5
) holds in this case.

Now, assume that
a+b\geq h+2
. Then, by Theorem
9.8
, we have that

(10.9)

\begin{array}[]{l}X_{0}^{t}=\prod_{l=h-t+2}^{a+b-t+1}(1-(-q)^{l}),\\
X_{1}^{t}=\prod_{l=h-t+2}^{a+b-t}(1-(-q)^{l}),\\
X_{2}^{t}=\prod_{l=h-t+2}^{a+b-t-1}(1-(-q)^{l}).\end{array}

Combining (
10.6
), (
10.7
), (
10.8
), and (
10.9
), we can see that the degree
t
-terms of the left hand side of (
10.5
) can be written as follows: if
1\leq t\leq a
, we have

(10.10)

\displaystyle\prod_{l=h-t+2}^{a+b-t-1}(1-(-q)^{l})\Big\{

\displaystyle(-q)^{a}\{\kappa_{a,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-1)}-(-q)^{2a+b-h}\kappa_{a,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-2)}\}

\displaystyle\times(1-(-q)^{a+b-t+1})(1-(-q)^{a+b-t})

\displaystyle+(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)\kappa_{a,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-1)}(1-(-q)^{a+b-t})

\displaystyle+\{-\kappa_{a,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-1)+2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{a+b-1})

\displaystyle+\kappa_{a,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-2)+4a+b-h-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})\}\Big\}

\displaystyle=((-q)^{a+2b}-(-q)^{t})(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-2)+3a+b-h-t-2}

\displaystyle\{-((-q)^{t+1}-(-q)^{2})\kappa_{a,t-1}+((-q)^{t}-(-q)^{a+1})\kappa_{a,t-2}\}.

Now, we claim that

(10.11)

\{-((-q)^{t+1}-(-q)^{2})\kappa_{a,t-1}+((-q)^{t}-(-q)^{a+1})\kappa_{a,t-2}\}=0.

Recall from Lemma
10.11
that

\begin{array}[]{l}(1-X)(1-(-q)X)\dots(1-(-q)^{a-2}X)=\sum_{t=0}^{a-1}\kappa_{a,t}X^{t},\\
(1-(-q)X)(1-(-q)^{2}X)\dots(1-(-q)^{a-1}X)=\sum_{t=0}^{a-1}\kappa_{a,t}(-q)^{t}X^{t}.\end{array}

Therefore,

(1-(-q)^{a-1}X)\sum_{t=0}^{a-1}\kappa_{a,t}X^{t}=(1-X)\sum_{t=0}^{a-1}\kappa_{a,t}(-q)^{t}X^{t}.

By comparing degree
t-1
terms, we have that

(10.12)

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\kappa_{a,t-1}-(-q)^{a-1}\kappa_{a,t-2}=\kappa_{a,t-1}(-q)^{t-1}-\kappa_{a,t-2}(-q)^{t-2}\\
\Longleftrightarrow&-((-q)^{t-1}-1)\kappa_{a,t-1}+((-q)^{t-2}-(-q)^{a-1})\kappa_{a,t-2}=0.\end{array}

Therefore, we have that (
10.11
) holds, and hence (
10.10
) is zero. This shows that the degree
t
-term of (
10.5
) is zero for
1\leq t\leq a
.

For
t=a+1
, we have that the degree
a+1
-terms of the left hand side of (
10.5
) are

\begin{array}[]{ll}\prod_{l=h-a+1}^{b-2}(1-(-q)^{l})&\Big\{-(-q)^{3a+b-h}\kappa_{a,a-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(a-1)}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})\\
&+\kappa_{a,a-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(a-1)+4a+b-h-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})\Big\}\\
=0.&\end{array}

This shows that (
10.5
) holds when
a+b\geq h+2
.

Now, the remaining cases are
a+b=h-1
,
h
, and
h+1
.

When
a+b=h-1
, we have that
X_{0}^{t}=1
,
X_{1}^{t}=X_{2}^{t}=0
by Theorem
9.8
. Therefore, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)+((-q)^{a}-1)D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)\\
=(-q)^{a}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h})\\
=0\quad(\text{since }a+b-h+1=0).\end{array}

Also, we have
D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)=0
,

D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)=0
, and

D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)=0
. This shows that (
10.5
) holds when
a+b=h-1
.

When
a+b=h
, we have that
X_{0}^{t}=X_{1}^{t}=1
and
X_{2}^{t}=0
by Theorem
9.8
. Therefore, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)+((-q)^{a}-1)D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)=(-q)^{a}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h});\\
\\
(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)=(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1});\\
\\
-(-q)^{2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)=0;\\
\\
-(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{a})D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)=0.\\
\end{array}

Therefore, the left-hand side of (
10.5
) is

\begin{array}[]{l}(-q)^{a}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h})+(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1})\\
=(-q)^{a}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1})\{(-q)^{a}-(-q)^{2a+b-h}\}\\
=0\quad(\text{since }2a+b-h=a).\end{array}

Now, assume that
a+b=h+1
. In this case, by Theorem
9.8
, we have that
X_{0}^{t}=(1-(-q)^{h-t+2})
,
X_{1}^{t}=1
, and
X_{2}^{t}=1
. Therefore, we have

(10.13)

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)+((-q)^{a}-1)D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)\\
=(-q)^{a}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h})\\
-(-q)^{a}(-q)^{h+1}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1}(-q)^{-1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h}(-q)^{-1})\\
=(-q)^{a}(1-(-q)^{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{a})(1-(-q)^{a+1}-(-q)^{h+1}+(-q)^{h+2}).\end{array}

Also, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)=(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)(1-(-q)^{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{a}),\end{array}

and

\begin{array}[]{l}-(-q)^{2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)\\
-(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{a})D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)\\
=-(-q)^{2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})(1-(-q)^{a+b-h})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1})\\
-(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{a})(1-(-q)^{a+b-h})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-2})\\
=-(-q)^{2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{1})\dots(1-(-q)^{a}).\end{array}

Therefore, the left-hand side of (
10.5
) is

\begin{array}[]{l}(-q)^{a}(1-(-q)^{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{a})(1-(-q)^{a+1}-(-q)^{h+1}+(-q)^{h+2})\\
+(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)(1-(-q)^{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{a})-(-q)^{2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{1})\dots(1-(-q)^{a})\\
=0.\end{array}

Therefore, (
10.5
) holds when
a+b=h+1
.

This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 10.13
.

Assume that
a\geq 2
. Then, we have

(10.14)

\begin{array}[]{ccl}&&D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)\\
+((-q)^{a-1}-1)&\times&D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c)\\
-(-q)^{a-1}&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)\\
+(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)\\
+(-q)^{2a+2b-1}(1-(-q)^{a-1})&\times&D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2)\end{array}=0

Proof.

To simplify notation, we follow the notation in the proof of Lemma
10.12
. Then, by Lemma
10.11
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)=X_{0}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+2}X_{0})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h}X_{0}),\\
D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c)=X_{0}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+2}X_{0})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1}X_{0}),\\
D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)=X_{1}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1}X_{1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1}X_{1}),\\
D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)=X_{2}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h}X_{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-2}X_{2}),\\
D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2)=X_{2}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h}X_{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-3}X_{2}).\end{array}

This implies that for
1\leq t\leq a
,
X_{0}^{t}
-terms of
D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)+((-q)^{a-1}-1)D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c)
are

(10.15)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(-q)^{a-1}\{\kappa_{a-1,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-1)}-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1}\kappa_{a-1,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-2)}\}X_{0}^{t},&\text{ if }t\neq a,\\
-(-q)^{3a+b-h-2}\kappa_{a-1,a-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(a-2)}X_{0}^{a}&\text{ if }t=a.\end{array}\right.

Similarly, for
1\leq t\leq a
,
X_{1}^{t}
-terms in
-(-q)^{a-1}D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)
are

(10.16)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-(-q)^{a-1}\{\kappa_{a-1,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-1)}-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1}\kappa_{a-1,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-2)}\}X_{1}^{t},&\text{ if }t\neq a,\\
(-q)^{3a+b-h-2}\kappa_{a-1,a-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(a-2)}X_{1}^{a}&\text{ if }t=a.\end{array}\right.

Finally, for
1\leq t\leq a
,
X_{2}^{t}
-terms in
(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)+(-q)^{2a+2b-1}(1-(-q)^{a-1})D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2)
are

(10.17)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\{\kappa_{a-1,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-1)+2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{a+b-1})&\\
-\kappa_{a-1,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-2)+4a+2b-h-3}(1-(-q)^{b}\}X_{2}^{t}&\text{ if }t\neq a,\\
-\kappa_{a-1,a-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(a-2)+4a+2b-h-3}(1-(-q)^{b})X_{2}^{a}&\text{ if }t=a.\end{array}\right.

First, assume that
a+b\geq h+2
. Then, by (
10.9
), (
10.15
), (
10.16
), and (
10.17
), we have that the degree
t
-terms of the left hand side of (
10.14
) are as follows: for
1\leq t\leq a-1
, we have

(10.18)

\begin{array}[]{l}\prod_{l=h-t+2}^{a+b-t-1}(1-(-q)^{l})\\
\times\Big\{(-q)^{a-1}\{\kappa_{a-1,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-1)}-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1}\kappa_{a-1,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-2)}\}\\
\times(1-(-q)^{a+b-t+1})(1-(-q)^{a+b-t})\\
-(-q)^{a-1}\{\kappa_{a-1,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-1)}-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1}\kappa_{a-1,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(t-2)}\}\\
\times(1-(-q)^{a+b-t})\\
+\{\kappa_{a-1,t-1}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-1)+2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{a+b-1})\\
-\kappa_{a-1,t-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-2)+4a+2b-h-3}(1-(-q)^{b})\}\Big\}\\
=(-q)^{(a+b-h)(t-1)+3a+2b-t-3}\\
\{-((-q)^{t+1}-(-q)^{2})\kappa_{a-1,t-1}+((-q)^{t}-(-q)^{a})\kappa_{a-1,t-2}\}.\end{array}

Now, by (
10.11
), we have that (
10.18
) is zero.

For
t=a
, we have that the degree
a
-terms of the left hand side of (
10.14
) are

\begin{array}[]{ll}\prod_{l=h-a+2}^{b-1}(1-(-q)^{l})&\Big\{-(-q)^{3a+b-h-2}\kappa_{a-1,a-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(a-2)}(1-(-q)^{b+1})(1-(-q)^{b})\\
&+(-q)^{3a+b-h-2}\kappa_{a-1,a-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h+1)(a-2)}(1-(-q)^{b})\\
&-\kappa_{a-1,a-2}(-q)^{(a+b-h)(a-2)+4a+2b-h-3}(1-(-q)^{b})\Big\}\\
=0.&\end{array}

This shows that (
10.14
) holds when
a+b\geq h+2
.

Now, the remaining cases are
a+b<h-1
,
a+b=h-1
,
h
, and
h+1
.

If
a+b<h-1
, then
X_{0}^{t},X_{1}^{t},
and
X_{2}^{t}
are all zero, and hence (
10.14
) holds.

If
a+b=h-1
, we have that
X_{0}^{t}=1
, and
X_{1}^{t}=X_{2}^{t}=0
by Theorem
9.8
. Therefore, we have that (
10.14
) is

\begin{array}[]{l}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+2})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h})+((-q)^{a-1}-1)(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+2})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1})\\
=0\quad(\text{ since }2a+b-h=a-1).\end{array}

This shows that (
10.14
) holds when
a+b=h-1
.

If
a+b=h
, then we have
X_{0}^{t}=X_{1}^{t}=1
and
X_{2}^{t}=0
by Theorem
9.8
. Therefore, we have that (
10.14
) is

\begin{array}[]{l}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+2})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h})+((-q)^{a-1}-1)(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+2})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1})\\
-(-q)^{a-1}(1-(-q)^{a+b-h+1})\dots(1-(-q)^{2a+b-h-1})\\
=0.\end{array}

This shows that (
10.14
) holds when
a+b=h
.

When
a+b=h+1
, we have
X_{0}^{t}=(1-(-q)^{h-t+2})
, and
X_{1}^{t}=X_{2}^{t}=1
. Therefore, by (
10.13
), we have that (
10.14
) is

\begin{array}[]{l}(-q)^{a-1}(1-(-q)^{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{a-1})(1-(-q)^{a}-(-q)^{h+1}+(-q)^{h+2})\\
-(-q)^{a-1}(1-(-q)^{2})\dots(1-(-q)^{a})\\
+(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q))\dots(1-(-q)^{a-1})\\
+(-q)^{2a+2b-1}(1-(-q)^{a-1})(1-(-q))\dots(1-(-q)^{a-2})\\
=0.\end{array}

Therefore, (
10.14
) holds when
a+b=h+1
, and this finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 10.14
.

Assume that
a=0
. Then, we have

(10.19)

\begin{array}[]{ccl}&&D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)\\
+((-q)^{a}-1)&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)\\
+(-q)^{a}((-q)^{a}-1)&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)\\
-(-q)^{2a}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})&\times&D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)\\
-(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{a})&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)\end{array}

{{=}}\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988pt\begin{array}[]{cl}0&\text{ if }b\geq h+2\text{ or }b\leq h-2,\\
1&\text{ if }b=h-1,h,\\
q^{2h+1}+(-q)^{h}&\text{ if }b=h+1.\end{array}

Proof.

Note that when
a=0
, we have that (
10.19
) is

D_{n,h}(1,b,c)-(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})D_{n,h}(1,b-2,c+2).

If
b\leq h-2
, then we have that both
D_{n,h}(1,b,c)
and
D_{n,h}(1,b-2,c+2)
are zero.

If
b\geq h+2
, then by Theorem
9.8
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(1,b,c)-(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})D_{n,h}(1,b-2,c+2)\\
=\prod_{l=h+1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{l})-(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})\prod_{l=h+1}^{b-2}(1-(-q)^{l})=0.\end{array}

Now, assume that
b=h+1
. Then, by Theorem
9.8
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(1,b,c)-(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})D_{n,h}(1,b-2,c+2)\\
=(1-(-q)^{h+1})-(1-(-q)^{h+1})(1-(-q)^{h})=(-q)^{h}(1-(-q)^{h+1})=q^{2h+1}+(-q)^{h}.\end{array}

Finally, if
b=h,h-1
, then by Theorem
9.8
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(1,b,c)-(1-(-q)^{b})(1-(-q)^{b-1})D_{n,h}(1,b-2,c+2)=1.\end{array}

This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 10.15
.

Assume that
a=1
. Then, we have

(10.20)

\begin{array}[]{ccl}&&D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)\\
+((-q)^{a-1}-1)&\times&D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c)\\
-(-q)^{a-1}&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)\\
+(-q)^{2a+b-1}(1-(-q)^{b})&\times&D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)\\
+(-q)^{2a+2b-1}(1-(-q)^{a-1})&\times&D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2)\end{array}

{{=}}\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988pt\begin{array}[]{cl}0&\text{ if }b\geq h+1\text{ or }b\leq h-3,\\
1&\text{ if }b=h-2,\\
0&\text{ if }b=h-1,\\
q^{2h+1}&\text{ if }b=h.\end{array}

Proof.

Note that when
a=1
, we have that (
10.20
) is

D_{n,h}(1,b+1,c)-D_{n,h}(1,b,c+1)+(-q)^{b+1}(1-(-q)^{b})D_{n,h}(1,b-1,c+2).

If
b\leq h-3
, we have that
D_{n,h}(1,b+1,c)=D_{n,h}(1,b,c+1)=D_{n,h}(1,b-1,c+2)=0
.

If
b\geq h+1
, by Theorem
9.8
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(1,b+1,c)-D_{n,h}(1,b,c+1)+(-q)^{b+1}(1-(-q)^{b})D_{n,h}(1,b-1,c+2)\\
=\prod_{l=h+1}^{b+1}(1-(-q)^{l})-\prod_{l=h+1}^{b}(1-(-q)^{l})+(-q)^{b+1}(1-(-q)^{b})\prod_{l=h+1}^{b-1}(1-(-q)^{l})=0.\end{array}

If
b=h-2
, then we have

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(1,b+1,c)-D_{n,h}(1,b,c+1)+(-q)^{b+1}(1-(-q)^{b})D_{n,h}(1,b-1,c+2)=1-0+0=1.\end{array}

If
b=h-1
, then we have

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(1,b+1,c)-D_{n,h}(1,b,c+1)+(-q)^{b+1}(1-(-q)^{b})D_{n,h}(1,b-1,c+2)=1-1+0=0.\end{array}

If
b=h
, then we have

\begin{array}[]{l}D_{n,h}(1,b+1,c)-D_{n,h}(1,b,c+1)+(-q)^{b+1}(1-(-q)^{b})D_{n,h}(1,b-1,c+2)\\
=(1-(-q)^{h+1})-1+(-q)^{h+1}(1-(-q)^{h})=q^{2h+1}.\end{array}

This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Theorem 10.16
.

Assume that
x\perp L^{\flat}
,
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2
, and
L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}\subset(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\subset L^{\flat}_{F}
. Let
\lambda
be the fundamental invariants of
L^{\prime\flat}
and
(a,b,c)=(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda))
. Assume further that
(a,b,c)\neq(1,h,n-h-2),(1,h-2,n-h),(0,h+1,n-h-2),(0,h,n-h-1),(0,h-1,n-h)
. Then, we have

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)=0.

Proof.

Recall that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)={\sum}_{(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle).

Since
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2
, we have that
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp}))\geq 2
. Therefore, by Proposition
10.3
,
D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)
depends only on
u^{\flat}
. Also, we have that

\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=\vol(L^{\prime\flat})\vol(\langle u^{\perp}\rangle).

This implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)

\displaystyle={\sum}_{(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\vol(L^{\prime\flat})\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}){\sum}_{i\geq 0}q^{-2i}{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\vol(L^{\prime\flat})\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle).

Now, it suffices to show that
{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=0
.

We claim that
{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)
is a sum of two equations (
10.5
) and (
10.14
). More precisely, we claim that

(10.21)

\displaystyle{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\times\eqref{eq5.34}

\displaystyle+\{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}\times\eqref{eq5.42}.

Note that both sides of (
10.21
) are linear sums of the Cho-Yamauchi constants
D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)
,
D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)
,
D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)
,
D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)
,
D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)
,
D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c)
,
D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2)
. Therefore, it suffices to show that both sides have the same coefficients.

First, consider the coefficients of
D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)
on both sides. By Proposition
10.6
(Case 1-1), we have that the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)
on the left-hand side of (
10.21
) is
\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}).
Also, the coefficients of
D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)
on the right hand side of (
10.21
) is
\mu(L_{\lambda\geq 2-2})
(note that
D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)
appears only in (
10.5
)). Therefore, the coefficients of
D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)
on both sides are the same.

For
D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)
, by Proposition
10.6
(Case 1-2), we have that the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)
on the left hand side of (
10.21
) is
q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}).
Now, the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)
on the right hand side of (
10.21
) is

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\{(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}-1\}+\{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}\\
=q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}).\end{array}

Therefore, the coefficients of
D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)
on both sides are the same.

For
D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)
, by Proposition
10.6
(Case 1-3+Case 3-2), we have that the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)
on the left hand side of (
10.21
) is
q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1}).
On the other hand, the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)
on the right hand side of (
10.21
) is

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\{(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}-1\}-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}\{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}.\end{array}

Therefore, it suffices to show that

\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1})+(-q)^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})+(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3}).

By Lemma
10.10
, we have that

\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1})-q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=-(q-1)(-q)^{|\lambda|-t_{1}(\lambda)-t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1},

and

\displaystyle(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})+(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})

\displaystyle=(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\{-(q-1)(-q)^{|\lambda|-t_{1}(\lambda)-2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}\}

\displaystyle=-(q-1)(-q)^{|\lambda|-t_{1}(\lambda)-t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}.

This shows that the coefficients of
D_{n,h}(a,b,c+1)
on both sides are the same.

For
D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)
, by Proposition
10.6
(Case 2-1+Case 4-1), we have that the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)
on the left hand side of (
10.21
) is
q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\times(\mu(L_{\lambda=1})-1).
On the other hand, the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)
on the right hand side of (
10.21
) is

-(-q)^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}(1-(-q)^{t_{1}(\lambda)})(1-(-q)^{t_{1}(\lambda)-1})\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}).

Now, by Lemma
10.10
, we have that

(10.22)

\mu(L_{\lambda=1})-1=q^{2t_{1}(\lambda)-1}-(-q)^{t_{1}(\lambda)-1}(q-1)-1=-(1-(-q)^{t_{1}(\lambda)})(1-(-q)^{t_{1}(\lambda)-1}).

This shows that the coefficients of
D_{n,h}(a+1,b-2,c+2)
on both sides are the same.

For
D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)
, by Proposition
10.6
(Case 2-2+Case 4-2), we have that the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)
on the left hand side of (
10.21
) is

q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\{\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\mu(L_{\lambda=1})\}.

On the other hand, the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)
on the right hand side is

\begin{array}[]{l}-(-q)^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+t_{1}(\lambda)-1}(1-(-q)^{t_{1}(\lambda)})(1-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)})\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\\
+(-q)^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+t_{1}(\lambda)-1}(1-(-q)^{t_{1}(\lambda)})\{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}.\end{array}

Note that by Lemma
10.10
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})=\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})=q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)+2t_{1}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(q-1)(-q)^{|\lambda|-2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}.\\
\end{array}

Combining these with (
10.22
), it suffices to show that

-(q-1)(-q)^{|\lambda|-1}=(-q)^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+t_{1}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})+(-q)^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)+2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+t_{1}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3}).

Since
\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-2})
, this follows from Lemma
10.10
. This shows that the coefficients of
D_{n,h}(a,b-1,c+2)
on both sides are the same.

For
D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c)
, by Proposition
10.6
(Case 3-1), we have that the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c)
on the left hand side of (
10.21
) is

\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1})-q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3}).

On the other hand, the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c)
on the right hand side of (
10.21
) is

((-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}-1)\{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}.

Therefore, it suffices to show that

\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1})+(-q)^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})+(-q)^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)+t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3}).

Now, by Lemma
10.10
, we have that

\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1})+(-q)^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=-(-q)^{|\lambda|-t_{1}(\lambda)-t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}(q-1),

and

(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})+(-q)^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)+t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})=(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}(-(-q)^{|\lambda|-t_{1}(\lambda)-2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1})(q-1).

This shows that the coefficients of
D_{n,h}(a-1,b+2,c)
on both sides are the same.

For
D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2)
, by Proposition
10.6
(Case 5), we have that the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2)
on the left hand side of (
10.21
) is

\mu(L_{\lambda\geq 2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})-q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1}).

On the other hand, the coefficient of
D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2)
on the right hand side of (
10.21
) is

(-q)^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+2t_{1}(\lambda)-1}(1-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1})\{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}.

Note that by Lemma
10.10
, we have

\mu(L_{\lambda\geq 2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})=q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+2t_{1}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1})-(q-1)(-q)^{|\lambda|-1},

and

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})=\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-2}\obot L_{\lambda=1})=q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)+2t_{1}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(q-1)(-q)^{|\lambda|-2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}.\\
\end{array}

Therefore, it suffices to show that

\begin{array}[]{l}q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+2t_{1}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1})-q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)+2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+2t_{1}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})\\
=(-q)^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)+2t_{1}(\lambda)-1}(1-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1})\{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}.\end{array}

Note that this is equivalent to

\displaystyle\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-1})-q^{2t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})

\displaystyle=(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\{\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})-q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})\}

\displaystyle=(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\{\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-2})-q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})\}.

Now, by Lemma
10.10
, we have that both sides are equal to
-(-q)^{|\lambda|-t_{1}(\lambda)-t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)-1}(q-1)
. This shows that the coefficients of
D_{n,h}(a-1,b,c+2)
on both sides are the same.

This finishes the proof of (
10.21
). Now, the theorem follows from Lemma
10.12
, Lemma
10.13
, Lemma
10.14
, and Lemma
10.15
.

∎

Theorem 10.17
.

Assume that
x\perp L^{\flat}
,
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1
, and
L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}\subset(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\subset L^{\flat}_{F}
. Let
\lambda
be the fundamental invariants of
L^{\prime\flat}
and
(a,b,c)=(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda))
. Assume further that
(a,b,c)\neq(1,h,n-h-2),(1,h-2,n-h),(0,h+1,n-h-2),(0,h,n-h-1),(0,h-1,n-h)
. Then, we have

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)=-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c).

Proof.

Recall that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)={\sum}_{(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle).

Since
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1
, we have that
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp}))\geq 1
. In the proof of Theorem
10.16
, we proved that

{\sum}_{(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times},{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp}))\geq 2}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=0.

Therefore, we have that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times},\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})=1\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle).

Therefore, by Proposition
10.4
,
D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)
depends only on
u^{\flat}
.
Also, we have that

\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=\vol(L^{\prime\flat})\vol(\langle u^{\perp}\rangle).

This implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)

\displaystyle={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times},\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp}))=1\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\vol(L^{\prime\flat})(q)^{-1}{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle).

Note that Proposition
10.3
and Proposition
10.4
are different only when (Case 1-1) and (Case 1-2). Also, it is easy to see that

\begin{array}[]{l}\text{(Case 1-2-1)}\\
|\{u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}\mid{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\flat}+u^{\perp},u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}))=1\}|\\
=\dfrac{q-2}{q-1}\text{(Case 1-2)}=\dfrac{q-2}{q-1}(q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})),\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}\text{(Case 1-2-2)}\\
|\{u^{\flat}\in(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 1}-(\pi^{2}(L_{2}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\obot\pi(L_{1}^{\prime\flat})^{\vee})^{\geq 2}\mid{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\flat}+u^{\perp},u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}))\geq 2\}|\\
=\dfrac{1}{q-1}\text{(Case 1-2)}=\dfrac{1}{q-1}(q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})).\end{array}

Now, note that if
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp}))\geq 2
, then
{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=0
. Therefore, for
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp}))=1
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)=\dfrac{1}{q}\vol(L^{\prime\flat}){\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\\
=\dfrac{1}{q}\vol(L^{\prime\flat})\{(D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)-D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c))\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\\
\qquad\qquad+(D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c))\dfrac{1}{q-1}(q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2}))\}\\
=\dfrac{1}{q}\vol(L^{\prime\flat})(D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c))\{\dfrac{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}}{q-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\dfrac{q}{q-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}.\end{array}

By Lemma
10.10
, we have

\dfrac{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}}{q-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\dfrac{q}{q-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=-(-q)^{|\lambda|-b-2a}.

Also, by Theorem
9.4
, we have

D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c)=-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c).

Finally, we have that
\vol(L^{\prime\flat})=q^{-|\lambda|}
. Therefore, we have

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)=\dfrac{(-q)^{|\lambda|+2n-2a-2b-2c-h-1}}{q^{|\lambda|+1}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c).

Now, note that
a+b+c=n-1
and
{\mathrm{val}}(L^{\prime\flat}\obot\langle x\rangle)=|\lambda|-1\equiv h+1\pmod{2}.
This implies that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)=-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c).

This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Theorem 10.18
.

Assume that
x\perp L^{\flat}
,
{\mathrm{val}}(\langle x,x\rangle)\leq-2
, and
L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}\subset(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\subset L^{\flat}_{F}
. Let
\lambda
be the fundamental invariants of
L^{\prime\flat}
and
(a,b,c)=(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda))
. Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{h-1}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(1,h,n-h-2),\\
q^{-h}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h),\\
q^{-(h-1)}\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(0,h-1,n-h),\\
q^{-h}\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(0,h,n-h-1),\\
q^{-(h+1)}(q^{2h+1}+(-q)^{h})\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(0,h+1,n-h-2).\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Proof.

In the proof of Theorem
10.16
, we proved that

\begin{array}[]{ll}\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)=\vol(L^{\prime\flat})\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle).\par\end{array}

Also, in (
10.21
), we showed that

\displaystyle{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\times\eqref{eq5.34}

\displaystyle+\{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}\times\eqref{eq5.42}.

First, assume that
(a,b,c)=(1,h,n-h-2)
,
\lambda=(\alpha,\overset{h}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}},\overset{n-h-2}{\overbrace{0,\dots,0}})
. In this case, we know that (
10.5
)
=0
. Also, it is easy to see that
\mu(L_{\alpha})=q^{2[\frac{\alpha}{2}]}
, and hence

\begin{array}[]{l}q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\alpha)}\mu(L_{\alpha-3})+q\mu(L_{\alpha-2})\\
=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1+q&\text{ if }\alpha=2,\\
q^{2+2[\frac{\alpha-3}{2}]}+q^{2[\frac{\alpha-2}{2}]+1}=q^{\alpha-2}(q+1)&\text{ if }\alpha\geq 3,\end{array}\right.\\
=q^{\alpha-2}(q+1).\end{array}

Now, by Lemma
10.15
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=q^{\alpha-2}(q+1)q^{2h+1}.\end{array}

Similarly, for
(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h)
,
\lambda=(\alpha,\overset{h-2}{\overbrace{1,\dots,1}},\overset{n-h}{\overbrace{0,\dots,0}})
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=q^{\alpha-2}(q+1).\end{array}

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)

\displaystyle=\vol(L^{\prime\flat})\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{h-1}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(1,h,n-h-2),\\
q^{-h}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h).\end{array}\right.

Now, assume that
a=0
. Then, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=1,\\
q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-(-q)^{t_{\geq 2}(\lambda)}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})=0.\end{array}

Therefore, by Lemma
10.14
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\text{ if }b=h-1,h,\\
q^{2h+1}+(-q)^{h}&\text{ if }b=h+1.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

This implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)

\displaystyle=\vol(L^{\prime\flat})\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}{\sum}_{u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{-(h-1)}\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(0,h-1,n-h),\\
q^{-h}\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(0,h,n-h-1),\\
q^{-(h+1)}(q^{2h+1}+(-q)^{h})\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(0,h+1,n-h-2).\end{array}\right.

This finishes the proof of the theorem.

∎

Theorem 10.19
.

Assume that
x\perp L^{\flat}
,
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1
, and
L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}\subset(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\subset L^{\flat}_{F}
. Let
\lambda
be the fundamental invariants of
L^{\prime\flat}
and
(a,b,c)=(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda))
. Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)\\
=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{h-2}(q+1)(1-q^{-2})^{-1}-q^{-h}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(1,h,n-h-2),\\
q^{-h-1}(q+1)(1-q^{-2})^{-1}-q^{-h}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h),\\
q^{-h-1}(1-q^{-2})^{-1}-q^{-h}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(0,h,n-h-1).\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Note that two cases
(a,b,c)=(0,h-1,n-h),(0,h+1,n-h-2)
are not possible since
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1
and hence
{\mathrm{val}}(L^{\flat})\equiv h\pmod{2}
.

Proof.

Recall that

(10.23)

\displaystyle\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)

\displaystyle={\sum}_{(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\vol(L^{\prime\flat}){\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})=1\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(\langle u^{\perp}\rangle)

\displaystyle+\vol(L^{\prime\flat}){\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})\geq 3\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(\langle u^{\perp}\rangle).
