In the proof of the Theorem
10.18
, we showed that

\begin{array}[]{l}\vol(L^{\prime\flat}){\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})\geq 3\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(\langle u^{\perp}\rangle)\\
=\vol(L^{\prime\flat})\dfrac{1}{q^{3}}(1-q^{-2})^{-1}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})\geq 3\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle).\end{array}

Also, we already computed the sum
{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})\geq 3\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)
in the proof of the Theorem
10.18
(more precisely, we computed this for
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp}))\geq 2
). Therefore, we only need to compute

\vol(L^{\prime\flat}){\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})=1\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(\langle u^{\perp}\rangle).

Note that Proposition
10.3
and Proposition
10.4
are different only when (Case 1-1) and (Case 1-2). Therefore, as in the proof of Theorem
10.17
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})=1\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)-{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})\geq 2\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\\
=(D_{n,h}(a+1,b,c)-D_{n,h}(a,b+1,c))\{\dfrac{q^{2t_{\geq 3}(\lambda)}}{q-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 3)-3})-\dfrac{q}{q-1}\mu(L_{(\lambda\geq 2)-2})\}\\
=(-(-q)^{2n-h-1-b-2c}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c))(-(-q)^{|\lambda|-b-2a})\\
=(-q)^{|\lambda|-h+1}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c).\end{array}

Since
\vol(L^{\prime\flat})=q^{-|\lambda|}
and
{\mathrm{val}}(L^{\prime\flat}\obot\langle x\rangle)=|\lambda|-1\equiv h+1\pmod{2}
, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}\vol(L^{\prime\flat}){\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(u^{\flat},u^{\perp})\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\times(\langle x\rangle^{\vee}\backslash\{0\})/O_{F}^{\times}\\
{\mathrm{val}}(\langle u^{\perp},u^{\perp}\rangle=1\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\vol(\langle u^{\perp}\rangle)\\
=\dfrac{(-q)^{|\lambda|-h+1}}{q^{|\lambda|+1}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)+\dfrac{\vol(L^{\prime\flat})}{q}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})\geq 2\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)\\
=-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)+\dfrac{\vol(L^{\prime\flat})}{q}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})\geq 2\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle).\end{array}

Now, the theorem follows from (
10.23
) and the fact that (see the proof of Theorem
10.18
)

\begin{array}[]{ll}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}u^{\flat}\in(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee,\geq 0}/L^{\prime\flat}\\
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\perp},u^{\perp})\geq 2\end{subarray}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime\flat}+\langle u^{\flat}+u^{\perp}\rangle)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{h-1}(q+1)q^{|\lambda|}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(1,h,n-h-2),\\
q^{-h}(q+1)q^{|\lambda|}&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h-1),\\
1&\text{ if }(a,b,c)=(0,h,n-h).\end{array}\right.\end{array}

∎

We will use the following lemma in the next section when we count the number of horizontal components.

Lemma 10.20
.

Assume that
\lambda\geq 2
. Consider
(\lambda,0^{n-1}),(\lambda,2^{n-1})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n}^{0+}
and
(0^{n-1}),(2^{n-1})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{0+}
. Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{n-1})},A_{(2^{n-1})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{n-1})},A_{(0^{n-1})})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{2n-2}&\text{ if }\lambda\geq 3,\\
q^{2n-2}\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-n}}{1-(-q)^{-1}}&\text{ if }\lambda=2.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

Proof.

We use
[
3
, Theorem 2.5]
to prove this. To simplify notation, we use the following convention: For
1\leq k\leq n
,
B_{1}\in X_{n}(O_{F})
, and
B_{2}\in X_{n-1}(O_{F})
, we define

\begin{array}[]{lll}R_{0}^{k}=\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,1^{k-1},0^{n-k})},B_{1}),&R_{1}^{k}=\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,2^{k-1},1^{n-k})},B_{1}),&R_{2}^{k}=\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,3^{k-1},2^{n-k})},B_{1}),\\
R_{3}^{k}=\mathrm{Den}(A_{(1^{k-1},0^{n-k})},B_{2}),&R_{4}^{k}=\mathrm{Den}(A_{(2^{k-1},1^{n-k})},B_{2}),&R_{5}^{k}=\mathrm{Den}(A_{(3^{k-1},2^{n-k})},B_{2}),\end{array}

and for a polynomial
f(X)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}X^{i}
, we denote by
f(R_{i})
the sum
f(R_{i})\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}R_{i}^{k}
. For example, if
f(X)=X+X^{2}
, then
f(R_{1})=\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},B_{1})+\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,1^{1},0^{n-2})},B_{1})
(not
\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},B_{1})+\{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},B_{1})\}^{2}
).

Also, we define the following polynomials

\begin{array}[]{ll}f_{1,k}(X)=X^{k}\prod_{l=k+1}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X),&f_{2,k}(X)=X\prod_{l=k+1}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X),\\
f_{3,k}(X)=X^{k}\prod_{l=k}^{n-1}(1-(-q)^{-l}X),&f_{4,k}(X)=X\prod_{l=k}^{n-1}(1-(-q)^{-l}X).\end{array}

Finally, for
0\leq j\leq i
, we define the following constants
k_{i,j}
:

\prod_{l=1}^{i}(1+(-q)^{-l}X)=\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}X^{j}.

First, we claim that

(10.24)

f_{2,i+1}(X)=\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j+1)}{2}}f_{1,j+1}(X),0\leq i\leq n-1,

and

(10.25)

f_{4,i+1}(X)=\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j-1)}{2}}f_{3,j+1}(X),0\leq i\leq n-1.

Let us prove (
10.24
) by induction on
i
. For
i=0
, the claim holds since
f_{2,1}(X)=f_{1,1}(X)
. Now, assume that the claim holds for
i
, i.e.,

(10.26)

X\prod_{l=i+2}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X)=\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j+1)}{2}}X^{j+1}\prod_{l=j+2}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X).

Note that
\sum_{j=0}^{i+1}k_{i+1,j}X^{j}=\prod_{l=1}^{i+1}(1+(-q)^{-l}X)=(1+(-q)^{-i-1}X)(\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}X^{j})
, and hence

(10.27)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i+1}k_{i+1,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j+1)}{2}}f_{1,j+1}(X)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j+1)}{2}}f_{1,j+1}(X)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{i}(-q)^{-i-1}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{(j+1)(j+2)}{2}}f_{1,j+2}(X).

Now, consider the equation (
10.26
) with
X\Rightarrow(-q)^{-1}X
,
n\Rightarrow n-1
. Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}&(-q)^{-1}X\prod_{l=i+3}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X)=\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{(j+1)(j+2)}{2}}X^{j+1}\prod_{l=j+3}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X)\\
\Longleftrightarrow&\sum_{j=0}^{i}(-q)^{-i-1}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{(j+1)(j+2)}{2}}f_{1,j+2}(X)=(-q)^{-i-2}X^{2}\prod_{l=i+3}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X).\end{array}

Combining this with (
10.27
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i+1}k_{i+1,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j+1)}{2}}f_{1,j+1}(X)

\displaystyle=X\prod_{l=i+2}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X)+(-q)^{-i-2}X^{2}\prod_{l=i+3}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X)

\displaystyle=X\prod_{l=i+3}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l}X).

This finishes the proof of (
10.24
). The equation (
10.25
) can be proved in a similar way.

Now, note that
[
3
, Theorem 2.5]
implies that if
B_{1}=A_{\eta_{1}}
,
B_{2}=A_{\eta_{2}}
such that
\eta_{1}\geq(\overset{n}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}})
,
\eta_{2}\geq(\overset{n-1}{\overbrace{2,\dots,2}})
, then we have

(10.28)

\begin{array}[]{ll}f_{1,j}(R_{0})=\dfrac{(-1)^{n-j}}{(-q)^{n(n-j)}}f_{2,j}(R_{1}),&f_{1,j}(R_{1})=\dfrac{(-1)^{n-j}}{(-q)^{n(n-j)}}f_{2,j}(R_{2}),\\
f_{3,j}(R_{3})=\dfrac{(-1)^{n-j}}{(-q)^{(n-1)(n-j)}}f_{4,j}(R_{4}),&f_{3,j}(R_{4})=\dfrac{(-1)^{n-j}}{(-q)^{(n-1)(n-j)}}f_{4,j}(R_{5}).\\
\end{array}

Furthermore, if
B_{1}=A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})}
and
B_{2}=A_{(2^{n-1})}
, then
R_{2}^{k}=R_{5}^{k}=0
for all
k>1
. Therefore,

(10.29)

\begin{array}[]{ll}f_{2,j}(R_{2})=R_{2}=\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})}),&f_{4,j}(R_{5})=R_{5}=\mathrm{Den}(A_{(2^{n-1})},A_{(2^{n-1})}).\end{array}

Since
\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,1,0^{n-2})},A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})})=0
, we have

(10.30)

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})})=R_{0}(1-(-q)^{-n}R_{0})=f_{2,n-1}(R_{0})&\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}k_{n-2,i}(-q)^{-\frac{i(i+1)}{2}}f_{1,i+1}(R_{0})&(\text{by }\eqref{eq5.52})\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}k_{n-2,i}(-q)^{-\frac{i(i+1)}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{n-i-1}}{(-q)^{n(n-i-1)}}f_{2,i+1}(R_{1})&(\text{by }\eqref{eq5.56})\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}k_{n-2,i}(-q)^{-\frac{i(i+1)}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{n-i-1}}{(-q)^{n(n-i-1)}}\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j+1)}{2}}f_{1,j+1}(R_{1})&(\text{by }\eqref{eq5.52})\\
=\{\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}k_{n-2,i}(-q)^{-\frac{i(i+1)}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{n-i-1}}{(-q)^{n(n-i-1)}}\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j+1)}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{n-j-1}}{(-q)^{n(n-j-1)}}\}R_{2}&(\text{by }\eqref{eq5.56},\eqref{eq5.57}).\end{array}

Similarly, note that
\mathrm{Den}(A_{(1,0^{n-2})},A_{(2^{n-1})})=0
, and hence

(10.31)

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{n-1})},A_{(2^{n-1})})=R_{3}(1-(-q)^{-(n-1)}R_{3})=f_{4,n-1}(R_{3})&\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}k_{n-2,i}(-q)^{-\frac{i(i-1)}{2}}f_{3,i+1}(R_{3})&(\text{by }\eqref{eq5.53})\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}k_{n-2,i}(-q)^{-\frac{i(i-1)}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{n-i-1}}{(-q)^{(n-1)(n-i-1)}}f_{4,i+1}(R_{4})&(\text{by }\eqref{eq5.56})\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}k_{n-2,i}(-q)^{-\frac{i(i-1)}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{n-i-1}}{(-q)^{(n-1)(n-i-1)}}\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j-1)}{2}}f_{3,j+1}(R_{4})&(\text{by }\eqref{eq5.53})\\
=\{\sum_{i=0}^{n-2}k_{n-2,i}(-q)^{-\frac{i(i-1)}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{n-i-1}}{(-q)^{(n-1)(n-i-1)}}\sum_{j=0}^{i}k_{i,j}(-q)^{-\frac{j(j-1)}{2}}\frac{(-1)^{n-j-1}}{(-q)^{(n-1)(n-j-1)}}\}R_{5}&(\text{by }\eqref{eq5.56},\eqref{eq5.57}).\end{array}

Comparing (
10.30
) and (
10.31
), we have

(10.32)

(-q)^{2n-2}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})})}=\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{n-1})},A_{(2^{n-1})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(2^{n-1})},A_{(2^{n-1})})}.

Now, by
[
5
, Lemma 4.6]
, we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathrm{Den}(A_{(2^{n-1})},A_{(2^{n-1})})=q^{2(n-1)^{2}}\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{n-1})},A_{(0^{n-1})})=q^{2(n-1)^{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{n-1}(1-(-q)^{-l}),\\
\mathrm{Den}(A_{\lambda,(0^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})})=q^{\lambda}(1-(-q)^{-1})\prod_{l=1}^{n-1}(1-(-q)^{-l}),\text{ if }\lambda\geq 1,\end{array}

\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{2(n^{2}-1)+\lambda}(1-(-q)^{-1})\prod_{l=1}^{n-1}(1-(-q)^{-l})&\text{ if }\lambda\geq 3,\\
q^{2n^{2}}\prod_{l=1}^{n}(1-(-q)^{-l})&\text{ if }\lambda=2.\end{array}\right.

Combining these with (
10.32
), we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{n-1})},A_{(2^{n-1})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{n-1})},A_{(0^{n-1})})}\\
=(-q)^{-2n+2}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,2^{n-1})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})},A_{(\lambda,0^{n-1})})}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{n-1})},A_{(0^{n-1})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(2^{n-1})},A_{(2^{n-1})})}\par\par=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{2n-2}&\text{ if }\lambda\geq 3,\\
q^{2n-2}\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-n}}{1-(-q)^{-1}}&\text{ if }\lambda=2.\end{array}\right.\end{array}

This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

11.
Tate conjectures and the proof of the main theorem

11.1.
The proof of the main theorem

Consider the Rapoport–Zink space
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
and the space of special homomorphisms
{\mathbb{V}}
. Assume that
L^{\flat}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
is an
O_{F}
-lattice of rank
n-1
. Recall that for
L^{\prime\flat}
such that
L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}\subset(L^{\prime\flat})^{\vee}\subset L^{\flat}_{F}
, we define the primitive part
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}
of
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})
inductively by setting

{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}\coloneqq{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})-{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime\prime\flat}\\
L^{\prime\prime\flat}\subset(L^{\prime\prime\flat})^{\vee}\subset L^{\prime\flat}_{F}\end{subarray}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\prime\flat})^{\circ}.

For example, for a lattice
L^{\prime\flat}
with fundamental invariants
(0,0,\dots,0,1)
, there is no integral lattice
L^{\prime\prime\flat}
such that
L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime\prime\flat}
and hence
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}={\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})
. For a lattice
L^{\prime\flat}
with fundamental invariants
(0,0,\dots,0,3)
, there is one integral lattice
L^{\prime\prime\flat}
satisfying the above conditions, and its fundamental invariants are
(0,0,\dots,0,1)
. Therefore, we have
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}={\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})-{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\prime\flat})^{\circ}={\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})-{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\prime\flat})
.

Now, let us define the derived special cycles
\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L)
.

Definition 11.1
.

For a lattice
L\subset{\mathbb{V}}
, choose its basis
x_{1},\dots,x_{r}
. We define the derived special cycle
\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L)
as the image of
O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}\dots\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x_{r})}
in the
r
-th graded piece of the Grothendieck group
\text{Gr}^{r}K_{0}^{{\mathcal{Z}}(L)}({\mathcal{N}})
. This does not depend on the choice of the basis by Proposition
2.12
.

Then, Conjecture
7.7
is equivalent to the following statement: for
x\in{\mathbb{V}}\backslash L_{F}^{\flat}
,

(11.1)

\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)\coloneqq{\sum}_{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee},L^{\prime}\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime})1_{L^{\prime}}(x),

where
L^{\prime}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
are
O_{F}
-lattices of rank
n
. For example, for a lattice
L^{\prime\flat}
with fundamental invariants
(0,0,\dots,1)
, it is obvious that Conjecture
7.7
is equivalent to (
11.1
). For a lattice
L^{\prime\flat}
with fundamental invariants
(0,0,\dots,3)
, there is one integral lattice
L^{\prime\prime\flat}
such that
L^{\prime\flat}\subsetneq L^{\prime\prime\flat}
, and the fundamental invariants of
L^{\prime\prime\flat}
is
(0,0,\dots,1)
. Therefore, Conjecture
7.7
is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{ll}\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})&={\sum}_{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime})1_{L^{\prime}}(x)-{\sum}_{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee},L^{\prime}\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime})1_{L^{\prime}}(x)\\
&={\sum}_{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime}\subset L^{\prime\vee},L^{\prime}\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat}}D_{n,h}(L^{\prime})1_{L^{\prime}}(x),\end{array}

which is equivalent to (
11.1
).

Now, note that there is a decomposition of the derived special cycle
{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\flat})
into a sum of horizontal and vertical parts (see Section
2.3
):

\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})=\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{H}}}+\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{V}}}.

We denote by
\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{H}}}^{\circ}
(resp.
\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{V}}}^{\circ})
the primitive part of
\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{H}}}
(resp.
\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{V}}}
).

We define

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)\coloneqq\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{H}}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)}),&\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)\coloneqq\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{V}}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)}),\\
\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)\coloneqq\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{H}}}^{\circ}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)}),&\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)\coloneqq\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{V}}}^{\circ}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)}).\end{array}

Then, Conjecture
7.6
is equivalent to

(11.2)

\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)+\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat}}^{n,h}(x).

or

(11.3)

\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)+\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x).

Now, we define
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)
by

\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)\coloneqq\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)-\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x).

Let
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{0+}
be the fundamental invariants of
L^{\prime\flat}
. Then, by Lemma
5.6
, we know that if
L^{\prime\flat}\notin H({\mathbb{V}})
(i.e.,
(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda))\neq(1,h,n-h-2),(1,h-2,n-h),(0,h-1,n-h),(0,h,n-h-1),(0,h+1,n-h-2)
), then we have

\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=0,

and hence we have that

\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x),

in this case.

When
L^{\prime\flat}\in H({\mathbb{V}})
, then the horizontal part of
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})
is not empty, and hence we need to be careful. Note that by Theorem
5.3
, the horizontal part of
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}
comes from
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{2}
or
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{2}
. By
[
13
, Theorem 1.1]
and reductions in Proposition
2.7
, we have a precise formula for
\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)
.

Now, we will prove the following theorem.

Theorem 11.2
.

(cf.
[
19
, Theorem 7.4.1]
)
Assume that
x\perp L^{\prime\flat}
and
(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda))=(a,b,c)
where
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{0+}
is the fundamental invariants of
L^{\prime\flat}
. Then, we have the followings.

(1)

If
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2
, we have
\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=0
.

(2)

If
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1
, we have

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)&\text{if }(a,b,c)\neq(1,h-2,n-h),\\
\\
-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)+\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}&\text{if }(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h).\end{array}\right.

Proof.

First, if
(a,b,c)\coloneqq(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda))\neq(1,h,n-h-2),(1,h-2,n-h),(0,h-1,n-h),(0,h,n-h-1),(0,h+1,n-h-2)
, then the assertion (1) follows from Theorem
10.16
, and the assertion (2) follows from Theorem
10.17
.

Now, assume that
(a,b,c)=(1,h,n-h-2),(1,h-2,n-h),(0,h-1,n-h),(0,h,n-h-1),(0,h+1,n-h-2)
. Since we already know the Fourier transform of
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)
by Theorem
10.18
and Theorem
10.19
, we only need to know the Fourier transform of
\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)
.

When
(a,b,c)=(1,h,n-h-2)
, let us write
L^{\prime\flat}=L_{2}\obot L_{1}\obot L_{0}
, where the hermitian matrix of
L_{2}
,
L_{1}
,
L_{0}
are
\pi^{\lambda}
(
\lambda\geq 2
),
\pi I_{h}
,
I_{n-h-2}
, respectively. Then, by Proposition
2.7
,
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
can be reduced to
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
N^{[h]}_{h+2}
. Therefore, by Theorem
5.3
, we have

(11.4)

{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{2}\obot L_{1}\subset L_{2}\oplus N\subset\pi^{-1}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})\\
N\simeq(\pi^{-1})^{h}\end{subarray}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}\cdot{\mathcal{Y}}(N)^{\circ}.

By Proposition
2.7
again,
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
can be reduced to
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}={\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{2}
. Now, note that by
[
13
, Theorem 1.1]
, we know that the Kudla-Rapoport conjecture holds in the case of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{2}
, and hence

\mathrm{Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=\mathrm{Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}^{2,0}(x).

By Theorem
10.18
and Theorem
10.19
, we have that

\widehat{\Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)=\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}^{2,0}(x)=q^{-1}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.

Now, by
[
30
, Proposition 8.2]
, we know that via the embedding
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
, the Fourier transform of
\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(N_{2})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{0})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})
is

\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\widehat{\Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)=q^{-h-1}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.

Now, by Lemma
10.20
, the number of lattices
L_{2}\oplus N
in the sum in (
11.4
) is

\begin{array}[]{l}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,(-1)^{h})},A_{(\lambda,1^{h})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{(\lambda,(-1)^{h})},A_{(\lambda,(-1)^{h})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{((-1)^{h})},A_{(1^{h})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{((-1)^{h})},A_{((-1)^{h})})}\\
=\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{((\lambda+1),0^{h})},A_{((\lambda+1),2^{h})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{((\lambda+1),0^{h})},A_{((\lambda+1),0^{h})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{h})},A_{(2^{h})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{h})},A_{(0^{h})})}=q^{2h}.\end{array}

This implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)

\displaystyle=q^{2h}q^{-h-1}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=q^{h-1}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.

Now, by Theorem
10.18
, Theorem
10.19
, we have that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)-\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2,\\
-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1.\end{array}\right.

This proves (1) and (2) when
(a,b,c)=(1,h,n-h-2)
.

Assume that
(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h)
and let us write
L^{\prime\flat}=L_{2}\obot L_{1}\obot L_{0}
, where the hermitian matrix of
L_{2}
,
L_{1}
,
L_{0}
are
\pi^{\lambda}
(
\lambda\geq 2
),
\pi I_{h-2}
,
I_{n-h}
, respectively. Then, by Proposition
2.7
,
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
can be reduced to
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{h}
. Also, in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{h}
, we have that
{\mathcal{Z}}(w)={\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{-1}w)
for any
w\in{\mathbb{V}}
, and hence
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
can be reduced to
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}={\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{2}
. Now, note that by
[
13
, Theorem 1.1]
, we know that the Kudla-Rapoport conjecture holds in the case of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{2}
, and hence

\mathrm{Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=\mathrm{Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}^{2,2}(x).

By Theorem
10.18
and Theorem
10.19
, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}^{2,2}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{-2}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2,\\
q^{-3}(q+1)(1-q^{-2})^{-1}-q^{-2}&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1.\end{array}\right.

Now, by
[
30
, Proposition 8.2]
, we know that via the embedding
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
, the Fourier transform of
\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{-1}L_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{0})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})
is

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{q^{h-2}}\widehat{\Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}q^{-h}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2,\\
q^{-h-1}(q+1)(1-q^{-2})^{-1}-q^{-h}&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1.\end{array}\right.

Now, by Theorem
10.18
, Theorem
10.19
, we have that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)-\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2,\\
-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)+\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1.\end{array}\right.

This proves (1) and (2) when
(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h)
.

Assume that
(a,b,c)=(0,h-1,n-h)
and let us write
L^{\prime\flat}=L_{2}\obot L_{1}\obot L_{0}
, where the hermitian matrix of
L_{2}
,
L_{1}
,
L_{0}
are
\pi I_{1}
.
\pi I_{h-2}
,
I_{n-h}
, respectively. Note that in this case,
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\neq-1
since
{\mathrm{val}}(L^{\flat}\obot\langle x\rangle)\equiv h+1\pmod{2}
. By Proposition
2.7
,
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
can be reduced to
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{h}
. Also, in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{h}
, we have that
{\mathcal{Z}}(w)={\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{-1}w)
for any
w\in{\mathbb{V}}
, and hence
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
can be reduced to
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}={\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{2}
. Now, note that by
[
13
, Theorem 1.1]
, we know that the Kudla-Rapoport conjecture holds in the case of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{2}
, and hence

\mathrm{Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=\mathrm{Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}^{2,2}(x).

By Theorem
10.18
, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}^{2,2}(x)=q^{-1}\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.

Now, by
[
30
, Proposition 8.2]
, we know that via the embedding
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
, the Fourier transform of
\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{-1}L_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{0})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})
is

\begin{array}[]{l}\dfrac{1}{q^{h-2}}\widehat{\Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)=q^{-h+1}\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.\end{array}

By Theorem
10.18
we have that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)-\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=0,

This proves (1) when
(a,b,c)=(0,h-1,n-h)
.

Assume that
(a,b,c)=(0,h,n-h-1)
and let us write
L^{\prime\flat}=L_{1}\obot L_{0}
, where the hermitian matrix of
L_{1}
,
L_{0}
are
\pi I_{h}
,
I_{n-h-1}
, respectively. By Proposition
2.7
,
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
can be reduced to
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{h+1}
. Therefore, by Theorem
5.3
, we have that
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}={\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{-1}L_{1})
and hence
\langle{\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{-1}L_{1}),{\mathcal{Z}}(x)\rangle
is
\langle{\mathcal{Z}}(x)\rangle
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{1}
. Now, By
[
13
, Theorem 1.1]
and Proposition
2.7
, we know that
\langle{\mathcal{Z}}(x)\rangle=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{\emptyset}^{1,0}(x)
.
By Theorem
10.18
and Theorem
10.19
, we have that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{\emptyset}^{1,0}(x)=\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.

Now, by
[
30
, Proposition 8.2]
, we know that via the embedding
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{1}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
, the Fourier transform of
\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{-1}L_{1})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{0})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})
is

\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{\emptyset}^{1,0}(x)=q^{-h}\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)

\displaystyle=q^{-h}\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}

Now, by Theorem
10.18
, Theorem
10.19
, we have that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)-\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2,\\
-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)&\text{if }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1.\end{array}\right.

This proves (1) and (2) when
(a,b,c)=(0,h,n-h-1)
.

When
(a,b,c)=(0,h+1,n-h-2)
, let us write
L^{\prime\flat}=L_{2}\obot L_{1}\obot L_{0}
, where the hermitian matrix of
L_{2}
,
L_{1}
,
L_{0}
are
\pi^{1}I_{1}
,
\pi I_{h}
,
I_{n-h-2}
, respectively. Note that in this case,
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\neq-1
since
{\mathrm{val}}(L^{\flat}\obot\langle x\rangle)\equiv h+1\pmod{2}
. By Proposition
2.7
,
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
can be reduced to
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
in
N^{[h]}_{h+2}
. Therefore, by Theorem
5.3
, we have

(11.5)

{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}={\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{2}\obot L_{1}\subset L_{2}\oplus N\subset\pi^{-1}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})\\
N\simeq(\pi^{-1})^{h}\end{subarray}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}\cdot{\mathcal{Y}}(N)^{\circ}.

By Proposition
2.7
again,
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2}\obot L_{1})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}
can be reduced to
{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}={\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}
in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{2}
. Now, note that by
[
13
, Theorem 1.1]
, we know that the Kudla-Rapoport conjecture holds in the case of
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{2}
, and hence

\mathrm{Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=\mathrm{Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}^{2,0}(x).

By Theorem
10.18
and Theorem
10.19
, we have that

\widehat{\Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)=\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}^{2,0}(x)=q^{-1}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.

Now, by
[
30
, Proposition 8.2]
, we know that via the embedding
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{2}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
, the Fourier transform of
\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(N_{2})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{0})}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})
is

\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\widehat{\Int}_{L_{2}^{\circ}}(x)=q^{-h-1}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.

Now, by Lemma
10.20
, the number of lattices
L_{2}\oplus N
in the sum in (
11.5
) is

\begin{array}[]{l}\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(1,(-1)^{h})},A_{(1,1^{h})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{(1,(-1)^{h})},A_{(1,(-1)^{h})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{((-1)^{h})},A_{(1^{h})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{((-1)^{h})},A_{((-1)^{h})})}\\
=\dfrac{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(2,0^{h})},A_{(2,2^{h})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{(2,0^{h})},A_{(2,0^{h})})}{\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{h})},A_{(2^{h})})/\mathrm{Den}(A_{(0^{h})},A_{(0^{h})})}=q^{2h}\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-h-1}}{1-(-q)^{-1}}.\end{array}

This implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)

\displaystyle=q^{2h}\dfrac{1-(-q)^{-h-1}}{1-(-q)^{-1}}q^{-h-1}(q+1)\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=q^{h}(1-(-q)^{-h-1})\vol(\langle x\rangle^{\vee})(1-q^{-2})^{-1}.

Now, by Theorem
10.18
, Theorem
10.19
, we have that

\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)-\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{H}}}(x)=0.

This proves (1) when
(a,b,c)=(0,h+1,n-h-2)
.

This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Proposition 11.3
.

(cf.
[
19
, Proposition 7.3.4]
,
[
18
, Proposition 2.22]
)
Assume that
L^{\flat}
be an
O_{F}
-lattice of rank
n-1
in
{\mathbb{V}}
. Then,
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)
extends uniquely to a compactly supported locally constant function on
{\mathbb{V}}
(we still denote it by
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)
).

Proof.

Even though our situation is more similar to
[
19
, Proposition 7.3.4]
, we do not have a functional equation like
[
19
, (3.2.0.2)]
yet. Therefore, let us follow the proof of
[
18
, Proposition 2.22]
. Basically, the proof is very similar to the proof of Theorem
11.2
(1).

Note that if
L^{\flat}
is not integral, then
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=0
. Therefore, we can assume that
L^{\flat}
is integral. Now, it suffices to show that for every
y\in L^{\flat}_{F}/L^{\flat}
, there exists an integer
\delta(y)>0
such that
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(y+x)
is constant for
x\in\pi^{\delta(y)}((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}\backslash\{0\}
, where
{\mathbb{V}}=L^{\flat}_{F}\obot(L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp}
and
((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}=\{(L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp}\mid(x,x)\in O_{F}\}
. If
L^{\flat}+\langle y\rangle
is not integral, then there exists
\delta(y)
such that
L^{\flat}+\langle y+x\rangle
is not integral for
x\in\pi^{\delta(y)}((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}\backslash\{0\}
, and hence
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(y+x)=0
. Therefore, we can assume that
L^{\flat}+\langle y\rangle
is integral. Let
(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{n-1})
(
a_{1}\leq a_{2}\dots\leq a_{n-1}
) be the fundamental invariants of
L^{\flat}
and let
\delta(y)=a_{n-1}+2
. Then, it suffices to show that for a fixed pair
(f_{1},f_{2})
of generators of
((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}
, we have

\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(y+\pi^{\delta}f_{1})-\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(y+\pi^{\delta-1}f_{2})=0,

for
\delta>\delta(y)=a_{n-1}+2
.

Now, let us introduce some notations from
[
18
, Lemma 2.24]
. Let
{\mathfrak{L}}
be the set of
O_{F}
-lattices in
{\mathbb{V}}
containing
L^{\flat}
, and let
{\mathfrak{C}}
be the set of triples
(L^{\prime\flat},\delta,\epsilon)
such that
L^{\prime\flat}
is an
O_{F}
-lattice of
L^{\flat}_{F}
containing
L^{\flat}
,
\delta\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and
\epsilon:\pi^{\delta}((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}\rightarrow L^{\prime\flat}\otimes_{O_{F}}F/O_{F}
is an
O_{F}
-linear map. Then, consider the map
\theta:{\mathfrak{L}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{C}}
sending
L
to
(L\cap L^{\flat}_{F},\delta_{L},\epsilon_{L})
where
\delta_{L}
is the maximal integer such that the image of
L
under the projection
\text{Pr}_{\perp}:{\mathbb{V}}\rightarrow(L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp}
is contained in
\pi^{\delta_{L}}((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}
, and
\epsilon_{L}
is the extension map
\pi^{\delta_{L}}((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}\rightarrow L^{\prime\flat}\otimes_{O_{F}}F/O_{F}
induced by the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow L\cap L_{F}^{\flat}\rightarrow L\rightarrow\pi^{\delta_{L}}((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}\rightarrow 0.

Then, as in the proof of
[
18
, Lemma 2.24 (1)]
,
\theta
is a bijection and its inverse is given by sending
(L^{\prime\flat},\delta,\epsilon)
to the
O_{F}
-lattice
L
generated by
L^{\prime\flat}
and
\epsilon(x)+x
for every
x\in\pi^{\delta}((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}
.

Now, for every
\delta^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we define the following sets

\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathfrak{L}}_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}\coloneqq\{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}\mid L\subset L^{\vee},\delta_{L}=\delta^{\prime},y+\pi^{\delta}f_{1}\in L\},\\
{\mathfrak{L}}_{2}^{\delta^{\prime}}\coloneqq\{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}\mid L\subset L^{\vee},\delta_{L}=\delta^{\prime},y+\pi^{\delta-1}f_{2}\in L\},\\
\end{array}

and for
L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}
, we define

\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathfrak{L}}_{1,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}\coloneqq\{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}\mid L\subset L^{\vee},\delta_{L}=\delta^{\prime},y+\pi^{\delta}f_{1}\in L,L\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat}\},\\
{\mathfrak{L}}_{2,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}\coloneqq\{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}\mid L\subset L^{\vee},\delta_{L}=\delta^{\prime},y+\pi^{\delta-1}f_{2}\in L,L\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat}\}.\\
\end{array}

Since
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)
is a certain sum of
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)
, it suffices to show that

\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(y+\pi^{\delta}f_{1})-\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(y+\pi^{\delta-1}f_{2})=0,

for
\delta>\delta(y)=a_{n-1}+2
and
L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}
.

By definition, we have that

\begin{array}[]{l}\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(y+\pi^{\delta}f_{1})={\sum}_{\delta^{\prime}\leq\delta}\hskip 11.74988pt{\sum}_{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{1,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}}D_{n,h}(L),\\
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(y+\pi^{\delta-1}f_{2})={\sum}_{\delta^{\prime}\leq\delta-1}\hskip 11.74988pt{\sum}_{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{2,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}}D_{n,h}(L).\par\end{array}

Therefore, it suffices to show that

(11.6)

{\sum}_{\delta^{\prime}\leq\delta}\hskip 11.74988pt{\sum}_{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{1,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}}D_{n,h}(L)-{\sum}_{\delta^{\prime}\leq\delta-1}\hskip 17.28004pt{\sum}_{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{2,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}}D_{n,h}(L)=0,

for all
\delta>\delta(y)=a_{n-1}+2
and
L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}
.

Since
\delta>a_{n-1}+2
, we have that for
\delta^{\prime}\leq 2
, we have

{\mathfrak{L}}_{1,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}={\mathfrak{L}}_{2,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}=\{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}\mid L\subset L^{\vee},\delta_{L}=\delta^{\prime},y\in L,L\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat}\}.

Therefore, (
11.6
) equals to

{\sum}_{\delta^{\prime}=2}^{\delta}\hskip 11.74988pt{\sum}_{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{1,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}}D_{n,h}(L)-{\sum}_{\delta^{\prime}=2}^{\delta-1}\hskip 17.28004pt{\sum}_{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{2,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}}D_{n,h}(L)=0.

Also, the automorphism of
{\mathfrak{C}}
sending
(L^{\prime\flat},\delta^{\prime},\epsilon)
to
(L^{\prime\flat},\delta^{\prime}-1,\epsilon(\pi\alpha\cdot))
, where
\alpha\in O_{F}^{\times}
,
f_{1}=\alpha f_{2}
, induces a bijection from
{\mathfrak{L}}_{1,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}}
to
{\mathfrak{L}}_{2,L^{\prime\flat}}^{\delta^{\prime}-1}
. Therefore, it suffices to show that

(11.7)

{\sum}_{L\in{\mathfrak{L}}_{1,L^{\prime\flat}}^{2}}D_{n,h}(L)=1_{L^{\prime\flat}}(y){\sum}_{L\subset L^{\vee},L\cap L_{F}^{\flat}=L^{\prime\flat},\delta_{L}=2}D_{n,h}(L)=0.

Note that by Theorem
11.2
(1), we have that for
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ}}^{n,h}(x)

\displaystyle={\sum}_{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L\subset L^{\vee},L\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat},x\in L^{\vee}}D_{n,h}(L)\vol(L)

\displaystyle=\vol(L^{\prime\flat}){\sum}_{L^{\prime\flat}\subset L\subset L^{\vee},L\cap L^{\flat}_{F}=L^{\prime\flat},x\in L^{\vee}}D_{n,h}(L)\vol(\text{Pr}_{\perp}(L))

\displaystyle=0.

Now, choose
x
to be generators of
\pi^{-2}((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}
and
\pi^{-3}((L_{F}^{\flat})^{\perp})^{\geq 0}
and then take the difference. Then, we have that

{\sum}_{L\subset L^{\vee},L\cap L_{F}^{\flat}=L^{\prime\flat},\delta_{L}=2}D_{n,h}(L)=0.

This shows that (
11.7
) holds which finishes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Theorem 11.4
.

Assume that Conjecture
6.3
above holds for
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
and Conjecture
7.6
holds for
{\mathcal{Z}}
-cycles in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h-1]}_{n-1}
. Then, Conjecture
7.6
holds for
{\mathcal{Z}}
-cycles in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n}
.

Proof.

As in
[
19
, section 8.2]
, we will prove this inductively. Let
L^{\flat}\subset{\mathbb{V}}
be a rank
n-1
lattice such that
L^{\flat}_{F}
is non-degenerate, and let
x\in{\mathbb{V}}\backslash L^{\flat}_{F}
. By definition of
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)
and
\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)
, it suffices to show that

(11.8)

\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}(x),

or equivalently

(11.9)

\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}(x),

for all
L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat\vee}
.

Now, assume that (
11.8
) holds for
L^{\prime\prime\flat}
such that
{\mathrm{val}}(L^{\prime\prime\flat})<{\mathrm{val}}(L^{\flat})
.

Let
(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{n-1})
(
0\leq a_{1}\leq\dots\leq a_{n-1}
) be the fundamental invariants of
L^{\flat}
. Let
M=M(L^{\flat})=L^{\flat}\obot\langle u\rangle
for some
u\in{\mathbb{V}}
such that
{\mathrm{val}}((u,u))=a_{n}\coloneqq a_{n-1}
or
a_{n-1}+1
, so that

a_{1}+a_{2}+\dots+a_{n}\equiv h+1\pmod{2}.

Now, assume that
(a_{1}^{\prime},a_{2}^{\prime},\dots,a_{n}^{\prime})
be the fundamental invariants of the lattice
L^{\flat}+\langle x\rangle
with a basis
(e_{1}^{\prime},\dots,e_{n}^{\prime})
such that
{\mathrm{val}}(e_{i}^{\prime},e_{i}^{\prime})=a_{i}^{\prime}
. Let
L^{\prime\prime\flat}=\langle e_{1}^{\prime},\dots,e_{n-1}^{\prime}\rangle
ane let
x^{\prime}=e_{n}^{\prime}
. Then, we have that

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat}}(x)=\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\prime\flat}}(x),&\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\flat}}^{n,h}(x)=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\prime\flat}}^{n,h}(x).\end{array}

By
[
19
, Lemma 8.2.2]
, if
x\notin M
, then
{\mathrm{val}}(L^{\prime\prime\flat})<{\mathrm{val}}(L^{\flat})
, and hence by the inductive hypothesis, we have that

\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\prime\flat}}(x)=\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\prime\prime\flat}}^{n,h}(x).

This implies that the support of

\phi=\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)-\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)\in C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{V}})

is contained in the lattice
M
. Here,
\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)-\partial\mathrm{Den}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)
is in
C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{V}})
by Proposition
11.3
, Theorem
6.5
,
[
19
, Lemma 6.2.1]
, and
[
24
, Lemma 2.11]
.

Now, let us consider
x
such that
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))<0
and
x\perp L^{\flat}
. Since we have assumed that Conjecture
6.3
below holds, by
[
19
, Lemma 6.3.1]
and
[
30
, Theorem 8.1]
, we have that

\widehat{\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\flat})_{{\mathscr{V}}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Z}}(x)})}=\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)=-\frac{1}{q^{h}}\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat},\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{Y}(x))\coloneqq-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\flat})_{{\mathscr{V}}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(x)}).

From now on, we use
\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat},\mathscr{V}}(\mathcal{Y}(x))
(resp.
\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}({\mathcal{Y}}(x))
) to denote
\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\flat})_{{\mathscr{V}}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(x)})
(resp.
\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{V}}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(x)})
).

Furthermore, note that we can decompose this into primitive parts

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}({\mathcal{Y}}(x)))

\displaystyle={\sum}_{L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat\vee}}-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}({\mathcal{Y}}(x))

\displaystyle={\sum}_{L^{\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat}\subset L^{\prime\flat\vee}}\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}(x).

Now, let us compare this with the analytic side.

When
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))\leq-2
, we have that
{\mathcal{Y}}(x)
is empty. Therefore, combining this with Theorem
11.2
, we have that

\widehat{\phi}(x)=\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)-\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=0-0=0.

When
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))=-1
, by Proposition
2.6
, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)=-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\mathrm{Int}_{L^{\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}({\mathcal{Y}}(x))

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h]}_{n},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{V}}}\otimes^{{\mathbb{L}}}O_{{\mathcal{Y}}(x)})

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h-1]}_{n-1},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{V}}})

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h-1]}_{n-1},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}-\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}).

Since we have assumed that Conjecture
7.6
holds for
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h-1]}_{n-1}
, we have that

-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h-1]}_{n-1},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ})=-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c),

where
(a,b,c)=(t_{\geq 2}(\lambda),t_{1}(\lambda),t_{0}(\lambda))
and
\lambda\in{\mathcal{R}}_{n-1}^{0+}
is the fundamental invariants of
L^{\prime\flat}
.

For
\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h-1]}_{n-1},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}})
, we have the following two cases.

First, assume that
(a,b,c)\neq(1,h-2,n-h)
. Then, by Theorem
5.3
(or see the proof of Theorem
11.2
), we have that
{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})_{{\mathscr{H}}}^{\circ}
is empty or the sum of
{\mathcal{Z}}(L)^{\circ}\cdot{\mathcal{Y}}(N)
for some lattice
N\simeq(\pi^{-1})^{h}
. Since
{\mathcal{Y}}(N)
is empty in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[h-1]}_{n-1}
, we have that
\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h-1]}_{n-1},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}})=0
.

Now, assume that
(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h)
. Then by Theorem
5.3
(or see the proof of Theorem
11.2
), we have that

{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}}={\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ}\cdot{\mathcal{Y}}(\pi^{-1}L_{1})\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{0}),

where
L_{2}\simeq\pi^{\lambda}
(
\lambda\geq 2
),
\pi^{-1}L_{1}\simeq\pi^{-1}I_{h-2}
, and
L_{0}\simeq I_{n-h}
. Therefore,

\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[h-1]}_{n-1},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L^{\prime\flat})^{\circ}_{{\mathscr{H}}})=\chi({\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{1},\text{}^{{\mathbb{L}}}{\mathcal{Z}}(L_{2})^{\circ})=D_{1,1}(1,0,0)=1.

Combining these, we have that

\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime\flat\circ},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)&\text{if }(a,b,c)\neq(1,h-2,n-h),\\
-\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}D_{n-1,h-1}(a,b,c)+\dfrac{1}{q^{h}}&\text{if }(a,b,c)=(1,h-2,n-h).\end{array}\right.

Therefore, by Theorem
11.2
, we have that

\widehat{\Int}_{L^{\prime}{\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}(x)-\widehat{\partial\mathrm{Den}}_{L^{\prime\flat},{\mathscr{V}}}^{n,h}(x)=0\text{ for }{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))<0.

This implies that
\widehat{\phi}=0
for
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))<0
.

Now, we only need to follow the proof of
[
19
, Theorem 8.2.1]
.

Since
\phi
is invariant under
L^{\flat}
and
\text{Supp}(\phi)\subset M
, we have that

\phi=1_{L^{\flat}}\otimes\phi_{\perp},

where
\phi_{\perp}\in C_{c}^{\infty}((L^{\flat}_{F})^{\perp})
and
\phi_{\perp}
is supported on
M_{\perp}=\langle u\rangle
. Then, we have

\widehat{\phi}=\vol(L^{\flat})1_{L^{\flat\vee}}\otimes\widehat{\phi_{\perp}},

and
\widehat{\phi_{\perp}}
is invariant under the translation by
\langle u^{\vee}\rangle=\langle\pi^{-a_{n}}u\rangle
. Since
{\mathrm{val}}((u^{\vee},u^{\vee}))=-a_{n}<0
and
\widehat{\phi_{\perp}}=0
for every
x
such that
x\perp L^{\flat}
,
{\mathrm{val}}((x,x))<0
, we have that
\widehat{\phi_{\perp}}
vanishes identically and hence
\phi=0
. This finishes the proof of the Theorem.
∎

Theorem 11.5
.

(cf.
[
19
, Theorem 8.2.1, Theorem 10.5.1]
)
Conjecture
7.6
holds for
{\mathcal{Z}}
-cycles in
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{n},{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n},{\mathcal{N}}^{[n-1]}_{n},{\mathcal{N}}^{[n]}_{n}
, and
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{4}
.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
11.4
and Theorem
6.5
.
∎

Remark 11.6
.

We remark that the Kudla-Rapoport conjecture for
{\mathcal{N}}^{[0]}_{n}
,
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n]}_{n}
(good reductions case),
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
(almost self-dual case) is already proved in
[
19
]
. Therefore, our new cases are
{\mathcal{N}}^{[n-1]}_{n}
and
{\mathcal{N}}^{[2]}_{4}
. We also remark that our work gives a different proof of the conjecture for
{\mathcal{N}}^{[1]}_{n}
.
